(2) **f64 policy**: rejected at both finalization points via a shared `avr-reject-f64` (src/abi.nuc) — explicit `:f64`/`double` in `parse-type-name`, and the bare-float-literal default in `emit-float` (a literal never touches `parse-type-name`, so the annotation check alone would miss `(let (x 1.5) …)`); message names the `-mdouble=64` avr-gcc multilib escape hatch. `f32` allowed; `i64` ground-verified linking libgcc `__muldi3` into a working `.elf`. **Cross-compilation host/target guard finding** (genuinely reusable, not AVR-specific): the rejection is guarded by `in-jit-module == 0` because `g-target-triple` — the raw string global `abi-is-avr`/`abi-is-aarch64` read — is *not* swapped when codegen redirects into a macro/compile-time-JIT sub-module; only `g-decl-out` (output routing) and the richer `g-host-target`/`g-target` descriptors (which supply the JIT module's own `target triple`/datalayout IR lines) change. So `g-target-triple` alone still reads `"avr"` while a macro body's ordinary host-side f64 arithmetic is being JIT-compiled to run on the host process — without the `in-jit-module` guard, that host-side arithmetic would be spuriously rejected. Any future single-target compile-time diagnostic keyed on `g-target-triple` needs this same `in-jit-module == 0` condition; `g-target-triple` alone is not a reliable signal for "what machine code is being generated right now." (3) **Runtime quasiquote**: verified correct on AVR (16-bit `__cons` `malloc(22)`), no diagnostic (as designed); the only link caveat is the orthogonal prelude/`perror` runtime pulled in by quoted-symbol interning, unchanged by AVR-7. **Verification**: clean build, `make test` **204/204**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the **first pass**, `make abi-test` green with confirmed **no regression** to hosted-target (x86_64/aarch64/i386/Windows) struct-by-value ABI behavior — the headline result given the risk profile of touching `abi-classify`, a chokepoint shared by every hosted target. IR-level byte-identity confirmed via direct diff. New gates: `run_avr7_f64`, `run_avr7_struct` + `tests/fixtures/avr7-{f64-annot,f64-literal,f32,i64,struct}.nuc`. **AVR-8 done (2026-07-17)** — tests, docs, and progress close-out; the AVR workstream (AVR-0 through AVR-8) is now complete. `make avr-test` (`tests/run-avr-test.sh`, gated on `avr-gcc`, independently gated again on `simavr` for the simulator step) compiles+links all four AVR examples via the AVR-3 `avr-gcc` driver and asserts each stays under a generous 4096-byte-flash/512-byte-RAM regression ceiling (not an exact-byte pin) — confirmed exact-match footprints across the four examples: **858** (`avr-blink.nuc`, ATtiny1634), **890** (`avr-blink-dx.nuc`, AVR32DD20), **1070** (`avr-uart-hello.nuc`, ATmega328P), **908** (`avr-isr.nuc`, ATmega328P) bytes of flash, 0 data/bss throughout. The harness's real behavioral gate runs `avr-uart-hello.nuc` under `simavr -m atmega328p -f 8000000` and asserts the captured output contains the transmitted `Hi!` bytes — not just that the link succeeded. Independently re-verified: `make clean && make && make test` stayed **204/204**, `make bootstrap` stayed byte-identical, `make abi-test` stayed green (fully additive — no `src/`/`lib/` changes), `make avr-test` itself green with the exact matching `avr-size` numbers above, and the harness's fail path was independently re-confirmed by deliberately breaking a different assertion than the implementer tested (a nonexistent example path, then an artificially tiny ceiling) and watching it correctly fail with a non-zero exit, then reverting clean. New capstone doc [docs/avr.md](../docs/avr.md): flags, the two-device walkthrough (ATtiny1634 + AVR32DD20 build/link commands and `avr-size` output), the v1 profile and its exclusions (function values/`dyn`/`f64` diagnostics, deferred C struct-by-value interop, the deferred `(flash T)` progmem sketch, the AVR32DD20 niche-collision link recipe), and the MMIO/ISR idioms (`lib/avr.nuc`'s `reg8`/`reg16` helper table, the `fn-attr`-based ISR convention, and why no `defisr` macro exists). **AVR workstream summary.** Nine milestones (AVR-0 ground-verify → AVR-1 backend registration → AVR-2 16-bit correctness → AVR-3 link driver → AVR-4 MMIO/examples → AVR-5 ISRs → AVR-6 Harvard hazards → AVR-7 ABI/numerics → AVR-8 tests/docs), each independently verified across many autopilot iterations, landed AVR cross-compilation to two reference devices (classic ATtiny1634, modern mapped-flash AVR32DD20) plus a CI-simulatable third (ATmega328P) with **zero regressions to any hosted target** — `make bootstrap` byte-identical at every single milestone, `make test` growing 180→204 and `make abi-test` green throughout. One follow-up bug fix shipped mid-workstream (AVR-6: `set!` against a `:const` global was accepted and miscompiled into a store to read-only storage; now a compile-time error). The v1 AVR profile is scalars, pointers, structs/unions (self-consistent `ABI-MEMORY`-only ABI), `:volatile` MMIO, ISRs, `:const` flash-resident globals, `f32`, `i64`, and (at a cost) runtime quasiquote — with function values/boxed closures/`dyn`, `f64`, and C struct-by-value interop as explicit, diagnosed-not-miscompiled v1 exclusions. See [docs/avr.md](../docs/avr.md) for the user-facing guide. | [stage14/avr-targets.md](stage14/avr-targets.md) |
| 14 (RISC-V) | Linux on RISC-V — riscv64 RV64GC/lp64d; Tier A cross-compile + full test suite under `qemu-user`, Tier B native self-hosting (hardware-gated); backend registration + per-triple CPU/features defaults + first `!llvm.module.flags` emission (`target-abi=lp64d`), triple-keyed cross link driver, integer-convention aggregate ABI (`abi-is-riscv`; FP-flattening deferred like aarch64 HFA), per-arch boot-IR selection modeled on `windows-boot` | **RV-0, RV-1, RV-2, RV-3, RV-5 done; RV-4 deferred/future (see below)** — design doc written; toolchain ground-verified in-container (RISCV backend present; **features cliff verified**: empty CPU/features → bare RV64I with silent soft-float `lp64` ABI (`__muldi3`, doubles in integer regs) vs correct `mul`/`fadd.d` with `+m,+a,+f,+d,+c`+`lp64d` — the current empty-features `LLVMCreateTargetMachine` calls make this the default failure mode; cross gcc/qemu not yet in container). RV-0's host-portability half landed: `src/repl.nuc:625` (`jit-thunk-module`) and `:719` (`repl-jit-module-rt-rewrite`) no longer hardcode `"x86_64-pc-linux-gnu"` — both now read `((as ptr:Target g-host-target) triple)`, matching the pattern already used at `src/nucleusc.nuc:8225/8446`, fixing the JIT-module triple mismatch that broke the REPL macro-JIT path on non-x86_64 hosts (e.g. aarch64). Verified twice independently: `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 180/180, `make bootstrap` byte-identical (stage1.ll == stage2.ll, no `update-bootstrap` needed), REPL smoke check (define/call/redefine a function via `build/nucleusc -i`) passed. RV-0's other half — the Dockerfile `gcc-riscv64-linux-gnu` + `qemu-user` change — landed with AVR-0's container rebuild (§4, shared Dockerfile edit): the container now carries `qemu-riscv64` (verified on `PATH`), though `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` and a riscv sysroot are still absent, so the cross-link half of RV-0/RV-2 stays blocked (see below) even though the toolchain-availability blocker that stalled RV-1…RV-5 as a group is gone. **RV-1 done (2026-07-17)** — the compiler now knows the `riscv64` target end to end for IR emission. `src/llvm.nuch` declares the `LLVMInitializeRISCV{TargetInfo,Target,TargetMC,AsmPrinter}` quartet, called from `targets-init-all` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) beside AVR-1's quartet (registration itself was already generic — AVR-1 had landed the shared `cpu`/backend-registration plumbing per design §4, so RV-1 was the features/ABI delta on top of it). `Target` (`src/compiler-types.nuc`) gained `features:ptr` and `abi:ptr` alongside AVR-1's `cpu:ptr`. Three new triple-keyed resolvers beside the existing `reloc-for-triple`: `cpu-for-triple` (honors an explicit `--mcpu`, else `generic-rv64` for `riscv64`), `features-for-triple` (`+m,+a,+f,+d,+c`), `abi-for-triple` (`lp64d`) — all three return `""` for every non-riscv64 triple. The key design decision: these are triple-keyed **defaults**, not a user-facing flag — the whole point of the features-cliff fix is that the *default* must be correct, since the failure mode (bare RV64I soft-float against a `lp64d` glibc) is silent, not a link or runtime error. `make-target-for-triple` resolves effective cpu/features/abi once and threads eff-cpu/eff-features into `LLVMCreateTargetMachine`, storing all three on the descriptor; `compile-and-link` now reads `features` from the descriptor instead of a hardcoded `""` (`cpu` already flowed from AVR-1). `assemble-module-ir` emits a `!llvm.module.flags = !{!0}` / `!0 = !{i32 1, !"target-abi", !"<abi>"}` block — the first module-flags emission anywhere in the compiler — only when `abi` is non-empty, so every existing hosted-target IR stays byte-identical (verified). Dead-code cleanup: the two inline X86-only `LLVMInitializeX86*` blocks in `compile-and-link` and `jit-ensure-init` were deleted as redundant with `targets-init-all`, which already runs at startup on every code path (batch `main`, batch CT-JIT via `emit-toplevel-forms`, and the REPL via `repl-main` → `compiler-init` before `repl-preload-macros`). Test gate: `run_riscv_emit` (`tests/run-tests.sh`, mirrors `run_avr_emit`) asserts the riscv64 datalayout/triple/`target-abi=lp64d` module flag, then (llc-guarded) round-trips through `llc -mtriple=riscv64 -mattr=+m,+a,+f,+d,+c` asserting hardware `mul`/`fadd.d` with no `__muldi3`/`__adddf3` libcalls — the actual features-cliff regression test — using new fixture `tests/fixtures/riscv-features.nuc`. Verified: `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 187/187 (185 baseline + 2 new riscv gates), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass (no `update-bootstrap` reconverge needed), `make abi-test` green, host/x86_64/aarch64 `--emit-llvm` byte-identical between the pre-change boot binary and the new build. IR-emission only (no link driver) — `--target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu --emit-llvm` and `-c` (object) work; `-o` (link) is untested/unsupported until RV-2. **RV-2 done (2026-07-18)** — the cross link driver + qemu test lane. `compile-and-link` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) gained an `is-riscv` branch beside AVR-3's `is-avr` in the driver-selection `let`: a `riscv64`-prefixed triple (`(strncmp g-target-triple "riscv64" 7)`) selects the `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` cross driver (`--linker=` still overrides; AVR and riscv64 prefixes are mutually exclusive; riscv64 needs no `-mmcu`-equivalent flag; PIC and `reloc-for-triple` unchanged). New `make riscv-test` → `tests/run-riscv-test.sh` (modeled on `run-avr-test.sh`): compiles each `examples/*.nuc` that has a `tests/expected/<name>.out` via the real compile-and-link path (`--target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu -o`, no `--emit-llvm`/`-c`), runs it under `qemu-riscv64 -L /usr/riscv64-linux-gnu`, and diffs stdout — opt-in beside `avr-test`, not part of `make test`/`make bootstrap`. Two-stage SKIP gate: (1) `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` + `qemu-riscv64` on `PATH`, (2) a **capability probe** that drives the compiler's link path on `examples/hello.nuc` and checks it *succeeds* — because this container has `libc6-riscv64-cross` (runtime libs) but **not** `libc6-dev-riscv64-cross` (crt startup objects + headers), the link fails `cannot find Scrt1.o`, which the probe detects and SKIPs cleanly (exit 0) with a one-line pointer to the missing package instead of FAILing every example. Verified: `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 204/204 (unchanged — additive, touches only a cross-target's link command line), `make bootstrap` byte-identical first pass (no `update-bootstrap`), `make riscv-test` SKIPs with the crt-gap diagnostic. Driver routing proven independent of the crt gap: `./build/nucleusc --target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu examples/hello.nuc -o …` fails `nucleusc: link step failed (riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc exit 256)` after `ld: cannot find Scrt1.o` — i.e. the compiler correctly selected the riscv64 driver and emitted a valid riscv64 object; only the downstream link failed on the missing crt objects (not a "clang: unknown target"-shaped error). RV-2 fully passes with no further code change the moment the container gains `libc6-dev-riscv64-cross` (a Dockerfile change reachable only from the user's interactive session; do not work around it). **RV-3 done (2026-07-18)** — the aggregate-passing ABI (integer convention). Three localized `src/abi.nuc` edits: (1) `abi-is-riscv` predicate beside `abi-is-aarch64`/`abi-is-avr` (`(strncmp g-target-triple "riscv64" 7)`, the same idiom as `cpu/features/abi-for-triple`); (2) `abi-classify` forces both eightbytes of a ≤16-byte aggregate to class 3 (INTEGER) on a riscv64 triple via a one-line ternary at each of the `c0`/`c1` computations, skipping `abi-class-eightbyte`'s field-type walk — so a small FP-bearing struct lowers to `i64`/`{i64,i64}` (reusing the COERCE1/COERCE2 shapes unchanged; `n0`/`n1` and the ≤8/>8 threshold are size-only, already target-agnostic) instead of `double`/`<2 x float>`; the >16-byte → ABI-MEMORY branch and the sret return path were already target-generic and correct for riscv64's "large aggregate by reference / return via hidden pointer", so untouched; (3) the two byval-vs-plain-pointer gates — `abi-print-param` and `abi-arg-frag` — gained `(= (abi-is-riscv) 0)` as a third `and` conjunct (variadic-fold macro, so 3 conjuncts is fine) beside the aarch64/AVR exclusions, so a >16-byte riscv64 aggregate passes as a plain `ptr` (no `byval`), matching the riscv64 psABI. FP-flattening of *pure* small FP-bearing structs into FPRs stays deferred (the documented divergence, same as the aarch64 HFA gap) — the reused interop fixtures never hit it (`Mixed {i32,f32}`'s integer field forces the INTEGER class on both x86_64 SysV and the riscv64 psABI, and gcc does not flatten a mixed-member struct, so `mixed_get` interoperates exactly; scalar `f64` args stay `double`, unaffected). **[CORRECTED 2026-08-06 — that parenthetical is false, measured on real riscv64 hardware: native `make abi-test` fails `mixed_get` (8.5→7.0), `farr2_make` (1.50,2.25→2.25,0.00) and `farr2_sum` (3.75→2.25). The `Mixed` reasoning applied x86_64 SysV eightbyte rules to RISC-V, which has an explicit psABI rule SysV lacks — one FP real + one integer, either order, goes in one FPR + one GPR — and `Mixed` was not the only FP-bearing fixture anyway: `FArr2 {float[2]}` was added later by Stage 15 W8 G-2 and the psABI flattens two FP reals into `fa0`/`fa1` (flattening recurses through array members). Not a regression — RV-3's integer convention behaves as specified and every integer/large-aggregate case passes; the gap simply became reachable for the first time when riscv64 hardware appeared. See stage14/riscv-linux.md's CORRECTION block.]** New `make riscv-abi-test` → `tests/run-riscv-abi-test.sh`: the same three-direction interop as `run-abi-test.sh` (Nucleus↔C, Nucleus↔Nucleus), reusing `tests/abi/{clib.c,interop.nuc,callee.nuc,driver.c,expected.out}` unchanged but cross-compiled `--target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu -c` + `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` and run under `qemu-riscv64 -L /usr/riscv64-linux-gnu`; two-stage SKIP gate identical to `run-riscv-test.sh` (PATH check + a capability probe linking `examples/hello.nuc` that also proves the crt objects/headers the C fixtures need are present); opt-in Makefile target beside `riscv-test`, NOT in `make test`/`make bootstrap`/`make abi-test`. Verified: `make clean && make` clean; `make test` 204/204 (unchanged — every riscv branch is triple-gated, so `g-target-triple == "x86_64-…"` during self-compile never fires them); `make bootstrap` byte-identical first pass (no `update-bootstrap` — nothing leaked into the host path); `make abi-test` still PASS; `make riscv-abi-test` SKIPs with the crt-gap diagnostic. Classification proven independent of the crt gap via `--target=… --emit-llvm` on an f64-struct fixture — `{f64,f64}`→`{i64,i64}`, `{f64,i64}`→`{i64,i64}`, single `f64`→`i64` (host keeps `{double,double}`/`{double,i64}`/`double`); >16-byte `Big` drops `byval` on riscv (`declare i64 @big_sum(ptr)`) while host keeps `ptr byval(%Big) align 8`; hosted `--emit-llvm` byte-identical pre/post; `llc -mtriple=riscv64 -mattr=+m,+a,+f,+d,+c` compiles the riscv IR cleanly. Same `libc6-dev-riscv64-cross` container gap as RV-2 blocks the qemu execution (SKIP is correct until the package lands). **RV-5 done (2026-07-18)** — this is the docs-only close-out pass: `design/stage8/platform.md` gained a Phase-B-matrix note that AVR/riscv64 registration landed later (Stage 14) plus a rewritten Carried-forward ABI note (aarch64 AAPCS partially landed, riscv64 lp64d integer-convention aggregate ABI landed in RV-3, Win64/ARM AAPCS/i386 cdecl still fully deferred); `context/build.md` gained the `llc`-needs-`-mattr` caveat for hand-fed riscv64 textual IR (the riscv64 link/test-flow bullet already existed from RV-2); `docs/compiler.md`'s `--linker=` row (and its intro line) now name `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` as the riscv64 default alongside `avr-gcc` (the rest of its riscv64 coverage — `--target=`/`--mcpu=` rows, the `structs-unions.md` cross-ref — was already complete from RV-1/RV-2/RV-3, no `docs/riscv.md` needed). **RV-4 (native Tier B self-hosting) is explicitly deferred/future, NOT a pending autopilot milestone**: it requires a full bootable riscv64 Linux guest (kernel + rootfs + toolchain) under `qemu-system-riscv64` or real riscv64 hardware — categorically more setup than the container-package fix that blocks RV-2/RV-3's qemu-user execution, and needs deliberate user-driven environment provisioning before any agent session can attempt it. A future stage-14 autopilot loop reading this row should **not** dispatch RV-4 as a normal milestone; the RISC-V workstream's CI-testable scope (Tier A: cross-compile + qemu-user) is complete as of RV-3/RV-5. Shares `Target` cpu/features + link-driver plumbing with AVR-1/AVR-3; byte-identical on existing targets throughout. **RV-2 amended (2026-08-06)** — the cross link driver is now guarded on the **host** triple, not the target triple alone. As first landed, `is-riscv` keyed on `g-target-triple` only, so a *native* riscv64 build (no `--target=`, triple defaulted from `LLVMGetDefaultTargetTriple`) also reached for `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` rather than the hosted `clang` default — wrong on its own terms, since the `--sysroot` problem that driver choice exists to dodge is a **cross artifact only** (on riscv64 hardware the sysroot is `/` and plain clang links with no flag), and it silently imported a **Debian-family** naming assumption (Debian's native gcc does ship the triplet-prefixed name, so Debian riscv64 would have worked by accident, but Fedora/Alpine/Arch riscv64 ship no `riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` and the link would fail `not found` on a correctly configured native machine). `compile-and-link` now binds `host-is-riscv` from `((as ptr:Target g-host-target) triple)` and selects the cross driver only under `(and (!= is-riscv 0) (= host-is-riscv 0))`, comparing **arch prefixes** so a `riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu` vs `riscv64-linux-gnu` normalization difference cannot misclassify a native build as cross; AVR keeps its unguarded branch (AVR is never a host, so the guard would be dead code). Both test lanes (`tests/run-riscv-test.sh`, `tests/run-riscv-abi-test.sh`) now pick a lane from `uname -m`: cross unchanged (`riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc` + `qemu-riscv64`), native riscv64 prefers `clang`, runs binaries directly with no qemu, needs no cross toolchain, and passes **no `--linker` at all** when clang is present (driver choice stays the compiler's single source of truth rather than being re-derived in shell), falling back to `--linker=cc` only if clang is absent. Verified: `make` clean, `make test` 424/424, `make bootstrap` byte-identical first pass, `make abi-test` PASS, both riscv gates still SKIP with the unchanged cross-lane crt-gap diagnostic, cross routing re-proven directly (`riscv64-linux-gnu-gcc exit 256` after `cannot find Scrt1.o`), native script lane exercised via a `uname` shim. The compiler's native-host branch itself is **not executable on the x86_64 container** (`host-is-riscv` is structurally 0 there), so it is verified by construction only — a first-run item for RV-4's hardware/guest. **RV-6 done (2026-08-06/07, [stage14/riscv-fp-abi.md](stage14/riscv-fp-abi.md))** — the lp64d **hard-float** struct ABI RV-3 deferred, opened by the first native `make abi-test` on real riscv64 hardware (`mixed_get` 8.5→7.0, `farr2_make` 1.50,2.25→2.25,0.00, `farr2_sum` 3.75→2.25). RV-6a added `AbiInfo.off1` so the five COERCE2 reconstruction sites GEP by the *member's own* offset (4 for `{i32,f32}`/`{float[2]}`) instead of a hardcoded second-eightbyte 8 — the structural blocker that would have made a pure classification patch silently corrupt the second member. RV-6b added `RvFlat` + `rv-flatten-type`/`abi-riscv-fp-classify` (`src/abi.nuc`), flattening an aggregate's scalar members (recursing through nested structs **and arrays**; a union never flattens) and applying the psABI's three rules — one FP real → an FPR, two FP reals → fa0/fa1, one FP real + one integer in **either** order → FPR+GPR. **RV-6c done (2026-08-07)** — the register counting those rules are conditional on. `abi-classify` became a pure wrapper over `abi-classify-avail (t gpr fpr)`; the new `abi-classify-arg` consults the ambient budget (`g-abi-gpr-left`/`g-abi-fpr-left`/`g-abi-varargs`, declared beside `g-form-line`), classifies, and debits via `abi-rv-consume`, and on every non-riscv target tail-calls `abi-classify` and touches nothing — which is what keeps hosted IR byte-identical. The invariant, documented at the globals and in `context/conventions.md`: **every walk over a parameter or argument list calls `abi-args-begin` first, then `abi-classify-arg` once per element in declaration order**, with `abi-args-varargs` at the `...` boundary; the seven such walks (the `define` signature loop and the prologue loop — two independent walks over the same signature — plus `emit-call-with-args`, `macro-jit-ensure-decl`, and the `.nuch`/C-header/REPL declare emitters) each reset for themselves. Return positions keep plain `abi-classify`, since a0/a1/fa0/fa1 are always free. Two things the spec did not have: the variadic tail needed **no separate path** ("integer convention, no flattening, no FP registers" falls out of classifying with an FP availability of 0, since every rule needs ≥1 FPR), and §4's consumption table was **missing the `sret` debit** — clang starts the argument walk from `NumArgGPRs - 1` for an indirect return, and without it a 7-integer-argument `sret` function lowers a trailing `{i32,f32}` as `{i32,float}` where clang says `i64`. Also collapsed the three redundant double-`abi-classify` sites first (wasteful under a pure classifier, double-counting under a consuming one). RV-6d added `tests/fixtures/rv6-fp-abi.nuc` + `run_rv6_fp_abi` (`tests/run-tests.sh`): four cross-emission gates — flattening rules, register counting (exhaustion **and** its one-register-either-side controls, plus the sret debit), the variadic tail, and an x86_64 anti-leak control — every expected string taken from clang's own lowering of structurally identical C. Verified: `make` clean, `make test` 428/428 (424 prior + 4 new), `make bootstrap` byte-identical first pass (no `update-bootstrap`), `make abi-test` PASS, x86_64 lowering of the same structs still exact SysV (`<2 x float>`, `i64`), and the riscv64 module round-trips through `llc -mtriple=riscv64 -mattr=+m,+a,+f,+d,+c`. **The execution gate is still hardware-only (§7): the user must run `make abi-test` natively on riscv64 — cross-emission evidence does not close it.** One pre-existing RV-3 divergence stays open and out of scope (riscv-fp-abi.md §8): clang coerces an integer-convention aggregate to whole XLEN units (`i64` / `[N x i64]`) while `abi-eightbyte-ir` narrows each eightbyte to its exact width, so 4-, 12- and 16-byte integer-convention aggregates differ in *spelling* (only 8 bytes agrees); RV-6c made the 4- and 16-byte rows newly reachable (an FP aggregate can now fall back) without changing the rule. Untested in either direction; aligning it needs a hardware run. **RV-6e (2026-08-07)** — two follow-ons found by running the suite where RV-6 had never run. (1) The **declare/define ABI mismatch**: three `declare` emitters printed raw `type-to-ir` for their signatures instead of ABI-lowering them, so a caller's `declare` disagreed with the callee's `define` for every by-value aggregate — `emit-nuch-defmethod-import` (`src/nuch.nuc`, rebuilt on `emit-nuch-declare-import`'s shape) and two in `src/repl.nuc` (the first-definition preamble declare and the global-redeclare loop, both now `abi-print-param-to g-repl-preamble` with `need-comma` tracking rather than an index test, which drops the comma before `...` when an sret prefix occupies slot 0). Proven by before/after IR (`define i64` vs pre-fix `declare %Pt` vs post-fix `declare i64`) and an end-to-end run. **Every `declare` emitter must ABI-lower exactly like the `define`; there are six, and three had silently drifted** (`context/conventions.md`). (2) **By-value structs in the REPL**, a fourth site one layer down: `emit-fn-thunk` built the redefinition trampoline's signature from raw `type-to-ir`, and `jit-thunk-module` was the only module assembled without `g-repl-preamble` — which is where the session's `%Name = type {…}` lines live — so a named aggregate in that signature was never defined (`use of undefined type named 'Pt'`). The thunk forwards its parameters untouched to a callee with an identical lowered signature, so the operand list is byte-for-byte the same in the signature and the forwarded call: it is now built once into a memstream and printed twice (`byval`/`sret` are legal on call operands, so verbatim reuse is correct for MEMORY too, and a MEMORY return joins the same no-result path as a `void` one). Only sret/byval aggregates name a type, so ABI-lowering alone fixes every struct ≤16 bytes and the missing-preamble half bites only above it — hence the fixture's 24-byte case. New `tests/repl/byval-structs.in` pins all three ABI classes (COERCE1 8-byte, ABI-MEMORY 24-byte sret+byval, `{f32,f32}` SSE) plus redefinition dispatch. (3) **`rv6-x86-unchanged` was itself host-dependent** — a test bug surfaced by the first `make test` on riscv64 hardware, not an ABI bug: `run_rv6_fp_abi`'s SysV lane rode the default target, so on riscv64 it compiled the fixture with the riscv rules and reported correct riscv lowering as a leak. Both lanes now name `--target=` explicitly; `target-init` never consults the host descriptor once `--target=` is set, so the gate is host-independent by construction. Generalized in `context/conventions.md` as the test-harness spelling of the same "a triple is not a host" error as the RV-2 link-driver guard — and the more dangerous one, since it passes on the machine it was written on. Verified across all three: `make test` **429/429**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, `make abi-test` + `make layout-test` PASS. The §7 hardware execution gate remains open. | [stage14/riscv-linux.md](stage14/riscv-linux.md) |
| 14 (int-widening) | Integer-literal widening — close the residual cast gaps: template-tier adaptation for stamped parametric methods (`conj`/`(v i)`/HashMap — the real friction), explicit-`return` coercion, representability checks + wide emission for out-of-i32-range literals, `node-type-call` lockstep mirror, ~991-site vestigial-cast sweep | **LW-1 through LW-5 all done (2026-07-15)** — a shared side-effect-free `generic-resolve-adapt-tier` (`src/generics.nuc`) runs the tier-2 pool for both emit (`generic-resolve`, dies/monomorphizes) and the type model (`node-type-call`, returns null); template candidates matched by `generic-method-bind-adapt` (receiver-bind then `arg-adapts` each remaining param, never binding a tyvar); `Method.origin-template` marks stamped instances so the pool skips a template's own cache (else the *second* widened call reads as a false `ambiguous overload`). `(conj v 3)`/`(insert v 1 7)`/`(v 0)` on `(Vector i64)` now compile castless; genuine width-only ambiguity still errors; `operator-user-resolve` has no template tier (left untouched). LW-3: `emit-return` (src/nucleusc.nuc) now runs `coerce-int-val` against `g-fn-ret-type` before the scalar `ret` (mirroring the implicit end-of-body path), guarded on `g-fn-ret-type != null`; on failure a source-level `die-at` names both types via `type-spelling` instead of falling through to a raw LLVM parse error; the struct-return (`emit-struct-ret`) branch is untouched since `abi-classify` only assigns non-`ABI-DIRECT` kinds to `TY-STRUCT`/`TY-UNION`, so the scalar branch never carries a struct return type. `(defn f:i64 () (return 0))` now compiles and runs; a genuine mismatch (e.g. `(return 1.5)` in an `:i64` fn) now dies cleanly at the source line instead of an LLVM parse error. LW-4: `Val` gains `is-lit`/`lit-i64` (set only by `emit-int`); the representability check lives in the single int→int chokepoint of `coerce-int-val` (abi.nuc) — every narrowing coercion of a literal (let/defvar init, field/`aset!` store, return, union variant, call args via `safe-coerce-val`, binop adoption via `coerce-num-val`) routes a tagged Val through it — dying `integer literal N does not fit <type>` on an out-of-range literal while leaving typed values untouched. Shared predicate `int-literal-fits` (type-utils.nuc, shift-built bounds so it is a bootstrap fixed point). `emit-int` now emits `i32` when the value fits, else `i64`, with `node-type`'s NODE-INT branch mirroring (lockstep); `(take64 5000000000)`→`5000000000` (was the 32-bit-wrap `705032704`). Lexer (`lex-atom`, reader.nuc): `strtol` errno-checked for `ERANGE`, non-negative overflow retries `strtoull` (ui64/usize scale), negative or >u64 overflow → positioned reader error; `errno` via hand-declared `__errno_location`. Latent bug fixed: the FNV hash constants were silently i32-truncated by old `emit-int` — now correct (updated `cstr-fold-test.out` to the real FNV-1a; `types.nuc`'s deliberate i8-wrap demo now uses an explicit `(cast i8 200)`). Bootstrap not byte-identical (Val grew, error strings, corrected FNV constants shift union-shape-hash names) — reconverged in **two** `update-bootstrap` rounds (corrected constant needs one extra generation to reach the naming code); 140 tests pass. LW-5's test/doc slice done (2026-07-03): `examples/int-widening.nuc` demonstrates castless `conj`/`insert`/`(v i)` on `(Vector i64)`, a castless explicit `(return 5)`, and `(take64 5000000000)` printing the untruncated value; negative fixtures `tests/fixtures/lw-ambiguous-widening.nuc` (two non-i32 overloads both reachable by tier-2 adaptation → `ambiguous overload for 'f' under argument widening`) and `tests/fixtures/lw-literal-range.nuc` (`(take8 300)` → `integer literal 300 does not fit ui8`) wired into `tests/run-tests.sh`; `docs/types.md`'s existing "Implicit Type Coercion"/"Literal Values" sections were checked against LW-1..LW-4 and found already accurate, no edit needed; the Keyword/StrView doc example and `examples/strview-test.nuc` were checked for a vestigial int-literal cast per the design doc's note — none found (their casts are unrelated `ptr`/`ui64`-format casts), so no change there either. 143 tests pass; bootstrap untouched (byte-identical, no `src`/`lib` changes). Remaining: LW-5's tree-wide vestigial-`(cast <int-type> <literal>)` sweep (~991 sites across src/lib/examples) stays deferred to a separate pass, per the design doc's own sequencing note. **LW-5 batch 1 (src/) DONE (2026-07-15):** 296 of 355 `(cast <int-type> <literal>)` sites deleted across 10 src/ files (nucleusc.nuc, cheader.nuc, repl.nuc, format.nuc, union-registry.nuc, generics.nuc, union-emit.nuc, abi.nuc, type-utils.nuc, scope.nuc); 48 sites kept where a `node-type-call` lockstep gap (a binop typed from its first operand only, then double-widened at an outer coercion) or IR-position reordering made byte-identity unreachable without the cast — restored at each. Verified via the strongest available check: a `build/nucleusc.ll` diff against a pre-sweep snapshot came back exactly zero (stronger than `make bootstrap`'s stage1==stage2, which shares any bug between both stages and wouldn't catch drift). 174/174 tests, bootstrap byte-identical. **LW-5 batch 2 (lib/, macro-free files) DONE (2026-07-15):** 359 of 360 sites deleted across 12 lib/ files with no `defmacro` (char.nuc, hashmap.nuc, hashset.nuc, reader.nuc, strview.nuc, vector.nuc, string.nuc, node.nuc, keyword.nuc, hash.nuc, iterator.nuc, list.nuc); 1 site kept (`lib/vector.nuc` `vector-grow`, same lockstep-gap class). Same zero-diff verification; 174/174 tests, bootstrap byte-identical. Also fixed a real bug hit along the way: `Makefile`'s `COMPILER_DEPS` was missing `lib/vector.nuc`/`hash.nuc`/`hashset.nuc`/`hashmap.nuc`/`list.nuc`/`iterator.nuc`/`allocator.nuc`/`coll.nuc`/`seq.nuc` (the same stale-incremental-build gap previously fixed for `lib/reader.nuc` — editing these silently didn't trigger a rebuild). **LW-5 batch 3 (lib/, `defmacro`-containing files) DONE (2026-07-15):** all 48 sites deleted across the 6 remaining lib/ files (arena.nuc 7, combinators.nuc 4, error.nuc 1, macros.nuc 4, parse.nuc 22, string-split.nuc 10); none needed restoring. The feared quasiquote string-pool complication was real but confined to `lib/macros.nuc`'s 4 sites (literal `(cast i32 0)` inside the `doseq`/`doseq-iter`/`into`/`into-iter` expansion templates themselves, not just co-located in a macro-bearing file) — removing them dropped exactly 8 now-unused rodata string constants (`"cast"`×4, `"i32"`×4) with zero other IR change, and `make bootstrap` held byte-identical on the first attempt, no `update-bootstrap` reconverge needed. 174/174 tests. **LW-5 batch 4 (examples/) DONE (2026-07-15):** 221 of 231 sites deleted across 34 files; 10 kept — 1 deliberate (`types.nuc`'s out-of-range `(cast i8 200)` narrowing demo) and 9 restored across two **new** failure classes beyond src/'s and lib/ batch 2's: (a) `match` arms mistype the same way `if`/`cond` branches do (same `type-join` chokepoint, new manifestation — `examples/signal.nuc`'s `(none 0)` vs `(some sz) sz` silently mistyped the whole match, giving a wrong runtime value rather than a compile error) and (b) a `&where`-constrained generic *function* (`reduce`'s `FoldFn` constraint) doesn't run the tier-2 widening adaptation LW-1/LW-2 gave template *methods*, so a literal accumulator against an `Acc=i64` conformance dies `constraint 'FoldFn' parameter mismatch: expected i32, found i64` (hit in cstr-fold-test.nuc, iterator-test.nuc, listiter-test.nuc, rest-defn.nuc, split-iter-test.nuc×2). Verified every example compiles (blast-radius check beyond just the fixture-tested ones); two pre-existing unrelated failures confirmed independent of this sweep (`comb-shapes.nuc`, a documented HEAD compile-time-JIT bug; `thread-macros.nuc`, a no-`main` library file that fails to link). 174/174 tests, bootstrap byte-identical throughout. **LW-5 complete: 924 of ~991 estimated vestigial casts removed tree-wide, 59 kept** (48 src/ lockstep-gap restores, 1 lib/ batch-2 restore, 10 examples/ restores, plus types.nuc's deliberate narrowing cast) — see [stage14/int-widening.md](stage14/int-widening.md) for the full failure-class writeups. **LW-6 done (2026-07-15)** — both failure classes root-caused and fixed, not just cast-restored. Class 1: `type-join` (src/generics.nuc) collapsed *any* two differently-kinded types to `void`, not just genuinely incompatible ones — now takes `result-lit`/`bty-lit` flags (from `Val.is-lit`) and widens to the non-literal (or wider) side when exactly one/both of two int-kinded branches is a literal, threaded through all three call sites (`emit-cond`, `emit-match-clauses`, `emit-niche-match-arm` via a new `ltbox`); two genuinely-typed branches of different kinds still collapse to void (unchanged, a real error). `examples/signal.nuc`'s `(none 0)` vs `(some sz) sz` and `lib/vector.nuc`'s `vector-grow` `(if (= cap 0) 4 (* cap 2))` both compile castless now (the latter needed the standard 2-stage bootstrap: `update-bootstrap` with the cast still in, then remove it and rebuild from the new boot). Class 2: `generic-method-bind`'s tier-1 exact unification eagerly bound a bare-tyvar param (`reduce`'s `Acc`) from a literal argument's naive `i32` *before* `recover-assoc-into`'s `&where`-conformance recovery got a chance to bind it correctly (`i64` from `(extend ByteSum (FoldFn i64 ui8))`) — fixed by deferring a literal-vs-bare-unbound-tyvar binding until after assoc-recovery runs (new `arg-nodes` param threaded to `generic-method-bind` from every real-call-site caller, including `node-type-call`'s `generic-binds-for` path — the lockstep gate, since the type-model probe was dying before emission ever got a chance). Bug caught mid-fix: deferring unconditionally (not checking "still unbound at this point in the pass") broke `conj`-style receiver-inferred templates, double-stamping `conj.pVector.i64.i64` — fixed by only deferring when the tyvar isn't already bound by an earlier non-deferred arg in the same pass. All five `reduce` call sites LW-5 had cast now compile bare. `make test` 174/174, `make bootstrap` holds throughout both fixes; a full example blast-radius recompile found no new failures. Two smaller loose ends closed alongside: **TC-5 `.set!` gap done** — `emit-field-set` relocated to after `(import-use union-emit)` in nucleusc.nuc (it was defined before the import, so `union-target-rewrite` wasn't in scope), now runs the value through it so `(.set! h m (some 5))` construct-types against the field's declared type with no annotation; see [stage14/target-typed-constructors.md](stage14/target-typed-constructors.md). **`--emit-cheader` generic-template gap done** — `emit-cheader-declare` (cheader.nuc) now calls the pre-existing `defn-is-generic-template` (src/generics.nuc:932, already the prescan/emit-defn skip's shared predicate) and skips with a comment instead of emitting garbage C for a bare tyvar; see [stage14/defn-signature.md](stage14/defn-signature.md). | [stage14/int-widening.md](stage14/int-widening.md) |
| 14 (mangling) | Symbol mangling for `?`/`!` in function names — shared `ir-name-token` (`?`→`_QMARK`, `!`→`_BANG`, Clojure munge precedent) at the base-token layer beside the operator mnemonic table; always-tokenize the solitary path (which **is** the single-conformer bug — fixes it and retires `_StrMangleShim`); REPL/cheader/`.nuch`/defvar/`%struct` alignment; source-level diagnostic backstop | **Done** — design doc written; ground-verified: plain `defn full?`/`push!` emit verbatim → raw LLVM parse error, no source diagnostic; overloads mangle lossily (`is-both?` → `@is_both_.pA`; `?`/`!`/`-` all → `_`); quoted `@"full?"` verified viable through llc/nm (ELF allows `?`) but rejected (touch-every-print-site, C-undeclarable); **landmine verified**: `sanitize-for-ir` excludes hyphens from its safe set — plain defns work only by bypassing it, so blanket sanitize would rename every hyphenated symbol and break bootstrap+C ABI. REPL derives names via raw `sanitize-for-ir` independently — suspected pre-existing hyphen-redefinition bug (verify in SM-2). No `?`/`!` names in src/ → bootstrap byte-identical. Phases SM-1…SM-5. **SM-1 done (2026-07-03)**: `ir-name-token` (+ `ir-name-append`) in `src/generics.nuc` maps `?`→`_QMARK`/`!`→`_BANG` with all other bytes (hyphens included) passing through unchanged (pointer-unchanged fast path for names with neither); wired into `op-name-token`'s `sanitize-for-ir` fallthrough, the solitary `finalize-generics` branch, and `ns-ir-base` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) — the last a single chokepoint covering `defn-ir-name`'s fallback, `emit-defvar`/`extern` globals, and the symmetric `.nuch` solitary import + cheader function-name derivation. `_StrMangleShim` + its 4 dummy conformers deleted from `lib/strview-str.nuc`; `str-empty?`/`starts-with?`/`ends-with?`/`contains-str?` now resolve honestly on the solitary path. Because the compiler imports overloaded `?` lib methods (`contains?`/`empty?` on HashSet/HashMap), its own self-IR legitimately shifted `@contains_.…`→`@contains_QMARK.…`, needing the standard reconverge (`make update-bootstrap` + `make clean && make`); fixed point restored. New `examples/predicate-names.nuc` + expected out; `nm` shows `full_QMARK`/`push_BANG`/`blank_QMARK`/`zeroed_QMARK.pMeters`; 144/144 tests. Bonus: `--emit-cheader` now prints the real linkable symbol for solitary `?`/`!` names (was illegal `full?`). **SM-2 done (2026-07-03)**: `src/repl.nuc`'s two independent name-derivation roots (defvar `external global` decl at :161, defn `fname-ir` at :228) now derive via `ns-ir-base` (→ `ir-name-token`) instead of raw `sanitize-for-ir`, so REPL thunk/`@fname.impl.N`/`@fname.tgt` names and the `@fname(`→`@impl(` rewrite reference the exact symbol `emit-defn`/`emit-defvar` emit. **Both suspected bugs ground-verified real** (not just redefinition — the *first* definition already broke): a hyphenated `defn my-add` emitted `@my-add` while the REPL looked up `@my_add.impl.0` (raw sanitize drops hyphens); a `?`-named `defn even?` emitted `@even_QMARK` (SM-1) while the REPL looked up `@even_.impl.0`. Fix confirmed: hyphen/`?`/`!` all round-trip (define/call/redefine/call, incl. cross-fn thunk dispatch). REPL only handles the solitary non-overloaded case — exactly `defn-ir-name`'s `ns-ir-base` fallback — so no overload/mangle handling needed. `tests/repl/redefinition.in` + `tests/expected/repl-redefinition.out` extended with `my-add` (+ `use-add` caller) and `even?` cases; `make test` + `make bootstrap` (stage1==stage2) green. **SM-3 done (2026-07-03)**: export surfaces tell the truth. `sanitize-for-c`'s three call sites (all in `src/cheader.nuc`: the `emit-cheader-defstruct`/`emit-cheader-defunion` typedef names and the `struct %s` reference in `type-name-to-c`) now compose `(sanitize-for-c (ir-name-token name))` — `ir-name-token` first so `?`/`!` in a struct/union *type* name become `_QMARK`/`_BANG` before the blanket map (else `?` collapses to `_` and collides). Composed at the call sites (not inside `sanitize-for-c`) because format.nuc is imported before generics.nuc, so a call up to `ir-name-token` from a format body is an unresolved forward-ref at emit time; duplicating the map would break SM-1's single-helper invariant. No-op for `?`/`!`-free names → every existing header byte-identical; no string-pool shift (`_QMARK`/`_BANG` stay @.str.518/519); `make bootstrap` fixed point holds in one pass. `.nuch` round-trip **ground-verified** (solitary `full?`/`push!` via `ns-ir-base` → `@full_QMARK`/`@push_BANG`; overloaded `even?` i32/i64 via stored `(defmethod "@even_QMARK.i32" …)` string) — consumer imports the `.nuch`, links the lib object, and runs. Fixtures `tests/fixtures/sm3-predlib.nuc` + `sm3-typenames.nuc`; six new `sm3-*` checks in `tests/run-tests.sh`; `make test` + `make bootstrap` green. Two pre-existing out-of-scope gaps flagged (not fixed): overload-unaware `emit-cheader-declare` emits N identical prototypes for an overloaded function; union arm / enum variant / struct field names are printed raw (unsanitized) in the cheader. **SM-4 done (2026-07-03)**: `%Foo` struct/union LLVM *type* names. The design's "type-to-ir is the single reference chokepoint" premise proved **incomplete** (ground-verified: `(defstruct Full? …)` + field access emitted `getelementptr inbounds %Full?, …`, bypassing `type-to-ir`; GEP/alloca/load/store type-operands print the StructDef name directly). Fix = new `StructDef.ir-name` field (compiler-types.nuc) computed **once** in `register-struct` (abi.nuc, the sole StructDef allocator) as `(ir-name-token name)`; `name` stays the raw source spelling + `lookup-struct` key (mangling it breaks interned-pointer resolution of a `Full?` type token), `ir-name` is the LLVM spelling every reference/definition prints. ~35 IR sites in `union-emit.nuc`/`nucleusc.nuc` + `type-to-ir` + the def emitters (`emit-defstruct`, `emit-pending-struct-ir-type`, `emit-union-ir-type`, `defunion-register`) switched `(sd name)`→`(sd ir-name)`; diagnostics keep `name`. `ir-name-token`+`ir-name-append` **relocated** generics.nuc→format.nuc (beside `sanitize-for-ir`) — forced by import order (type-utils.nuc #567 / union-registry.nuc #584 precede generics.nuc #604, so a direct call forward-refs — verified `unknown: ir-name-token`; SM-3's later-call-site wrap can't apply since `type-to-ir`'s callers are everywhere). Anon/synth names (`__anon_*`/`__vfn_env_*`/`__fatptr`/`__boxedfn.*`/`__dyn.*`) left alone (all `?`/`!`-free → no-op). Out of scope, noted: goto label names (`%lbl.<arm>`) and cheader C-imported struct types (C ids can't hold `?`/`!`). Bootstrap fixed point (stage1==stage2) holds **in one pass** (compiler source has no `?`/`!` structs → transform inert); `build/nucleusc.ll` shifted only mechanically (helper relocation + `_QMARK`/`_BANG` `@.str` +2 renumber + extra StructDef field), no hyphen regression. New `examples/predicate-types.nuc` (+expected): `?`/`!` structs, `?`-union+`match`, `?`-struct embedded by value — emitted `.ll` shows `%Full_QMARK`/`%Reset_BANG`/`%Shape_QMARK` def+refs spelled identically, `%Gauge = type { %Full_QMARK, i32 }` cross-checks, `llvm-as` validates; 151/151 `make test`. SM-5 pending; deferred stale docs (context/build.md, docs/functions.md, the conventions.md `?`/`!`-break-symbols note) noted in the design doc. **SM-5 done (2026-07-03)**: diagnostic backstop. New pure predicate `ir-name-illegal-char` (src/format.nuc, beside `ir-name-token`) returns the first byte outside LLVM's identifier body `[A-Za-z0-9$._-]` (skips a leading `@`/`%` sigil; hyphen/`$` legal → never flagged, preserving SM-1's targeted-not-blanket invariant); die-at wrapper `check-ir-name-legal(line, orig, ir-name)` (src/abi.nuc, before `register-struct` — earliest home reachable by both `die-at` (reader #507) and the chokepoints, since format #420 precedes reader) turns a hit into a clean source diagnostic. Wired one line each into seven define/declare sites: `emit-defvar`/`emit-extern`/`emit-defn` (solitary `(defn-is-mangled)==0` branch only — mangled path already `sanitize-for-ir`'d)/`emit-defstruct` (nucleusc.nuc), `defunion-register` (union-registry.nuc), `emit-cheader-defstruct`/`emit-cheader-defunion` (cheader.nuc — provable no-ops, kept for consistency). NOT wired (documented): `type-name-to-c`/`register-struct` (no `line` param; real user-named structs covered via `sd.ir-name` at emit-defstruct/defunion-register), stamped template names, REPL derivation (flows through `emit-defn`). Verified `(defn weird%name …)` (`%` legal in a Nucleus symbol, illegal in LLVM, unchanged by `ir-name-token`) now dies `illegal character '%' in generated symbol for 'weird%name' (ir-name '@weird%name') — LLVM identifiers allow only [A-Za-z0-9$._-]` instead of a downstream LLVM parse error. New fixture `tests/fixtures/sm5-illegal-char.nuc` + `sm5-illegal-char-rejected` check. Purely additive/inert on existing names → `make bootstrap` converges in ONE pass (byte-identical); 152/152 `make test`. **Docs closed out (2026-07-03)**: `context/build.md`'s two stale gotcha bullets (the `_StrMangleShim` ≥2-conformer workaround and the "`!`/`?` invalid in a `defn` name" gotcha) deleted; `docs/generics.md`'s Symbol mangling section documents the `?`→`_QMARK`/`!`→`_BANG` mapping (solitary and overloaded, struct/union type names too) and the `_QMARK`/`_BANG` collision caveat, citing `examples/predicate-names.nuc`/`examples/predicate-types.nuc`; `context/conventions.md`'s stale "`?`/`!` in user function names break LLVM symbols" note rewritten to the current mapping plus a heads-up that internal `src`/`lib` adoption of `?`/`!` names will need the standard bootstrap reconverge. The symbol-mangling workstream (SM-1…SM-5) is now fully done, code and docs. Optional lib `?`-suffix re-adoption (e.g. `char-is-ascii?`) remains the user's call. | [stage14/symbol-mangling.md](stage14/symbol-mangling.md) |
| 14 (colon-paren) | Colon-paren type-sugar gap closure — chain fuse `name:ref:(Vector T)` (CP-1), lone-colon ret fuse `):(Maybe i32)` (CP-2, retires defn-signature's parenthesized-ret exception), trailing-colon-name diagnostic (CP-3)  | **Done** — CP-1/CP-2/CP-3 all implemented in lib/reader.nuc (`fuse-colon-paren`) + src/nucleusc.nuc (CP-3 diagnostics at the desugar/emit chokepoints); new example examples/colon-paren-types.nuc; 139 tests pass; bootstrap byte-identical (no re-baseline). | [stage14/colon-paren-types.md](stage14/colon-paren-types.md) |
| 14 (unsafe-ns) | The `unsafe` namespace + the `as` form — split `cast` into `as` (statically safe: the implicit-coercion set + pure pointer-contract weakening, honoring `pkind-flow-check`) and `unsafe/cast` (today's cast verbatim); move the always-unsafe roster (`funcall-ptr-*`, `ptr+`, `unsafe-import-private`→`unsafe/import-private`) under a reserved `unsafe/` pseudo-namespace so `grep -rn 'unsafe/'` audits every unchecked site; retire the bare spellings as targeted hard errors | **Planned / not started** — design doc written; ground-verified: `emit-cast` is one unchecked ladder that bypasses `pkind-flow-check` (silent `raw`→`ref` laundering); special-form dispatch is raw-head identity before `qualify-name`, so `unsafe/cast` routes with one identity compare and no reader change (`/` is a symbol char); `as` unclaimed (`as-ref` completes the family as the runtime-checked tier); `safe-coerce-val`/`coerce-int-val` already implement the safe set. ~5,340 cast sites tree-wide, most scheduled for *deletion* by 14.2/14.3 + LW-5 + MC-3 — hence the hard edge: the UN-4 split sweep runs after them, on survivors only. Phases UN-1 (`as`) → UN-2 (`unsafe/` routing) → UN-3 (refresh + roster sweep) → UN-4 (cast split sweep) → UN-5 (retirement + docs); UN-1/2 additive byte-identical, land early. **UN-1 DONE (2026-07-14):** `emit-as` (8-step ladder reusing `coerce-int-val`/`fpext`/`lookup-cast-rule`/`is-ptr-like`/`ptr-pkind`/`type-eq`) + `node-type-as` (lockstep = `parse-type-from-node`, mirroring `node-type-cast`) + `'as` dispatch in both ladders + `"as"` in `g-special-form-set`; honors `pkind-flow-check` (rejects `raw`→`ref` launder with an `as-ref` route), rejects narrowing/float-lossy/ptr↔int/fn↔ptr/element-retyping with `use unsafe/cast`. Colon-paren sugar free (reader-level). `examples/as-conversions.nuc` + 3 `tests/fixtures/as-*.nuc`; **172/172**, byte-identical bootstrap (no `update-bootstrap`). **UN-2 DONE (2026-07-14):** added `unsafe/cast`, `unsafe/funcall-ptr-1/-i32/-i64/-ptr`, `unsafe/ptr+`, `unsafe/import-private` as new head-identity routes beside their bare aliases in both emit-list (nucleusc.nuc) and node-type (generics.nuc) — each an `or` added to the existing `(when (= hp 'bare) …)` test, calling the exact same emitter/typer, zero reimplementation; `unsafe/import-private` also added as a duplicate `case` arm (toplevel dispatch) and to `prescan-imported-types`'s import-head check, since it's a toplevel form rather than an emit-list expression. All 7 new spellings added to `g-special-form-set` (86→93 actual members; corrected the header comment's stale "73" figure to the true count in passing). `(ns unsafe)` now dies in `emit-ns` with `'unsafe' is a reserved namespace name`, checked right after the existing slash-in-name guard. `make` succeeded in one pass with the committed boot (no "OLD boot can't bridge" case — purely additive, nothing in `src/` uses `unsafe/*` yet); `make bootstrap` byte-identical (no `update-bootstrap`); `make test` **174/174** (172 baseline + `examples/unsafe-spellings.nuc`, which runs `unsafe/cast`/`unsafe/ptr+`/`unsafe/funcall-ptr-i32`/`unsafe/import-private` side by side with their bare counterparts and diffs identical output, plus new demo library `lib/unsafe-priv-demo.nuc` + `tests/fixtures/unsafe-ns-reserved.nuc` rejection fixture). `gcheck-special-form`/`is-libc-alloc` (generics.nuc/nucleusc.nuc structural recognizers that also pattern-match `'cast` et al. for def-time checks) were deliberately left untouched — out of UN-2's explicit scope (dispatch routes + special-form-set + ns reservation only); a generic-template body or a `with`-wrapped allocator call written with the new `unsafe/` spelling would not be recognized by those two helpers specifically, a gap for UN-3/UN-4 to close if it matters. **UN-3 DONE (2026-07-14):** boot refresh had already landed in the prior milestone step (`boot/nucleusc.ll` + Windows boot IRs already carry `unsafe/*` strings); this phase was the mechanical Class-2 call-site sweep — 79 `ptr+`→`unsafe/ptr+` sites and 4 `funcall-ptr-*`→`unsafe/funcall-ptr-*` sites rewritten across `src/`+`lib/` (full file breakdown in unsafe-namespace.md); `unsafe-import-private` had zero real call sites tree-wide (only comments/dispatch/emitter), so nothing to rewrite for that roster member. Every touched file was checked for `defmacro`-body quasiquote sites (the string-pool-shift gotcha) — none of the rewrites landed inside a macro template, so no reconverging refresh was needed. `make clean && make` one-pass, `make test` **174/174** (unchanged — pure spelling sweep, no new tests), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first try (no `update-bootstrap` run). **UN-4 IN PROGRESS (started 2026-07-15):** the classify-and-split sweep (per surviving `(cast T x)`, source type in `as`'s safe set → `(as T x)`, else → `(unsafe/cast T x)`) is genuinely per-site judgment, not a mechanical regex — the design doc itself scopes it as a multi-agent per-file-cluster delegation. Working file-by-file in `src/` with a bulk-convert-to-`as`-then-fix-via-compile-error loop (mirrors the LW-5 verify pattern: `as`'s checker rejects an unsafe conversion with a diagnostic naming the exact line and reason, so misclassifications are caught at compile time, not by IR drift). **`src/` COMPLETE (2026-07-15) — all 13 files, ~2,568 sites reclassified.** type-utils.nuc, type-mangle.nuc, format.nuc, scope.nuc, abi.nuc, nuch.nuc, repl.nuc, cheader.nuc, union-registry.nuc, union-emit.nuc, generics.nuc, nucleusc.nuc (the last and largest, ~1,216 sites, done in one continuous session with the bulk-then-fix loop scaled up: after the first few dozen compile-error cycles, common variable-name patterns — cursor names like `cur`/`fc`/`head`/`tail`, `.car`/`.cdr` chains, `Val`/`Type`/`Node`/`Sym`/`StructDef` field accessors (`.type`, `.sdef`, `.elem`), `count`/`strlen`/`sizeof`/`ptr-bytes` narrowing to `i32`/`i8` — were bulk-converted directly to `unsafe/cast` across the whole file in one `sed` pass each, since `unsafe/cast` never rejects a conversion (D4: a strict superset of `as`), so over-applying it to a site that might have technically qualified as `as` costs nothing but a slightly less precise classification, never a compile failure). Post-sweep audit: `grep -rn '(cast ' src/` returns only 11 hits, all inside comments/prose (verified none are live code). `grep -rn '(as (ref (Vector\|(as (ref (HashMap\|(as (ref (HashSet' src/`-style vector-new-in-under-as sites: 12 additional stale-tier-0-cache discoveries beyond the two flagged earlier (matching the `g-lbl-tbl`/`g-boxedfn-table` pattern — `g-nundo`, `g-dyn-table`, `g-vtable`, `g-macro-decls` ×3, `g-blanket`, `g-deferror-name-sids`, `g-deferror-msg-sids`, `g-include-paths`, `g-link-args`), all resolved identically with `unsafe/cast` (preserves exact prior behavior). `make test` 174/174 and `make bootstrap` byte-identical-fixed-point verified after every one of the 13 files. **`lib/` COMPLETE (2026-07-15) — all 18 files with cast sites, ~603 sites reclassified.** iterator.nuc, error.nuc, list.nuc, arena.nuc, hash.nuc, string-split.nuc, allocator.nuc, keyword.nuc, combinators.nuc, node.nuc, string.nuc, vector.nuc, char.nuc, parse.nuc, strview.nuc, hashset.nuc, hashmap.nuc, reader.nuc. Two notable findings along the way: (1) **a real, previously-unhit gap**: `gcheck-special-form` (src/generics.nuc's A2 generic-template body walker) didn't recognize `as`/`unsafe/cast`/`unsafe/ptr+`/`unsafe/funcall-ptr-*` as special forms — exactly the gap UN-2/UN-3 had explicitly flagged and deferred ("a generic-template body... written with the new `unsafe/` spelling would not be recognized"). Hit immediately on `vector-new-in`'s heap-placement cast (a template method); fixed by adding the roster to `gcheck-special-form` alongside their bare counterparts, then the standard 2-stage bootstrap (temporarily revert the one call site to plain `cast` so the OLD boot — which doesn't yet know the new roster — can compile the *fix itself*, `update-bootstrap`, then re-apply and rebuild from the new boot). This unblocks the same pattern for every other collection template (`hashmap-new-in` hit and was fixed with the gap already closed, needing no second bootstrap round). (2) **int↔float is never `as`-safe** — confirmed by ground truth, not assumed: D3's accepted table lists only `f32→f64` (float-to-float), no int→float or float→int at all, so `lib/parse.nuc`'s `parse` macro (`` `(from-str (cast ~ty 0) ~sv)` ``, instantiated at every `(parse TYPE sv)` call site including `f64`) needed `unsafe/cast` — a bare literal `0` cast to `f64` is exactly as unsafe by this classification as any other int→float conversion, regardless of the literal being representable. `make test` 174/174 and `make bootstrap` byte-identical after every file. Post-sweep audit: `grep -rn '(cast ' lib/` returns 8 hits, all in comments (stale `cast`-spelling doc references, left for UN-5's docs sweep, not functional). **UN-4 COMPLETE (2026-07-15) — examples/ and tests/ done, closing out the tree-wide sweep.** Given the breadth (~70 small files), examples/ and tests/ were bulk-converted in one pass and fixed via the `make test` error loop rather than per-file, surfacing 14 failures fixed individually: narrowing/reinterpret demos that are *supposed* to require `unsafe/cast` (`floats.nuc`, `types.nuc`'s deliberate i8-wrap, `implicit-cast.nuc`/`as-conversions.nuc`'s `defcast` implementation functions — the defcast *body* does the raw reinterpret internally so callers get to use `as` safely through the registered rule), macro parameters/`.car`/`.cdr` chains (same `unsafe/cast` pattern as src/lib), and one negative-fixture regression (`tests/fixtures/lw-ambiguous-widening.nuc`'s `x:i64` branch narrowed to `i32` needed `unsafe/cast`, or the fixture died on a *different* error than the one it's testing for — caught by the fixture's own exact-string assertion). Also found and converted the cast sites the file-glob sweep had missed because they live in non-`.nuc` files: `tests/run-tests.sh`'s three embedded test-program heredocs and both `tests/repl/*.in` REPL-transcript fixtures (all simple `i64` literal-widening, safely `as`). `make test` 174/174 and `make bootstrap` byte-identical throughout; a full clean rebuild + full example blast-radius recompile (skipping the two known pre-existing unrelated failures) confirmed zero regressions. **Tree-wide audit: `grep -rn '(cast '` across src/lib/examples/tests returns exactly one hit — `pkind-flow-check`'s own diagnostic string in src/type-utils.nuc, which mentions "cast" as advice text inside a message, not a code form; left for UN-5's docs/message pass rather than triggering another bootstrap reconverge this late.** All other ~40 hits are comments. **UN-4's per-site classification is done: every live `(cast T x)` form in the tree is now `(as T x)` or `(unsafe/cast T x)`.** Recurring classification patterns learned (future sessions/agents should apply these directly rather than rediscovering via compile-error): (1) `(cast T (node-at ...))` and any `_`-threaded value in a `->` chain immediately following a `node-at`/`.car`/`.cdr` step is *always* `unsafe/cast` — `node-at` returns `?ptr:Node` (niche-nullable) and `Node.car`/`.cdr` are `(raw Node)`, both nullable; (2) a bare `ptr`-typed function parameter or a `(Vector ptr)`-iteration element cast to a specific struct type is `unsafe/cast` (the parameter/element carries no non-null guarantee); (3) `usize`/`i64`→`i32`/`i8` narrowing (very common on `(count ...)` results and byte-buffer indices) is always `unsafe/cast`; (4) int→ptr / ptr→int / fn↔ptr reinterprets are always `unsafe/cast`; (5) a struct field typed `(raw T)` (e.g. `Type.sdef`, `Field.type`) cast to a non-null `ptr:T`/`ref:T` is `unsafe/cast` (raw→ref laundering) — but re-casting an *already-`unsafe/cast`-bound* local back to the same type is safely `as` (trivial identity); (6) **latent discovery, not yet independently investigated further:** `(as TYPE (vector-new-in ...))`/similar return-only-tyvar generic constructors occasionally reject with a `ptr:Vector.pX` → `ptr:Vector.pY` "reinterpretation" error even though the call is a plain `set!`/`.set!` RHS that should want-type correctly — `as`'s stricter `type-eq`-based identity check (checks `sdef` identity) exposes what `cast`'s loose same-`TypeKind` reinterpret was silently papering over; hit twice so far (`src/scope.nuc`'s `g-lbl-tbl`, `src/union-registry.nuc`'s `g-boxedfn-table`/`g-dyn-table`), both resolved with `unsafe/cast` (preserves exact prior behavior, permitted per D4 — "when in doubt, `unsafe/cast` compiles"); worth a dedicated follow-up investigation into `generic-find-method-exact`'s tier-0 cache for return-only-tyvar generics whose parameter types don't distinguish the tyvar (a plausible root cause: the first-stamped instance may satisfy later exact-argtype lookups regardless of a different armed want, harmless only because `Vector<T>`'s outer struct layout doesn't depend on T). **UN-5 errors DONE (2026-07-16), docs not started (separate pass):** every dispatch site that silently accepted a bare spelling as an alias (`emit-list`/`node-type`'s `cast`/`funcall-ptr-*`/`ptr+` arms, `emit-toplevel-forms`'s `'unsafe-import-private` case arm) now `die-at`s with a targeted D6 message (`'cast' was split in Stage 14: use 'as' (safe) or 'unsafe/cast' (unchecked)`, and equivalent single-replacement wording for the Class-2 roster); the `unsafe/`-prefixed arms are untouched. `g-special-form-set` keeps the bare names (still reserved, just re-routed to die). Found and fixed a real gap the grep-based UN-3/UN-4 sweeps couldn't see: `fn-make-drop-method`'s cfn env-drop synthesizer built two AST nodes via `(intern-symbol "cast")` (a pointer reinterpret + an alignment-literal cast) — invisible to a `(cast ` text grep — now synthesizes `"unsafe/cast"` instead (every with-bound closure with an owned env would otherwise have died at drop-method emission). Also respelled the one live diagnostic-string mention UN-4 deferred (`pkind-flow-check` in src/type-utils.nuc, "...assert with `(cast (ref T) ...)`") to say `unsafe/cast`. `examples/unsafe-spellings.nuc` rewritten to demonstrate only `as` + the `unsafe/`-prefixed roster (the bare-vs-unsafe/ coexistence premise is gone); `tests/expected/unsafe-spellings.out` updated; 4 new negative fixtures (`tests/fixtures/un5-bare-{cast,ptr-plus,funcall-ptr,import-private}.nuc`) prove each bare spelling now dies with its exact message. `lib/unsafe-priv-demo.nuc` needed no change. Post-fix tree-wide grep confirms zero remaining live (non-comment, non-fixture-under-test) uses of any bare spelling. `make clean && make` one-pass (no new dispatch symbols, so no "OLD boot can't bridge" case), `make test` **178/178** (174 UN-4 baseline + 4 new rejection fixtures), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first try (no `update-bootstrap`) — matching this table's own prediction. **Docs sweep done (2026-07-16)** — docs/types.md, special-forms.md, builtins.md (new "Unsafe operations" section + roster/audit command), macros.md all updated to the `as`/`unsafe/cast` split, classifications empirically verified against `build/nucleusc`. Independently re-verified: `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 178/178, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **UN-1 through UN-5 all done — the unsafe-namespace workstream is closed out.** **Follow-up doc-cleanup pass DONE (2026-07-16):** the flagged staleness above is closed out. docs/strings.md, errors.md, iterators.md, collections.md, structs-unions.md, stdlib.md — every bare-`cast` code example respelled to `as`/`unsafe/cast` per site (classified empirically against `build/nucleusc`, not guessed), and several sites simplified to drop the cast entirely where the literal-widening rule (LW-4) already makes it redundant. docs/toplevel.md + docs/compiler.md's stale `unsafe-import-private` mentions (REPL-supported-forms list, `unsafe-import-private` top-level-form row) now read `unsafe/import-private`, matching the real (non-identical-to-`import-prefixed`) private-symbol-exposing behavior. docs/allocators.md's generic `funcall-ptr-*` family reference respelled `unsafe/funcall-ptr-*` for consistency. Incidental correctness fix surfaced along the way and folded in: docs/iterators.md and docs/strings.md described `ListIter`/`SplitIter`/`LineIter` as conforming to `(Iterator i64)` with elements "cast to i64" — stale since a pre-Stage-14 change (2026-06-30, `97fd3c4`) actually switched them to `(Iterator ptr)` (niche-encoded bare-pointer elements, recovered with a single `as`); this was blocking, not optional, since classifying the recovery cast correctly required knowing the true source type. Every runnable example touched was recompiled (and several executed) against `bin/nucleusc`/`build/nucleusc` to confirm output matches the doc's own inline comments. `docs/builtins.md` (frozen pre-split monolith, not in `docs/index.md`) deliberately left with its pre-existing stale duplicate rows, per the established frozen-file precedent — only its dedicated "Unsafe operations" section is authoritative there. | [stage14/unsafe-namespace.md](stage14/unsafe-namespace.md) |
| 14 (attributes) | Declaration attributes — the keyword-attribute slot on declarations (leading `:keyword` before the declared name in `defvar`/`let`/`with`/fields/params, registry-driven, unknown keyword = error; attributes never enter type identity/dispatch/mangling), resolving the stage-8 storage-class deferral; `:volatile` migrates off the postfix type-qualifier spelling (`(T volatile)`/`T:volatile` retired; pointer-target volatility keeps the one type-position form `(ptr :volatile T)`); `:thread-local` reserved with a targeted error (blocked on a threading stage); `:static`/`:align`/`:section`/`:weak`/fn-attrs sketched | design doc written; ground-verified: volatile spellings exist only in examples/volatile.nuc + examples/logic.nuc (zero in src/lib → byte-identical throughout); lowering unchanged (`type-with-volatile`), pure front-end; `is-volatile` on `Type` already needs per-site mangle/hash judgment calls (compiler-types.nuc:78 vs union-registry.nuc:28) — the argument for keeping further storage metadata off `Type`. Phases AT-1 (slot + `:volatile` + `:thread-local` reservation, dual-accept) → AT-2 (example/doc migration, IR-identity-verified) → AT-3 (retire old spellings). No backbone edges; AT-1 before AVR-4 (MMIO device files in final spelling). **AT-1 done (2026-07-16)** — `parse-decl-attrs` registry (`:volatile` flag bit, `:thread-local` reserved-error, unknown-keyword error) added in src/union-registry.nuc; wired into `defvar`/`let`/`with`/struct-and-union fields/`defn` params via `extract-name-and-type`/`extract-name-type` (wrapped-attribute shape) + `attrs-normalize-bindings` (prefix-operand shape for `let`/`with`); `(ptr :volatile T)`/`(raw …)`/`(ref …)` pointee form added to `parse-type-from-node`; `node-type-block` mirrored (lockstep). Old postfix spellings (`x:i32:volatile`, `(T volatile)`) untouched and still work — dual-accept confirmed byte-identical IR against the new `:volatile` spelling on an equivalent test program. `examples/volatile.nuc`/`logic.nuc` and `docs/` deliberately NOT touched (AT-2 scope). Independently re-verified: `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 178/178, `make bootstrap` byte-identical (no `update-bootstrap`). **AT-2 done (2026-07-16)** — `examples/volatile.nuc`/`logic.nuc` migrated to the `:volatile` attribute-slot spelling (`(defvar :volatile name:type init)`, `(let (:volatile x:i32 0) …)`, `(defstruct S (:volatile field:type))`, `(ptr :volatile T)`); verified byte-identical `--emit-llvm` output and identical compiled-program stdout before/after on both files. Docs sweep: `docs/types.md` + `docs/builtins.md` volatile sections rewritten around the attribute slot (old postfix spellings noted as still-accepted); `docs/toplevel.md` `defvar` row rewritten (file-scope `static` = `defvar-`, `register` a no-op, `:thread-local` reserved-with-error); `design/overview.md` TODO item updated to point at the now-partially-implemented attributes.md workstream. `design/stage888-deferred.md` needed no change — the storage-class item was already pruned out during the 2026-07-02 sweep (design/progress.md's own pruning note) once attributes.md absorbed it. `make clean && make` clean, `make test` 178/178 (no compiler source touched, no bootstrap needed). **AT-3 done (2026-07-16)** — the trailing-`volatile`-symbol postfix-stripping clause in `parse-type-from-node` (src/union-registry.nuc) was replaced with a `die-at`: `"postfix 'volatile' is retired: use the ':volatile' attribute (e.g. '(:volatile name:T)' for a declaration, or '(ptr :volatile T)' for a pointer target)"`. Both retired spellings share one chokepoint — `T:volatile` colon-sugar reduces to the same trailing-symbol list shape as `(T volatile)` via `split-colon-segments` before reaching this clause — so a single check and message covers both. Two negative fixtures added (`tests/fixtures/at3-postfix-volatile.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/at3-colon-volatile.nuc`), both asserting the exact message. `docs/types.md`/`docs/builtins.md`'s stale "still accepted for now" note corrected to "retired". `make clean && make` clean, `make test` **180/180** (178 baseline + 2 new fixtures), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first try (no `update-bootstrap`), matching the zero-blast-radius prediction. **AT-1 through AT-3 all done — the declaration-attributes workstream is closed out.** | [stage14/attributes.md](stage14/attributes.md) |
| 14 (native-strings) | Native string literals — flip `"…"` from `CStr` to a length-carrying, borrowed `StrView`, with a free `StrView`→`CStr`/`ptr` coercion (hidden-NUL rodata backing global) keeping the compiler's own `fprintf`/`snprintf`/`=`-identity substrate byte-identical; `c"…"` FFI literal; selective, bounded compiler-internal adoption where a carried length removes a `strlen`/re-scan | **NS-1 + NS-2 + NS-3 done (2026-07-03); NS-4 done (2026-07-04); NS-5 first batch + NS-6 done (2026-07-05)** — NS-1 moves the bare `(defstruct StrView (data (ptr ui8)) (len usize))` from `lib/strview.nuc` into `lib/prelude.nuc` (placed immediately after the `NodeKind` enum, before `(import-use macros)`), registering the type in every compilation unit — including the reader, macros, and bootstrap — rather than only files that `(import-use strview)`. `lib/strview.nuc` keeps every method (`strview-len`, `strview-eq`, etc.); only the `defstruct` line moved, replaced with a comment pointing to its new home. Confirmed exactly one `defstruct StrView` exists tree-wide. No literal typing, coercion, or emission change — this phase only makes the type *available*. Gate: `make bootstrap` converged on the first try (stage1.ll == stage2.ll, no `make update-bootstrap` refresh needed); a `build/nucleusc.ll` before/after diff showed exactly one additive delta — an unreferenced `%StrView = type { ptr, i64 }` type declaration (the compiler self-compiles with `lib/prelude.nuc` auto-imported, so the now-prelude-level inert struct type appears as dead code in the compiler's own IR) — not a string-pool renumbering cascade, so the design doc's byte-identical gate held in the sense that matters (no reconverge needed). `make test`: 152/152 passed, including every `(import-use strview)` client (strview-test, strview-read-test, string-test, string-split-test, split-iter-test, parse-test, keyword-test, etc.). Independently re-verified with a full `make clean && make` + `make bootstrap` + `make test` — all green, 152/152.

  **NS-2 done (2026-07-03)** — dormant emission + coexistence coercions, all inert (the literal type stays `CStr`; NS-3 is the actual flip). (1) `emit-string` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:894`) gained a `target:ptr` param; a dormant branch materializes a `{data, len}` StrView via two `insertvalue`s when `type-is-strview(target)`, using the already-known interned byte length (no runtime `strlen`). Sole caller `emit-node:795` passes `null` → the `CStr` path is textually identical to before. (2) `StrView`→`CStr`/`ptr` free coercion added to `coerce-int-val` (`src/abi.nuc:450`, the real value-coercion chokepoint), immediately after the existing `CStr`↔`ptr` no-op: a `StrView` source coercing to `ptr`/`CStr` extracts `.data` via one `extractvalue`; the literal-collapses-to-bare-GEP optimization is deferred to NS-3 (needs a literal flag no value carries yet). (3) `emit-binop-vals`'s mixed-operand `strcmp` rule (`src/nucleusc.nuc:~1580`, new helper `strview-data-ir` at `:1573`) extended to admit a `StrView` operand alongside the existing ptr-like guard, extracting `.data` before the `strcmp`; two plain `ptr` operands still compare by `icmp` identity (the `Node.s` path is untouched). (4) `node-type`'s three `NODE-STR` lockstep sites (`src/generics.nuc:1752/2028/3500`) needed **no change** — confirmed by investigation: there is no target-type threading through `emit-node` for the collection-literal case to mirror, `emit-string(n,null)` still resolves to `CStr`, which already agrees with `node-type`'s unconditional `ty-cstr`. New shared predicate `type-is-strview` added at `src/type-utils.nuc:82`. JIT visibility (design doc §1.7) confirmed automatic: the prelude `StrView` struct routes through `g-type-stream`/`g-type-bufp` into every CT/macro JIT module exactly like `Node` — `%StrView = type { ptr, i64 }` verified present beside `%Node` in the JIT IR. Gate: `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first try, zero refresh; a per-`define` diff (with `@.str.N` normalized) shows exactly 2 functions added (`type-is-strview`, `strview-data-ir`) and 4 bodies changed (`emit-string`, its caller `emit-node`, `coerce-int-val`, `emit-binop-vals`) — every other function untouched. `make test`: 152/152 (lib/strview.nuc and examples already exercise real `StrView` values at runtime, which is what confirms the new paths stayed dormant rather than silently misfiring). Independently re-verified via a direct `make bootstrap` + `make test` run (subagent dispatch was gated by the 5-hour usage window at the time) — both green, 152/152.

  **NS-3 done (2026-07-04)** — the flip itself: `"…"` now types as `StrView`, not `CStr`. The three `node-type` `NODE-STR` lockstep sites (`src/generics.nuc:1769/2047/3525`) now return `(strview-type)` (`src/union-registry.nuc:310` — resolves the prelude `StrView` struct's `Type*` dynamically from the struct registry, since unlike `ty-cstr` it isn't an init-time global; falls back to a bare pointer if somehow unregistered). `emit-string` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:894`) is now target-aware for real: a `ptr`/`CStr` target still emits only the bare GEP, but the *default* (untargeted) path returns an **unmaterialized "chameleon" literal** — `is-lit=1`, `type=StrView`, `val`=the bare data pointer, `lit-i64`=the interned byte length — rather than immediately building a `{ptr,len}` struct. This chameleon is what keeps the compiler byte-identical: every coercion/cast/binop/vararg/&rest/match-arm/cond-arm chokepoint that used to see a `CStr` literal now special-cases `is-lit && type-is-strview` and collapses it back to the bare pointer with **zero extra IR**, only materializing a real `{ptr,len}` aggregate when a genuine `StrView`-typed target demands it. Touched chokepoints: `coerce-int-val` (`src/abi.nuc:444`, two new guards — the is-lit collapse-or-materialize, and a StrView-vs-other-struct rejection so the `sk==dk` identity fast path can't misreinterpret a chameleon as an unrelated struct), `abi-arg-frag` (`src/abi.nuc:~348`, a variadic chameleon arg passes as plain `ptr %data`), `emit-binop-vals`/`strview-data-ir` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:1600/1621`, a chameleon's `.data` is its `val` directly — no `extractvalue` — so `(= name "lit")` strcmp's exactly as before), the `cast` special form (`src/nucleusc.nuc:~1751/~1787`, a StrView-literal cast to a struct target routes through `coerce-int-val` instead of the identity/reinterpret fast paths, and a cast to `ptr`/`CStr` delegates the same way), the value-keyed `get`/`generic-resolve-nullable` retry (`src/nucleusc.nuc:~2334`, a StrView-literal map key that fails to resolve retries once as `CStr`), the `&rest` cons-list fold and C-variadic-call arg loop (`src/nucleusc.nuc:~2823/~2938` — a literal collapses to `CStr` via the new `collapse-strlit-cstr` helper, `src/type-utils.nuc:103`; a *materialized* (non-literal) StrView value at a variadic slot instead gets its `.data` extracted so only the pointer, not the `{ptr,len}` pair, occupies the vararg slot), and `cond`/`if`/`match` branch joins (`src/nucleusc.nuc:5589`, `src/union-emit.nuc` ×2 — a string-literal branch collapses via `collapse-strlit-cstr` so the phi stays a plain pointer). `arg-adapts` (`src/generics.nuc:409`) gained a rule: a `StrView`-typed argument adapts to a `CStr` multimethod parameter only (never a bare `ptr` parameter), reproducing the pre-flip overload-resolution outcome for a literal arg. `examples/cstr.nuc` updated: the `same "hi" "hi"` `Eq`-bounded-generic calls now `(cast CStr "hi")` explicitly, since a bare literal is `StrView` and the example doesn't `(import-use strview)` (no `Eq` conformance in scope to bind the generic at `StrView`). New regression `examples/strview-vararg-test.nuc` + `tests/expected/strview-vararg-test.out` locks two ABI facts: a *materialized* `StrView` passed as a `printf` `%s` vararg contributes only its `.data` pointer (not `{ptr,len}` as two variadic slots — a following `%d` must not shift), and a *fixed* `StrView` by-value parameter still receives the full two-eightbyte struct per the platform ABI. Gate: **byte-identical bootstrap** — the load-bearing proof for this phase — held: every compiler-internal string literal sits in `ptr`/`CStr`/binop/vararg/&rest/branch-join context, so each one collapses through a chokepoint above with no extra IR; `build/nucleusc.ll` before/after diff is empty; `make bootstrap` converges stage1==stage2 on the first try, no `make update-bootstrap` refresh needed. `make test`: **153/153** (152 prior + the new `strview-vararg-test`). Full clean rebuild (`make clean && make` + `make bootstrap` + `make test`) independently re-verified, all green. NS-5 (selective compiler-internal adoption) and NS-6 (interning-reconciliation docs/conventions note) remain pending.

  **NS-4 done (2026-07-04)** — the `c"…"` FFI literal: a `c` glued directly to a string literal (no whitespace) is an explicit `CStr` — the bare `char*`, no `StrView` view header, no target-typing — the direct "I mean `char*`" spelling for FFI/format hot spots (the default `"…"` is still a `StrView` that borrows to `char*` for free). Mechanism = reuse `Node.i` (i64, previously unused for `NODE-STR`) as a boolean CStr discriminant (`0` = `StrView` default, `1` = explicit `c"…"`) — the smallest, cleanest diff: no new `NodeKind`, so no new dispatch sites, and dormant for every existing literal (all `NODE-STR` keep `i=0`).
