Reader (`lib/reader.nuc`): `next-tok` detects `c` (99) immediately followed by `"` (34) via one-char lookahead (`char-at g-src (g-pos+1)`) — an adjacency otherwise unreachable, since a bare symbol `c` is delimiter-separated from a following string — consumes both, lexes the body exactly as a plain literal, and flags the token `i=1`; the `TOK-STRING`→`NODE-STR` conversion now copies `(t i)` into `NODE-STR.i` (a plain token has `i=0` from arena zero-fill, so byte-identical for every existing string). The three `node-type` `NODE-STR` lockstep sites (`src/generics.nuc:1769/2047/3525`) now branch: `i≠0` → `ty-cstr`, else `(strview-type)`. `emit-string` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:894`) gained an NS-4 short-circuit right after the bare GEP: `i≠0` returns the GEP as a plain `CStr` Val regardless of the ambient target, ahead of the NS-3 chameleon/StrView-materialization logic. Gate: **purely additive/dormant** — no compiler source uses `c"…"` (every internal `NODE-STR` has `i=0`), so `make bootstrap` converges stage1==stage2 **on the first try, byte-identical, no `update-bootstrap` refresh** (no new string-pool literals in `src`/`lib`; the added branches are present identically in both stages). New `examples/cstr-lit-test.nuc` + `tests/expected/cstr-lit-test.out` exercise all three contract cases: a `c"…"` literal as a `printf` `%s` vararg (bare `char*`), a `c"…"` literal into an `extern`-declared `strlen` expecting `char*`, and a plain `"…"` literal free-coercing to that same `char*` extern (NS-3 collapse, no regression); IR confirmed zero `insertvalue` for any of them. `make test`: **154/154** (153 prior + `cstr-lit-test`). Out of scope per the milestone split: quoted `c"…"` is not carried (`emit-quote-tree` deliberately does not store `Node.i` for `NODE-STR`, keeping quoted plain strings byte-identical); NS-5 selective adoption and NS-6 docs/conventions reconciliation (docs/strings.md, docs/types.md, the conventions note) are separate future items. | [stage14/native-strings.md](stage14/native-strings.md) |
  **NS-5 first batch done (2026-07-05)** — two polymorphic overloads (same name, different argument types/arity), not `-sv` name variants. (1) `intern-string` (`src/scope.nuc:98`) gained a `(sv:StrView):i32` overload beside the original `(bytes:ptr len:i32):i32`; the 2 literal callers (`src/union-emit.nuc:84`/`:214`, the `"nucleus: unwrap on error %s: %s\n"` diagnostic literal) now call `intern-string "…"` with no `strlen` — the StrView overload is selected by arity (1 arg). The other 8 callers pass `Node.s`/arena/`token` ptrs (2 args) and dispatch to the original. (2) `register-rmacro` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:9343`) gained a `(prefix:StrView wrap-sym:CStr):void` overload beside the original `(prefix:ptr wrap-sym:ptr):void`; `init-rmacros`'s 5 literal callers dispatch to it by type (StrView literal → StrView/CStr params). The `def-rmacro` handler's ptr caller (`:8548`) dispatches to the original. `RMacro.prefix` audit: sole consumer (`lib/reader.nuc:429-434`) does byte-`char-at` — never identity-compared, safe. Two gotchas hit (now in conventions.md + the design doc): (a) a by-value `sv:StrView` param needs `(addr-of sv)`→`ptr:StrView` before head-position `(.data)`/`(.len)` access (it's `TY-STRUCT`, not `TY-PTR`); (b) the StrView overload's second param must be `CStr`, not bare `ptr` — `StrView` adapts to `CStr` in dispatch but not to `ptr` (the error message that revealed this was improved: `generic-resolve`'s "no matching method" diagnostic now prints the caller's argument types via `arg-type-spellings`, e.g. `(StrView, StrView)`). conventions.md now has a "Prefer overloads over name variants" section. Gate: `make test` **166/166**. `make bootstrap`: stage1.ll == stage2.ll. | [stage14/native-strings.md](stage14/native-strings.md) |
  **NS-6 done (2026-07-05)** — interning reconciliation, docs, conventions. `context/conventions.md`'s "`CStr` is ABI-identical to `ptr`" section was retitled **"The string-type lattice: `ptr` / `CStr` / `StrView`"** and rewritten to name all three types up front (`ptr` identity-`=`, `CStr` content-`=` single-word, `StrView` 16-byte borrowed-view struct — the `"…"` literal's type since NS-3) and state the interned-`ptr` substrate stays `ptr`. Three additions: (1) a free `StrView`→`CStr`/`ptr` coercion paragraph (the hidden-NUL hinge — a literal's `data[len]` is `\00`, so `data` is a sound `char*`; no IR for a chameleon, one `extractvalue` for a materialized view); (2) the mixed-operand `=`/`!=` rule extended to fire `strcmp` when either operand is `CStr` *or* `StrView` (a `StrView` operand contributes its `.data`); (3) the `Node.s` trap generalized — never retype an identity-`=` `ptr` to `CStr` *or* `StrView`, naming the NS-5 exclusion list (`Node.s`, scope keys, struct-field names). The dispatch asymmetry note now records that a `StrView` argument adapts to a `CStr` parameter but not a bare `ptr` parameter. The interning distinction (`g-strs` per-literal rodata emitter vs `intern-str` `ptr`-identity dedup — §1.4 of the design doc) is unchanged and documented. `docs/strings.md` §3 and `docs/types.md` were already written during NS-3/NS-4 (the literal-type paragraph, the coercion-lattice row, the `"…"`/`c"…"` literal-type table) and needed no further edit; `docs/toplevel.md` does not name the literal's type and needed none. Gate: docs + one conventions note only; no IR, no code, byte-identical by construction. The native-strings workstream is **complete** (NS-1 through NS-6 all done). Further `StrView` adoption in `src/` is possible (the remaining 8 `intern-string` ptr callers, format helpers, sanitize seams) but is now ordinary per-site optimization work, not a tracked phase. | [stage14/native-strings.md](stage14/native-strings.md) |
| 14 (target-typed-ctors) | Target-typed generic constructors — a one-shot downward expected-type ("want") channel from declared-type positions (let/with init, `set!` RHS, `return`, `.set!` value) into generic resolution, filling tyvars the arguments left unbound; lifts the return-only-tyvar registration gate so zero-arg constructors become value-returning generics | **TC-1 + TC-2 done (2026-07-08); the def-time gate "type variable '%s' is not determined by any parameter" is retired (return-only tyvars register as METHOD-GENERIC; `determined` retained as advisory, re-derived at resolution via `method-undetermined-tyvar` for the dedicated diagnostic). `generic-resolve`/`generic-resolve-adapt-tier`/`generic-method-bind`/`generic-method-bind-adapt`/`node-type-call` (src/generics.nuc) carry a `want:?ptr:Type`; when tyvars stay unbound after args+assoc, `unify-tpat` fills them from want over the return pattern (fill-only — sets a still-null slot, never overrides an arg binding; inert when want is null or the bind is already complete, so every existing generic is untouched). New `g-want-type` (src/nucleusc.nuc:194, beside `g-fn-ret-type`) is armed at `emit-let`/`emit-with` binding inits, `emit-set` RHS, explicit + implicit `emit-return`, and `emit-field-set` value position (save/set/restore), and consumed once at `emit-generic-call` entry (read into a local + nulled before emitting arguments, so an argument sub-call never inherits the outer want). `generic-resolve` now reports `cannot infer type variable '%s' for '%s': no expected type at this position — annotate the binding` (via `method-undetermined-tyvar`) instead of the misleading `no matching method`. node-type mirror: `node-type-call` takes want; checker/dispatcher callers (gcheck, valid-walk, the `node-type` dispatcher) pass `null` → a want-dependent call types as null (the documented escape hatch). **Gate**: `make` clean; `make bootstrap` stage1==stage2 (byte-identical, additive by construction — no return-only generics exist in-tree, want is only consulted when tyvars stay unbound); scratch test proves want fills T=i32 (a `(Box i32)` binding stamps `%Box.i32`) and the no-want case yields the new diagnostic. `make test` green except one **pre-existing, unrelated** failure (`examples/string-test.nuc:91`, Stage-10 escape analysis × the `with`-refactor commit `0d1ac6a`; reproduces with committed pre-TC `bin/nucleusc`). **TC-3 done (2026-07-08)** — binding materialization (value→ref at declared-ref bindings). New coercion rule at `let`/`with` binding position only: when the declared type is `(ref S)` for a struct `S` and the init value is a by-value `S`, the compiler materializes — allocas an `S` backing slot, stores the value, and binds the name as the ref to that slot — instead of today's `let: init type mismatch` death. Implementation: `tc3-emit-binding-init` (src/nucleusc.nuc:5536) + `tc3-materialize` (:5508) factor the want-derivation + emit-once + materialize logic shared by `emit-let` (:5632) and `emit-with` (:5709). The want passed to the init is derived from the declared type via two non-emitting `node-type` probes (deterministic, two probes max): probe 1 arms the whole `(ref S)` (what heap constructors return — TC-4); probe 2, only if probe 1 fails to resolve, arms the pointee `S` and arms materialization iff the resolved return is by-value `S`. To make the probe drive generic resolution, `node-type-call`'s tier-2 (src/generics.nuc:3685) now falls back to the armed `g-want-type` when no explicit want is passed — safe under the consume-once discipline (the channel is nulled before any stamping/cross-check, so self-compilation, which has no return-only generics, is unaffected). Drop/move/escape need **no new rules**: the materialized backing is frame storage, and `with` ownership drops it through the existing `with-drop-method` TY-PTR arm. **§1.2 residual verified**: a stamped parametric `(Box i32)` with a printing `drop`, bound via `with`, prints `body 42 / dropped box 42 / end` (the METHOD-GENERIC drop instantiation at with-drop-method works). **Test**: a concrete `make-res` by-value constructor bound `(let ((v (ref Res)) (make-res)) (v n))` returns 42 (was: `let: init type mismatch`); emitted IR shows the `%tc3.mat.N = alloca %Res` + `store %Res` + `store ptr %tc3.mat.N` shape; with a Drop method + `with`, the drop fires at scope end. **Gate**: `make` clean; `make test` 166/166; `make bootstrap` stage1==stage2 (byte-identical, additive — materialization fires only where today's emit dies, so no compiler-internal binding shifts). **TC-4/TC-5 remain.** Boundary note for TC-4: a generic function returning a struct-template type is still rejected at def-time — `(defn make-box ((v T)):Box …)` dies `unknown type: Box` (union-registry.nuc:291), and `(defn vec-empty () (Vector T) …)` dies `unable to parse type expression`; the struct-template-as-type-in-generic-context resolution is TC-4's prerequisite. TC-3's materialization rule is independent and fully exercised via concrete constructors regardless. **TC-4a done (2026-07-08)** — the gcheck A2 false-positive for struct-template type operands is fixed. `gcheck` (src/generics.nuc:~1934) now recognizes a cell whose head names a registered struct template (via `node-template-of`) and defers (returns null) instead of walking it as an unknown-function call, so `(alloca (Box T))` / `(cast (Box T) x)` / `(sizeof (Vector T))` type operands in a generic body no longer die `in generic body: unknown function 'Box'`. Chosen integration: struct-template-head recognition only (not form-specific alloca/cast/sizeof skipping) — it is the most direct fix for the root cause (struct-template cells mis-walked as calls) and handles every type-operand position uniformly; no new helper function (inlined one `when`). Inert for existing code: after full SSA-temp + `@.str.N` normalization the only self-IR change is inside `gcheck` (gcheck is itself compiled into the compiler, so the literal `nucleusc.ll` before/after diff is non-empty — the real byte-identical gate for a self-hosted-compiler edit is `make bootstrap` stage1==stage2 + per-program IR identity, both green); `make test` 166/166. Verified: local `(defstruct (Box T) (val i32))` + `box-empty` body `(let (s (alloca (Box T))) (.set! s val 0) (return (deref s)))` bound `(let (b:(Box i32) (box-empty)) …)` prints `0`; real `Vector` via `(defn vec-ensure-fresh ((r (ref (Vector T)))) (let (tmp (alloca (Vector T))) (vector-init tmp) (conj tmp 7)))` + `conj` prints `len=1`. **TC-4b still needed:** (1) `gbind-decl-type` dies on a *declared* struct-template binding type mentioning a tyvar (`(let ((s (ref (Box T))) …) …)` → `unknown type: T`) — the design's constructors use untyped bindings inferred from `alloca`, so this doesn't block the `vector-new` family but should be deferred for robustness; (2) zero-arg return-only-tyvar constructor calls need TC-4b's `*-new` library + want positions. **TC-4b done (2026-07-09)** — added the `*-new`/`*-new-alloc`/`*-new-capacity`/`*-new-in` constructor families to lib/vector.nuc, lib/hashmap.nuc, lib/hashset.nuc (T / K,V bind from the declared type via want). Deleted `make-vec`/`mkvec`/`mkhash` from src/nucleusc.nuc and rewrote all ~43 compiler registry-construction sites to `(vector-new-in (addr-of g-arena-alloc))` / `(hashmap-new-in …)` — the `cast`-over-`make-vec` type hole (stage999-future.md) is retired (element types now honest via want). Reader empty-literal hints retargeted; `examples/constructors.nuc` + negative fixture `tests/fixtures/tc-cannot-infer-tyvar.nuc` added. A second gcheck fix was required: `ref`/`raw`/`ptr` wrapper-keyword heads (`(ref (Vector T))`) are now type spellings too (src/generics.nuc:~1948) — needed for the `-in` heap bodies; it required a 2-stage manual bootstrap (boot had to gain recognition before compiling those bodies). Also fixed a latent TC-1 bug surfaced by zero-arg constructors: `generic-method-bind-adapt` (~:1500) early-exited `(when (= any-remaining 0) (return 0))` before want-fill, so a zero-arg return-only-tyvar generic never bound — the early-exit now fires only when the binding is complete. **TC-5 part 1 done (2026-07-09)** — `union-target-rewrite` (src/union-emit.nuc:867) parameterized by target type; run at let/with binding-init and `set!` RHS want positions (not just return), so union construction works without explicit `make` there. **TC-5 part 2 done (2026-07-09)** — the rewrite distributes into the value-position tails of `if`/`cond`/`do`/`let`/`with`/`match`: each emitter captures the armed want at entry and re-arms it before emitting a value tail (a prior test/scrutinee/statement may have consumed it via emit-generic-call's consume-once), so `(let (m:(Maybe i32) (if c (some 5) none)))` constructs both arms. Byte-identical/additive (those positions previously died). **The full TC-1..TC-5 design is now implemented.** **`.set!` value-position gap closed (2026-07-15)**: `emit-field-set` was relocated to just after `(import-use union-emit)` in src/nucleusc.nuc (it was defined before the import, so `union-target-rewrite` wasn't in scope at its definition point — nucleusc.nuc's own whole-unit prescan makes same-file forward calls order-independent, but a not-yet-imported sibling file's symbols aren't registered until the import is textually reached); its value expression now runs through `union-target-rewrite` against the field's type, so `(.set! h m (some 5))` on a `(Maybe i32)` field constructs correctly with no explicit annotation, matching every other want position. `make test` 174/174, `make bootstrap` holds. **Gate (all)**: `make` clean; `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` stage1==stage2; boot artifacts (incl. Windows IRs) converged. || [stage14/target-typed-constructors.md](stage14/target-typed-constructors.md) |
| 14 (defn-signature) | `defn` return type after the parameter list — dual-accept `(defn name (params):ret body…)` (matching `fn`/`vfn`/`mfn`/`cfn`) alongside legacy `(defn name:ret (params) …)`, funneling every read site through one accessor as the prerequisite cleanup for the 14.3 param/return-typing work | **S1 done (2026-07-04); S2 done (2026-07-04); S3 done (2026-07-05); S4 done (2026-07-05)** — `defn-parse-sig` + `sig-name-is-bare`/`legacy-ret-node`/`normalize-ret-node`/`defn-ret-node`/`proto-sig-parse` (nucleusc.nuc) are the single style-detection/return-extraction chokepoint; a legacy form routes to the exact `extract-name-and-type`/`binding-type-node` parse (byte-identical), a new-style form reads the index-3 ret operand with the `fn` grammar (`fn-parse-ret-type`, reused as-is). Wired through `emit-defn`, `prescan-defn-signatures`, the compile-time block prescan, `desugar-form` (defn ret rides untouched in the body tail; declare rebuilt to preserve index-3+), generic template registration + tyvar counting (`defn-ret-node`), template stamping (`generic-instantiate` + a new `NODE-KEYWORD` branch in `subst-tyvars-node` so a new-style `:T` return substitutes at stamp time), protocol-sig readers (`proto-sig-parse`, `sig-provides-call`, `proto-sigs-resolve`, and `emit-dyn-forward` — the one read site the in-progress diff missed, which returned `void` for a new-style `(dyn P)` method), `extend`/conformance comparisons, cheader (`cheader-defn-ret-node`), `.nuch` export (`print-defn-name-legacy` normalizes solitary/overloaded to legacy `name:ret`; templates export verbatim new-style) + import (`emit-nuch-declare-import` new-style branch; `register-generic-defn` re-registers a verbatim new-style template), the toplevel `declare`, and REPL redefinition (needs no change — extracts the bare `fname`, delegates the sig parse to the patched `emit-defn`/prescan). The `emit-defn` early `<4` guard now emits `defn-parse-sig`'s targeted "expected return type after the parameter list" for a bare-name missing-ret (error path only; legacy bodyless names keep "bad form"). Change is additive — no source uses the new style yet — so **`make bootstrap` is byte-identical, no `update-bootstrap`**; a new-style program emits IR byte-identical to its legacy twin (verified by diff). Tests (+8, 162/162): `examples/defn-newstyle.nuc`, `tests/repl/s1-newstyle-defn.in`, and a `run-tests.sh` S1 block (`s1-sugar-rets`/`s1-missing-ret`/`s1-newlib` fixtures) covering keyword/`:void`/colon-chain/list-form/`?`/`!`-sugar/tyvar returns, `noreturn` in the new slot, the missing-ret diagnostic, and the cross-unit `.nuch`/cheader round-trip (plain + overloaded link-and-run, template stamps). Pre-existing out-of-scope gap, closed 2026-07-15: `--emit-cheader` did not skip generic templates (garbage C in **both** styles). `emit-cheader-declare` (cheader.nuc) now calls the existing `defn-is-generic-template` (src/generics.nuc:932 — already the prescan/emit-defn skip's shared predicate; no new logic needed, just reuse it) and skips with a `/* NAME: generic template; not exported */` comment. Verified against both `&where`-bounded and receiver-tyvar-inferred templates; `make test` 174/174, `make bootstrap` holds.
| 15 (stress test) | Stress testing — deficiencies found porting Doom to Nucleus ([stage15-stress-test/overview.md](stage15-stress-test/overview.md)) | **W4a done** — no compiler diagnostic reports `:0:` any more: the whole name-resolution family (`unknown:`/`undefined:`, `let`/`with` initializers, retired special-form spellings, `defvar` initializers, `match` arm patterns, `addr-of`/`set!`/`goto`/`export` subjects) now names the line of the *reference*, plus a did-you-mean over the registered-name table and a `no unary 'bit-not'; write (bit-xor x -1)` rewrite hint; guarded by a suite-wide `:0:` sweep over every fixture. **W4b done** — `defconst`'s type-annotation mistake (`(defconst K:i32 2)`, silently registered under the wrong key) is now rejected at its own line; the same silent-registration bug, found by sweeping every sibling top-level definer, is fixed the same way in `defenum`/`defstruct`/`defprotocol`/`defmacro`/`defunion`/`deferror` (worst case: `defstruct` crashed into a confusing `:0:` diagnostic, not silence). **W4c done** — an unterminated form now reports two locations: the innermost unclosed form's opening line (which it already did — the doc's premise that it blamed the *outermost* was wrong) plus a `note:` naming the first line that opens a new form in column 0 while a form is still open, the closer it is waiting for, and how many forms are open there; depth tracking spans `(`/`[`/`{`/`#{`, an excess `)` gets the opening line of the form it would have closed, and the extra-`)`-in-a-`let`-binding-list shape (which leaves an **even** binding list, so the spec's proposed odd-count check could not have caught it) is diagnosed in `emit-let`/`emit-with` instead. **W4d done** — `case`'s documented-but-wrong nested-clause form now names the real flat syntax (fixed at `emit-invoke-with-callee`, not inside the macro body — a `defmacro` body cannot call `die-at`, confirmed empirically); one-armed `if` says `if requires an else branch; use (when test then…) for a guard`; the generic macro-arity messages now name the macro and both counts instead of a bare `macro: wrong number of args`/`macro: not enough args`. No `lib/macros.nuc` changes, so `make bootstrap` was byte-identical on the first pass. **W4e done** — two parts: `docs/stdlib.md`'s hand-curated availability table (wrong in both directions — `close`/`dup`/`dup2`/`isspace`/`isdigit` claimed but unavailable, ~190 real names undocumented) replaced by `scripts/gen-stdlib-table.py`, which probes the full transitive header chain the prelude actually pulls in (`stdio.h`+`stdlib.h`+`string.h`, not just `string.h`) against `build/nucleusc --emit-llvm` and is wired into the suite (`run_stdlib_table`, fails only on a false claim, not on host-legitimate incompleteness); `docs/special-forms.md`'s `case` row (a nonexistent `switch`/`unreachable` lowering) deleted — `case` is a macro (`lib/macros.nuc:91`) and belongs solely in `docs/macros.md`'s Standard Macros table, which already had it right; `addr-of`'s row restated positively (passing a frame-local address as a call argument is an allowed borrow — verified by compiling and running the C out-parameter idiom before documenting it) instead of reading as more restrictive than reality; `docs/toplevel.md`/`docs/errors.md` gained the "takes no type annotation" note on `defstruct`/`defunion`/`defprotocol`/`defmacro`/`deferror` (had it only on `defconst`/`defenum` before, despite W4b fixing all seven identically). `make test` 231/231 throughout W4; `make bootstrap` byte-identical at every chunk. **All of W4 (a–e) is now done. W2a done** — a binary operator's statically inferred type now equals the type it emits: `node-type-call`'s intrinsic-binop branch returned *operand 1's type alone* while emit unified both operands, so `(malloc (* 4096 (as i64 (sizeof i32))))` emitted `i64` but typed `i32` (the argument coercer then stacked a bogus `sext i32 <i64 value>`) and `(> t:ui32 (* 2 cl:ui32))` was rejected as mixed-sign while `(* cl 2)` compiled. The rule now lives in exactly one function — `binop-result-type` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`, above `binop-coerce`) — called by `binop-coerce` for the type decision and by `node-type-call` for the propagated type, so the two cannot drift again; a binop's result is the unified operand type and is symmetric in operand order. Also closed the float direction found alongside the specced repros: an untyped float literal now adapts to an `f32` operand (`(* alpha:f32 2.0)` is `f32`, not `double` mis-stored into a `float` slot), while still never adapting to an *integer* operand; two typed operands of different signedness remain rejected. 235/235 tests, bootstrap byte-identical on the first pass. **W2b done** — a `defconst`/`defenum` name now behaves like the integer literal it stands for: it adapts to the other binop operand in either order (`(<= ans:ui32 K)` compiles iff `(<= ans:ui32 512)` does), carried as `Sym.const-lit`/`const-lit-i64` — the VALUE, not just a flag — read by the one shared `binop-result-type` via a new `operand-is-int-literal`, with the scope threaded rather than shortcut to `g-globals` so a shadowing local stays an ordinary typed value; `deferror` constants are the deliberate exception (`Err` is `is-int-type`-true and must not unify with integers). Also fixed the silent wrong answer in the same three lines: `emit-defconst` hardcoded `ty-i32` while `const-val` carried the full decimal string, so `(defconst BIG 5000000000)` printed `705032704`; constants now type at `emit-int`'s truthful width via a shared `int-literal-type` (three former copies of that `if` collapsed into one), which also arms the existing `coerce-int-val` range check — and a second, pre-existing narrowing hole found alongside it (`defvar-init-ir` hands a decimal string straight to LLVM, which truncates `i32 5000000000` without complaint) is closed for the named and inline spellings alike. The trap worth carrying forward: the variadic `+`/`*` macros wrap the tail, so `(* v K)` expands to `(_* v (* K))` and a binop's second operand node is a CELL — a predicate over binop operands must macroexpand it or the fix silently works in operand-1 position only. **W2c done** (doc-only, folded into W2b's doc pass). 240/240 tests, bootstrap byte-identical on the first pass. **W2d done** — the §3.6 finding turned out to be "the float coercion path is missing", not merely "float literals do not adapt": `coerce-int-val` (the general implicit-coercion chokepoint, despite its name) had no float case at all, so eight typed-slot positions *rejected* an `f32` target while the one caller that treats a null coercion as "leave the argument alone" (`emit-call-with-args`) silently **miscompiled** — `(take 2.5)` against `(defn take (x:f32):f32 …)` emitted `call float @take(double 2.5)` and printed `0.000000`. A third bug surfaced in the position previously recorded as working: LLVM accepts a decimal constant for `float` only when it round-trips exactly, so `(defvar g:f32 3.14)` emitted an IR-invalid `global float 3.14` (and a global initializer, being a constant, cannot be fixed with an `fptrunc`). All closed at the one chokepoint, with `coerce-num-val`/`safe-coerce-val` delegating to it instead of carrying copies; a float literal now adapts at every typed target with **no** instruction (re-rendered as a single-precision constant), and a typed `f64` value narrows silently via `fptrunc` — **Option A**, matching what the integer branch five lines up has always done and what C does. Multimethod dispatch admits the literal too (strictly additive; tier 0 still claims an exact `f64` overload) but never a typed value. `-ffast-math` was removed from the compiler's own link line — it set FTZ/DAZ process-wide and folded every denormal literal to zero. Accept criterion met: a float DSP kernel written with bare literals is bit-exact with the equivalent C program at `-O0` and `-O2`. 245/245 tests, bootstrap byte-identical on the first pass. **All of W2 (a–d) is now done. W3a done** — §1.6: a C header's `struct Foo;` / `typedef struct Foo Foo;` was skipped entirely, so the type never registered and a later `ptr:Foo` died `unknown type: Foo` — C's standard opaque-handle idiom, which made every SDL handle type and `FILE` unusable. Such a type is now registered layout-less (`StructDef.opaque`, cleared in `struct-set-fields`, the single chokepoint every field-populating path funnels through), legal behind a pointer everywhere including a `defn` signature, refused by value at six sites with the misuse's own line **and** the header:line it was declared on (recovered from clang -E's linemarkers), and upgraded **in place** by a later definition in all three orderings real headers use. Two premises in the spec were wrong: the fix is not “one branch” (`c-parse-type` had to keep returning `ptr` for an opaque tag, or a by-value `struct Tag` would have emitted `declare void @f(%Tag)` for an undefined type — more of the §1.5 invalid-IR failure the stage exists to remove), and the spec's own §1.6 probe cannot pass from import-time registration at all, because `prescan-defn-signatures` resolves signatures *before* any import runs and `prescan-imported-types` skips C-string imports by design — so a name-only `cheader-prescan-opaque` pass registers exactly the names the real import will define. `unknown type:` also gained a location (the prescan blamed the defn's interned NAME node, line always 0; `desugar-typed` stamped every desugared binding cell the same way — both now borrow the enclosing form's line, which fixes the whole defn-parameter / `defvar`/`extern`/`declare`-name / `defstruct`-field / `let`-binding-name family). Incidental: `MAX-STRUCTS` 256→1024 (three mainstream umbrella headers in one unit need 200–256 slots), and preprocessed header text is cached by path (5 clang runs → 3 per build, ~30% faster than before W3a), which surfaced a use-after-free — `emit-c-include` ended with `(free buf)`, correct per-call but not once the buffer is shared; it presented as a *hang*, only on the second import of the same header. 255/255 tests; bootstrap byte-identical first pass, and because W3 touches shared code, `make lib-cheaders`/`make lib-headers` plus the emitted IR of every `lib/*.nuc` and `examples/*.nuc` were additionally diffed byte-for-byte against a compiler built from the pre-change tree. **W3b done** — §1.5 was **not** a `void` problem: `c-parse-type` accepted C type qualifiers only in *leading* position, so an "east" qualifier (`int const *p` — legal C for the same type) terminated the type, its token was eaten as the parameter's **name**, and the following `*p` began a phantom second parameter defaulting to `ptr`. `void _mm_clflush(void const *__p)`, reached transitively from `SDL2/SDL.h`, became the IR-invalid `declare void @_mm_clflush(void, ptr)` — the only spelling that failed loudly; `int const *p` became `(i32, ptr)` and `double const *p` became `(double, ptr)`, representable and silently accepted with the wrong arity and ABI, which **no validity gate can catch** (so the parse fix is the deliverable and the gate only the net). Qualifiers (`const`/`volatile`/`restrict`/`__restrict`/`__restrict__`/`_Atomic`) are now consumed and discarded in every position C permits — throughout the declaration-specifier sequence and after each `*`. The gate (`c-decl-skip-reason`) then runs on every recognized function declaration before registration and **skips with `<header>:<line>: warning: skipping C declaration '<name>': <reason>`** on: a by-value struct/union with no known layout (a declaration-scoped flag set inside `c-parse-type`, cleared again for function-pointer and array parameters — glibc's `utimensat(…, const struct timespec [2], …)` proved that necessary), a recorded `void` parameter, an opaque parameter/return type, and an arity over 32 (counted past the array's capacity so a truncated signature is never registered — it was 16 and was being written past). Warnings are always on and deduplicated by name; measured volume across stdio/stdlib/string/unistd/fcntl/math/time/sys-stat/pthread/netinet-in/signal/png/SDL.h/SDL_mixer.h is **zero**. A third root cause was found by measuring rather than trusting the committed behaviour: a stray `)` (present since `6ef16dc Add union types`) closed the top-level dispatch `cond` after its first clause, leaving `true` as a no-op statement and running the function-declaration parse *unconditionally* after every `struct`/`union`/`typedef` — which mis-attributed the following declaration's warning to the type declaration's line and silently dropped a second consecutive forward declaration. Two spec premises corrected: the suggested "no unresolved type names" gate arm is not implementable (an unfollowed typedef resolves to `ptr`, which *is* representable — that is §1.4's defect to fix at the source), and "compiler-internal headers must still pass the gate" is **false** — 3 of 33 `make lib-cheaders` outputs produce 11 skips, every one a real `--emit-cheader` defect (it emits a parametric template's type variable as `struct T`). Rung 2 of the header ladder reached: `SDL2/SDL.h` imports with zero warnings and 1663 declarations, and the spec's two-line probe compiles, **links and runs**. 258/258 tests; bootstrap byte-identical first pass, with `make lib-cheaders` and every `lib/`+`examples/` IR (and the compiler's own) diffed byte-for-byte against a pre-change compiler. **W3c done** — §1.4 was far broader than the spec's framing: **no scalar typedef resolved at all**, so `lseek`'s `off_t` return *and* parameter, `getline`/`ftello`, SDL's `Uint8`/`Uint32`, and whole struct **layouts** (`%timeval`, `%__fpos_t`, `%Mix_Chunk`) were silently wrong. Typedefs now resolve transitively through a flat table filled where each `typedef` is parsed (one lookup per use; cycles impossible by construction), with `enum` added as a declaration specifier and glibc's `__extension__ typedef` consumed; an unfollowable typedef is never a silent `ptr`, which makes W3b's deferred "unresolved type names" gate arm implementable at last. An explicit `(declare …)` now beats a header-derived one in **both** orders (measured, it was silent *first*-wins, not the spec's "last wins"), warning with both rendered signatures; the explicit declaration is emitted at the point of first need, because suppression alone leaves the name undefined between the import and the declare. Two pre-existing defects surfaced only once typedefs resolved: the C-header `declare` emitter never applied the aggregate C ABI, and the cheader path never set `StructDef.emitted` (so a C union with a struct member was never defined while the struct containing it referenced it). Warning policy split in two — W3b's measured volume of zero became 165 the moment typedefs resolved — so parse failures stay loud at import while "Nucleus has no such type" is reported at the point of *use*. Also took W3b's deferred musl `struct Tag *f(int);` fallback. Ladder closed: rung 1 runnable end-to-end, rungs 2 and 3 reached. 261/261 tests; bootstrap byte-identical after a boot reconverge; `lib` header generation byte-identical. **W3c fallout, fixed** — the precedence rule surfaced that `declare`'s parameter list **ignored every written type unless the parameter was named**, silently emitting `i32` (`(declare f (ptr i64 ui32):i64)` → `(i32, i32, i32)`), correct exactly when the signature was all-`i32` — which is why it survived, including in the compiler's own `(declare repl_print_f64 (ptr):void)`, declaring an `i32` against a `void *` shim. Worse under W3c: a bare-list declaration that *agreed* with a header now "conflicted" with it, warned, and the corrupted signature won. An unannotated element is now parsed as a **type operand**, so an unresolvable spelling is a located error rather than a default; an *annotated* element stays a named parameter by decision (documented and pinned, since desugar erases the distinction and reinterpreting it would silently retype generated `.nuch` headers); `&rest`/`&optional` are refused in a declaration, which caught three `tests/run-tests.sh` heredocs relying on the defect — heredoc-generated sources are invisible to a tree-wide grep. 265/265 tests; the codegen diff is *exactly* the two `repl_print_f*` declarations, with all 189 compiling programs byte-identical. **All of W3 is now done. W5a/W5b/W5c/W5d done** — `\xHH` string escapes; unary `bit-not`; a `CStr`-typed `defvar` (which exposed and fixed a `strcmp(ptr, null)` segfault on any `(= cstr null)` guard); and the array-literal relaxations: a bare `(S …)` compound literal may now be an `(array S …)` element (fixed once at the `coerce-int-val` chokepoint, where it also reaches struct-typed locals, struct-literal fields, `aset!` stores and by-value returns — the *argument* position had had this rule since Stage 13 CE-3, which is why `(take (P 1 2))` compiled while `(let (v:P (P 1 2)) …)` did not), and an `(array T …)` initializer refines an elem-less `:ptr` binding to `ptr:T`; the two spellings emit byte-identical IR and a 1000-row table's compile time is unchanged. Two pre-existing `emit-zero-store` crashes on that path fixed with it (`store %P 0` / `store ptr 0` for struct and `CStr` slots — both LLVM parse errors, the latter the documented TY-PTR-vs-`is-ptr-like` trap). **W1a + W1b done** — cross-file function references now resolve on **reachability**, not import order; the ordinal rule *"X may reference a function in Y ⇔ Y begins processing before X is emitted"* is retired. A second whole-graph prescan pass (`prescan-imported-signatures`, `src/nucleusc.nuc`) walks the same depth-first import graph `prescan-imported-types` already walks and registers every reachable `.nuc` file's protocols and defn signatures before any form is emitted; **two** passes rather than one because a signature's types must resolve against the whole graph's type names (a one-pass walk would prescan a file before a sibling its parent imports later — the compiler's own `src/generics.nuc`/`src/compiler-types.nuc` pair hits that immediately). The design's crux question resolved as its possibility (3): registration is **not** idempotent at all (`generic-register-method` appends unconditionally, `generic-add-method` clears `finalized`), so a per-path guard `g-prescan-sigs` skips the file's own re-prescan while `finalize-generics` still runs at exactly the moment it used to — only *registration* moved earlier, and the duplicate-signature check is untouched (two files defining the same name+arity still error). W1b is part of it: the walk applies each visited file's own leading `(ns …)`/`set-ir-prefix`, since `scope-define` qualifies a global's key against `g-current-ns` and `generic-new` snapshots the ir-prefix `finalize-generics` bakes into a solitary method's ir-name. W1e turned out to need **no mechanism**: `emit-nuch-declare-import` already returns early when the name is in `g-globals`, so a `declare` matching a reachable `defn` is a no-op — and W1a makes that fire more often, not less. Exposed and fixed a **pre-existing** defect reproducible on the committed boot compiler: `defunion-register` wrote a union backing struct's `%X = type { i32, %anon }` line eagerly while its anon payload union sat on the deferred queue, so any module assembled before the payload's own struct type arrived (`!String` waiting for `%String`) carried a dangling reference and died `use of undefined type named '__anon_union_…'`; the backing struct is now queued exactly when writing it would dangle. Also fixed a second pre-existing defect found by a whole-tree pre/post sweep: a function emitted **before** a later import overloaded its name kept the solitary `@name` symbol while every later call site emitted the mangled `@name.<tok>` — an undefined symbol (`lib/list.nuc`'s `append` + `lib/vector.nuc`'s `append` template links-errors on the boot compiler); the decision is now final before the first `define` is written. Sweep: 104 programs byte-identical, 55 differing only in type-definition order, 2 real differences (both that symbol fix), **0 regressions**. 293/293 tests (+14 units, every positive one compiled, **linked and run** for its exit status); `make abi-test`/`make layout-test` green. `make bootstrap` was **not** byte-identical on the first pass — expected for this item, and proven inert before reconverging: all 44 changed lines are `%Name = type {…}` definitions moving within the type section (sorting the type lines makes both files byte-identical; zero `declare`/`define`/string-table/body lines differ), LLVM named types are order-independent in a module, and the new compiler is a fixed point on its own output. **W1c done** — the diagnostic surface W1a left behind now distinguishes its three remaining failures: a name whose C-header declaration was skipped (W3c's tier, unchanged and still first), a name that is **defined in a file no import reaches** — §2.7's reachability constraint, which W1 deliberately keeps — and a plain typo. The middle tier is new and is the point: on failure the compiler scans the `.nuc`/`.nuch` files in exactly the directories `resolve-import` searches (current source file's directory, `lib/`, each `-I`), skips every file already in the unit, and names the first one outside it that defines the name — `note: 'y-later' is defined in ./yf.nuc, which no import in this unit reaches`, which turns an `unknown:` into a one-line import. It suppresses the did-you-mean rather than stacking with it (naming the file beats guessing a spelling; they collide almost never, since the scan matches the name exactly), and the same note is attached to `unknown type:` so the type half of §2.7 gets it too. With nothing found anywhere the message now says so — *"not defined anywhere in this compilation unit"* — instead of a bare `unknown: <name>`, which after W1a reads as "not imported yet". Deliberate choices: a **textual** scan of file bytes (re-entering `read-program`/`desugar` from a diagnostic would clobber the reader globals mid-message), comment- and string-literal-aware so the two obvious false positives are out; **error path only** — every caller is a `die-at`, which is `noreturn`, so it runs at most once per compile and a failing compile is no slower than a clean one. Directory enumeration is hand-declared POSIX `opendir`/`readdir`/`closedir` (the C-header reader registers `struct dirent` **opaque** — its `char d_name[256]` defeats the declaration parser — and the compiler already requires POSIX `popen` for `clang -E`), with `d_name`'s byte offset **validated rather than trusted**, so a platform with a different layout finds nothing instead of printing garbage. One structural gap found and closed on the way: the unit's **root file is on none of the import lists** (nothing imports it), so it needed `g-unit-entry-path` to keep the scan from advising "no import reaches your own entry point". No existing test expectation needed touching — both new tiers only append to the `verb: name` prefix. 297/297 tests (+4 units, including a negative control that compiles, **links and runs** the program once the named import is added — the note has to be advice that works); `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `abi-test`/`layout-test` green. **W6 §3.4 done** — a `null` **global** initializer into a typed non-null pointer is now rejected exactly as the identical local is. `defvar-init-ir` is a *constant renderer* that never routes through `coerce-int-val`, so it never ran `pkind-flow-check`: `(defvar g:ptr:Thing null)` / `(defvar g:(ref Thing) null)` compiled clean and segfaulted on first use while `(let (p:ptr:Thing null) …)` was correctly rejected — the same "the global-initializer path bypasses the coercion chokepoint" class W2b found for integer truncation. The fix calls the **same predicate** the local path calls (source type `ty-raw`, exactly what `emit-symbol-ref` gives the `null` symbol; ctx `"defvar"`, so the wording matches word for word) rather than re-deriving its rules, which is what makes the three carve-outs automatic: `(raw T)` / `?T` are not `PTR-REF` and return at once, and an elem-less bare `ptr` — the `void*` escape hatch, ~1550 bindings in the compiler's own source — is exempt by `pkind-flow-check`'s untyped-destination refinement. `CStr` keeps its own explicit early return above the check (a null `char*` is ordinary C), pinned by W5c's tripwire. The design's blast-radius measurement was one site short — it scanned `src/`+`lib/` only, and `examples/colon-paren-types.nuc` terminated a hand-rolled linked list with `(defvar empty:(ptr Link) null)`; its three nullable slots became `(raw Link)`, with the example's output and its actual subject (colon-paren type sugar) unchanged. `emit-defvar`'s **no-init** sibling (`(defvar g:ptr:Thing)` emits `global ptr null` for any `is-ptr-like` type) is the same unsoundness in a different spelling and was measured, then deliberately left open: **53** such globals exist in the compiler's own source (20 `ty-*:ref:Type` singletons, `g-globals:ref:Scope`, 32 `(ref (Vector …))`/`(ref (HashSet …))`/`(ref (HashMap …))` registry tables), every one filled by `types-init`/`compiler-init` after definition, so closing it is a deferred-initialization *language design* question for W6 proper, not a check. 303/303 tests (+3 units: two located rejections, one accept matrix over bare `ptr`/`(raw T)`/`raw:T`/`?ptr:T`/`CStr`); `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `abi-test`/`layout-test` green. **W1d done (2026-07-31)** — mutual-import policy, decided twice the same day: first Option 1 (keep `circular import` a hard error, now-correct advice since W1a made "a common parent imports both, neither imports the other" the recommended order-independent spelling), then **superseded by the user's choice of Option 2** — import cycles are legal. Both `g-importing` sites in `do-import` now call `note-import-cycle` and return instead of `die-at`; the skipped path is deliberately kept off `g-imported` (that list means "finished", and its slice is what a later prefixed import replays), so emission is idempotent per path — each file emits once, at first reach. Four things a cycle cannot carry, because they only exist once a file is *emitted* and a cycle member's body emits before the rest of its back-import: a partner's `defmacro` (registers at emission time, and there is no macro prescan — JIT-compiling one early is its own stage), a partner's `defconst`/`defenum` **member** (name registration, not layout — `(sizeof S)`/`(alloca S)` across a cycle are actually fine, since both lower to a GEP/alloca over the LLVM *named* type LLVM itself resolves later in the module), a partner's struct/union **layout** (field access, struct literal, by-value field/param/return/arg — plus a fourth, unspecced coupling found by measurement: a by-value struct at an ABI boundary sized itself 0 and silently miscompiled the call site before this was caught), and a `prefix/name` alias over a cycle member (the bare `(import foo)` spelling *is* the prefixed one). Each is now a located diagnostic naming the cycle, gated on `g-import-cycles != null` so a unit with no cycle takes the identical pre-W1d path. Also fixed alongside it: two `.nuc` string-path imports in one file no longer collide on a hardcoded `"c"` prefix (now defaults from the path's basename, like the symbol spelling), and a `src/format.nuc` stack over-read (`snprintf`'s *would-have-been* length handed to `arena-strndup`, which trusted it) found while lengthening these diagnostics. 303 → 319 tests; `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass (any program that reaches the old guard was already a hard error, so no *compiling* program's emission order moves) — measured further with a 168-file `--emit-llvm` sweep, 0 differing. **W5e done (2026-08-01)** — `defn-` name isolation, decided by census: `src/` uses **zero** private definers of any kind, so no private-definer change can move the bootstrap, which removed the reason to hedge toward the cheaper "qualify only on collision" variant (rejected outright — it also has a real hazard, since `finalize-generics` stamps an ir-name and freezes it before a later colliding file can be seen). Shipped as **Option 1, unconditional**: a private definer in a file with no `(ns …)` is keyed under an implicit per-file namespace (`#p1/helper`) — an ordinary `<ns>/<name>` spelling, so `qualify-name`/`strip-ns-qualifier` need no change and private-shadows-public falls out of the key scheme for free. A second, unreported defect fell out of the same census and was fixed by the same mechanism: `defvar-` collided too, with a worse symptom (two `@g = internal global` lines in one module, an unlocated link-time IR parse error). The public-collision diagnostic now names both files' `file:line:`. 319 → 328 tests (+9); `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass; a 165-file sweep shows exactly the 5 private-definer files differing, each hunk an `internal`-symbol rename. **W7 done** (provenance: the author's own stress testing, not the Doom port) — a bare symbol in selector position always meant "field name", so `(m k)`/`(get m k)` on a `HashMap` died `no field 'k'` while `(m "foo")` worked; fixed by letting a bare local-name selector demote to a value read when the callee provably has no such field, and by giving `invoke` a `get` fallback, with marked selectors (`'field` mandatory) recorded as the correct long-term end state and deferred to their own stage. 296 → 300 tests; zero blast radius measured by A/B-diffing every example's IR. **All seven original work items (W1–W7) are done.** Gate for them: **328 PASS / 0 FAIL**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, `make abi-test`/`make layout-test` green. Stage success was verified **externally** against the ~25,000-line Doom port at `/home/zak/code/nuc-doom-claude` (2026-08-01): both demo gates (`test_demo`, `test_demo_monsters`) bit-exact with the load-bearing `g_game.nuc` import ordering and the `(as ui32 CONST)` comparison casts deleted. That run surfaced two findings for the next stage: the W6 §3.4 fix broke 13 `(defvar- g:ptr:T null)` singletons in the port (the no-init-spelling asymmetry §3.4 deliberately left open, hit immediately in real code), and `defconst`/`defenum` members are still import-order dependent (W1 covered `defn` signatures and protocols only), with a diagnostic that falsely claims the name is undefined anywhere in the unit rather than merely unprocessed yet. **Reopened 2026-08-01 with two new items; W8 is now complete — all six steps G-0 through G-5 landed (2026-08-01, 2026-08-02 ×5), plus one regression-fix interlude between G-3 and G-4; `compiler-init` is eliminated (52 statements → 0) and `(defvar g:ptr:T)` with no initializer is rejected, closing nullability.md §1.5's remaining half. G-2 added the `(array T N)` type in both storage positions (a `defvar` type and an aggregate field) plus constant array/struct aggregate initializers, all five spec shapes, 361 → 385 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical; its `g-arena-alloc` conversion was deliberately **split out** because the spec's own *Verify* clause demands it and a byte-identical bootstrap in the same breath and those are incompatible — see the G-2 as-built record in [global-init.md](global-init.md). G-3 made a `defvar` initializer the compiler cannot fold run at **startup** from one synthesized `void @__nucleus_init()` registered with `llvm.global_ctors`, in reach order, lowered to an ordinary `set!` so §2.8's `pkind-flow-check` applies unchanged (`(defvar g:ptr:T (mk))` now typechecks with `g` non-null); zero-cost-when-unused is enforced by making the drain at `emit-toplevel-forms` depth 1 the ONLY emission point and is pinned by a two-sided tripwire on host and AVR; the queue predicate is `defvar-init-ir`'s own answer via a one-shot `g-defvar-soft` flag, not a second classifier; `ctor-mechanism-for-triple` answers `global_ctors` (hosted) or `none` (AVR, a located error naming the offending `defvar`); 385 → 393 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, and an old-vs-new IR sweep against a compiler built from HEAD's source gives 210 byte-identical / 0 differing / 0 regressed. Two pre-existing defects reported not fixed: a function-pointer-typed `defvar` cannot be declared at all (a G-0 regression in `name-existing-kind`, which G-5 needs), and `aref` emits a hardcoded `i64` GEP index that is invalid IR on AVR. **The fn-pointer defect was fixed in an interlude between G-3 and G-4**: `name-existing-kind` classified any `TY-FN`-typed global `Sym` as a function; fixed with an `(= (sym is-local) 0)` conjunct, the same test `emit-dispatch` already used; tests 393 → 396. **G-4** rejects a `defvar` initializer that syntactically names a global whose own `defvar` has not yet been reached, naming both sites, via a `defvar-state` field on `Sym` (DECLARED at G-0's prescan, REACHED at emission) rather than a list of already-emitted `Sym*`s (which would go stale across a `scope-define` array growth); `(addr-of g)` is exempted as not-a-read; tests 396 → 406, bootstrap byte-identical on the first pass. **G-5 — `compiler-init` eliminated, then the flip — acceptance criteria (A) and (B) both met.** 52 statements → 0: 48 globals converted to combined declaration+initialization, 20 dead statements deleted, seven helpers removed; the sole survivor is `(target-init)`, called directly from `main`/`repl-main`, because it reads argv and derives five globals from one call. The flip rejects `(defvar g:ptr:T)` with no initializer, closing [stage15-stress-test/nullability.md](stage15-stress-test/nullability.md) §1.5's remaining half; the warn stage measured the cost first (54 sites, all in `src/`, zero in `lib/`/`examples/`/`tests/fixtures/`). Three premises measured false: §2.10's reorder goes downward not up; a second, unpredicted reorder was needed (`g-current-ns` before `g-generics`); the two late-binding hooks cannot be constants after all (1 constant + 47 runtime, not 3 + 45). A prerequisite defect (`as` not arming the want channel for a return-only-tyvar generic in `scope-new`) was found and fixed at its root. Bootstrap deliberately moved and was reconverged via a two-hop fixed-point proof (the committed `bin/nucleusc` predates G-1/G-3 and cannot compile the migrated source). Tests 406 → **410 PASS / 0 FAIL**; `make abi-test`/`make layout-test`/`make avr-test` green.** **W8 — combined declaration and initialization** (spec [global-init.md](global-init.md)): eliminate `compiler-init` or reduce it to a few genuinely special cases; a global that should not be nullable is declared and initialized in **one** operation; the startup call is **zero-cost when unused** (no `@__nucleus_init`, no `llvm.global_ctors` entry, no synthesized `main` for a program with no runtime initializer — a hard requirement, for microcontroller binary size). It closes the half of W6 §1.5 that item deliberately parked, and it assigns **both** findings the external run produced: its **G-0** is the `defconst`/`defenum` import-order one, its **G-5** the `(defvar- g:ptr:T null)` one. Measured: `compiler-init` is 51 statements running exactly once per process — 30 become combined declaration+initialization covering **48** globals (3 constant, **45** runtime), 20 are dead, and 1 (`target-init`, which reads argv) is the genuine residue; a third spelling of the same unsoundness turned up in three type-erasure registries that are declared non-null and never initialized at all. Staged G-0 → G-1/G-2 (static half) → G-3 → G-4 → G-5 — **all six done**. **G-0 done (2026-08-01)** — `prescan-value-names` extends W1a's whole-graph walk to `defvar`/`defconst`/`defenum` names; all four §2.5 probes now resolve in both import orders; a latent silent-wrong-answer bug fell out and was fixed (a private constant referenced earlier in its own file had resolved to another file's public constant of the same spelling); W1d's cycle diagnostic narrowed to what still cannot cross a cycle (macros, `deferror` ids, `extern`); `make bootstrap` reconverged after a proven-inert 48-line type-definition-order diff; 328 → 347 tests. **G-1 done (2026-08-02)** — folds arithmetic/bit ops, `(sizeof T)`, `(as T lit)` including `(as CStr "…")` (superseding W5c's out-of-scope note — the general rule W5c cited is exactly what changed), and `(addr-of g)` in a `defvar` initializer, with no JIT; overflow/division-by-zero/shift-range are located compile-time errors, never silent wraps; the inherited `int-literal-fits`/`pkind-flow-check` checks still fire on folded values, plus a new `reject-cycle-pending-layout` call `cfold-sizeof` needed that the value path does not; `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass; 347 → 361 tests. **Function-pointer-typed globals fixed (2026-08-02, G-5 prerequisite)** — `(defvar h:(fn ret)(params) …)` could not be declared at all; two stacked defects, both now closed: `name-existing-kind` classified any global `Sym` carrying a `TY-FN` type as *a function* on the strength of its type alone, so once G-0's `prescan-value-names` began defining that `Sym` during the whole-graph prescan the `defvar` collided with **itself** (`'h' already names a function`) — it now also asks `is-local`, the same two-conjunct test `emit-dispatch` already uses; and behind that (pre-existing since long before G-0, and the error the pre-G-0 compiler actually reached) `defvar-init-ir`'s `null` gate tested `is-ptr-like`, from which `TY-FN` is deliberately excluded, so the explicit `null` initializer was refused while the *implicit* zero for the same slot was already `null` — `null` is now admitted for `TY-FN` by name, exactly as `emit-zero-store`/`type-zero-const-ir` spell the same three-way rule. A fn pointer carries no non-null contract (`ptr-pkind` answers `PTR-RAW` for every non-`TY-PTR` kind), so this does **not** re-open W6's hole: the wrapper spellings `ptr:(fn …)` / `(ref (fn …))` are ordinary `PTR-REF` pointers and still reject `null` (pinned). `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass; 393 → 396 tests. **W9 — reconciled at stage close to twenty defects found, four now fixed, sixteen open** while measuring W8's design (the original six from G-0, four more surfaced by G-1 and by re-verifying its test counts, three more surfaced building G-2, two more surfaced building G-3, two more surfaced building G-5 (one of which, `as` not arming the want channel, is now fixed), three more found alongside the fn-pointer interlude and never filed until this reconciliation; [global-init.md](global-init.md) §7 and [stage15-stress-test/progress.md](stage15-stress-test/progress.md)'s W9 table, reported except where fixed): `make lib-objs`/`make lib-so` broken on the committed boot; two separately compiled Nucleus objects unlinkable (7 duplicate prelude globals); `--emit-cheader` exports no globals; `--emit-cheader` emits hyphenated invalid C identifiers (**a missed `sanitize-for-c` call site** — it reaches type names but not field names or function names); `(exclude-prelude)` in an imported file dies `unknown top-level form`; the misleading `not defined anywhere in this compilation unit` message (**closed at the cause by G-0**, for the import shapes it covers); `pkind-flow-check` only diagnoses a `TY-PTR` source, so a `CStr` source flows into a non-null `(ref T)` unchecked, in both a `defvar` initializer and the identical local; `emit-as`'s int→int rule ignores the literal value on the Val, so `(as i8 5)` is refused as lossy even though 5 fits; `(defvar g:i1 5)` emits `global i1 5`, which LLVM silently truncates to `true` (`int-literal-fits` returns 1 at width ≤ 1); `tests/run-tests.sh`'s parallel unit output can interleave, making its PASS count unreliable by ±1 (FAIL detection unaffected); three `fmt-s` call sites pass two substitutions to a one-argument helper, a cold diagnostic path that segfaults the compiler with no output if ever hit; a wrong-arity call to a solitary (non-overloaded) `defn` is not diagnosed at all, very likely why those `fmt-s` sites have gone unnoticed; and `parse-type-from-node`'s `unable to parse type expression` diagnostic is unreachable for an unrecognized type-expression head, surfacing instead as a misleading `defstruct: field 'x' missing :type`; a fn-pointer-typed `defvar` could not be declared at all (**FIXED**, in the interlude between G-3 and G-4 — a G-0 regression in `name-existing-kind`); `aref` emits a hardcoded `i64` GEP index invalid on AVR; a `defvar` may be declared twice in one unit with no diagnostic (**FIXED**, in Stage 15 B4 — `emit-defvar` reads `Sym.defvar-state` and names both definitions; the same rule now covers every other definer, whose duplicates were silent rather than merely unlocated); `as` did not arm the want channel for a return-only-tyvar generic (**FIXED**, in G-5); `(= h null)` does not compile for a fn-pointer value (`emit-binop-vals` gates on `is-ptr-like`, which excludes `TY-FN`); `type-size` has no `TY-FN` case, so a fn-pointer slot emits `align 1` (conservative, not a miscompile); and `coerce-int-val` has no `raw`→`TY-FN` case, so `(let (f:(fn i32)(i32) null) …)` still dies though the identical `defvar` now compiles — **Items 11 and 12 were fixed together on 2026-08-02** — the seven (not three) format-helper arity violations, and the missing call-arity check on the solitary-`defn` call path (`(f 1 2)` against a one-parameter `f` used to emit `call i32 @f(i32 1, i32 2)` and link); the fix is one shared rule function the direct, fn-pointer and BoxedFn paths all call, it found two latent wrong-arity calls in the compiler's own source, and a hand-written `declare` stays open-tailed so a C variadic can still be called. That pass also corrected two recorded premises (only two of the format sites segfault; the other two printed a garbage *count*) and disproved the recorded causal link between the two items. **The W9 table was then re-probed against the current compiler at the close of the B series (2026-08-09) and worked through in order: items 1, 2, 5, 3, 4, 6 (its string-path half), 7, 8, 9, 13, 15, 18, 19, 20 23 (its symbol half), 24, 25, 26 and 27 are now fixed, and closing them measured fourteen further pre-existing defects — items 25–38 — each filed with a cause rather than a symptom. Items 18 and 19 were two sites of one rule ("a function pointer is one `ptr` register"), which now has a name (`is-ptr-repr`) instead of a convention telling each site to rewrite it, and item 19 showed that self-compilation cannot witness a defect confined to a construct the compiler does not itself use. Item 20 is the deliberate *limit* of that rule — `null` reaches a fn-pointer slot but a data pointer does not — and could not be gated on it, since the literal and a `raw` binding share a type singleton; chasing its one working position surfaced items 33 and 34, where a call's argument types are effectively unchecked. Item 23 is the same shape one registry up: R2 merges every namespace's methods into one bare-keyed `Generic`, and mangling asked that merged set two questions — which prefix, and whether to suffix — that are per-namespace facts, so a namespace's exported symbol depended on what else the compilation unit contained and on import order (measured: a `(ns qb)` library that merely imports a `(ns qa)` one defining the same name became unlinkable through its own `.nuch` and C header). Both decisions now come from the method; the dispatch half — two namespaces defining one name with one signature — is split out as item 35, since it needs a ruling revisited rather than a bug fixed. Item 24 is the same registry asked to hold something it never received: every callable name is written into `g-globals` (what symbol is this) *and* `g-generics` (which method matches these types), `emit-defn` writes both even for a solitary function, and a `.nuch` `declare` wrote only the first — so an imported implementation was invisible to `(dyn P)` and to `extend`'s conformance check while ordinary calls, which ask `g-globals`, worked throughout. Registering it restored the invariant and required two things the tree had only ever handled locally: an imported symbol is a fact `finalize-generics` may not revise (`Method.ir-fixed`), and a predicate over a merged registry must say which of its members it is about, since R4's duplicate-*definition* check began firing between two headers over definitions neither importing file makes. Item 25 is a generated C header that never completed the struct **tag** its own references use, so no library type could be used by value from C; the recorded one-word fix held (`typedef struct Rec { … } Rec;`, at the union producer too, retiring item 3's `cheader-by-value-c` workaround), while its recorded blast radius did not — the three headers it named break for two other causes, one closed alongside (`Char`/`Err` are builtin scalars the name-keyed renderer spelled as structs) and one split out as item 37 (a header naming an imported library's type emits no `#include` for it). 340→349 of 375 generated headers compile, none regressed. Item 26 is that same header emitter *deriving* each function's exported symbol instead of asking the registry that decided it: `ns-ir-base` is the symbol only for a solitary non-operator `defn`, so an overload or an operator (`=` links as `eq.String.String`, never `=`) was declared under a name no object defines — 100 of the 236 symbols the committed `lib/*.h` bound, now 170 and all defined, taking `lib/*.h` from 29 to 33 of 34 compiling. The fix gives the C-header pass the prescan sequence the `.nuch` emitter has always run, which also closed item 27 (a template tyvar exported as `struct T` — the recorded cause was wrong; the template predicate answers correctly and was simply reading an unpopulated struct-template registry) and most of item 38 (29 files that used to get a header for a program that does not compile now get the compiler's own byte-identical diagnostic), and surfaced a crash `--emit-nuch` had carried since it was written — an anonymous struct in a signature wrote IR to a null stream during the prescan. Item 28 is the last of that family: `sanitize-for-c` maps illegal *characters* and has no notion of a reserved word, so `union`, `signed` and `class` — ordinary Nucleus names — reached the header intact and it did not parse. Each is renamed with a trailing `_` and re-bound through the existing asm label, so the symbol never moves; the table carries **C++'s** keywords too, which is what the corpus actually needed — the one committed header the defect touched was `lib/allocator.h`, whose parameter named `new` is legal C and fatal C++, taking `lib/*.h` from 32 to 33 of 34 parsing as C++. Two refinements the corpus forced: a struct **tag** is an identifier too, so definition and reference escape in lockstep, and the escape goes on a *joined* name rather than its fragments, leaving every prefixed enum constant unchanged. It also measured item 39 — a name beginning with a digit passes every front-end pass and dies in LLVM's IR parser, whose message names a line in the generated IR and nothing in the user's source. Item 29 leaves that family for the import graph: a `.nuch` header was the one file kind the whole-graph prescan skipped, so its names became known when its import form was *reached* rather than when the graph was walked, and a use above the import reported `not defined anywhere in this compilation unit` for a name that is in the unit — while the `.nuc` spelling of the same library resolved fine. Measured kind by kind against that spelling, the recorded list of four was one short: a `defmethod` fails too, and real parity with a `defn` includes every arm of an overload set. The ruling was the item's own (a registration-without-emission pass over header forms, under the per-path guard the tree already uses rather than a second implementation of the registrars); the design question it did not pose is where the line between the halves falls, since hoisting *emission* as well would move every `declare` to the top of the declaration stream and change the IR of every module that imports a header — the compiler's own included. Registration moves and emission does not, which keeps the corpus at 231 identical / 0 differing, and which in turn makes "already registered" a per-name question: all three importers skip a name something else has bound, so an emit-only pass that trusted the header as a whole would declare one global twice. The residue it measured is **symmetric** with `.nuc` — a macro, a union's arm constructors and a struct's layout still need the import above the use in either spelling — and is filed as item 40 rather than absorbed. Fixed alongside: `tests/run-tests.sh` could fail an assertion that matched, because `grep -q` under `set -o pipefail` SIGPIPEs its producer (186 of 200 identical runs on one 54KB IR).** Items 28–31 followed: 28 (a sanitized C name colliding with a reserved word, filing item 39), 29 (a `.nuch` header's names missing from the whole-graph prescan, closing item 6 and filing item 40), 30 (`as` refusing a float literal the implicit path accepts — the float twin of item 8, whose fix supplied its own second asker in `defvar-init-ir` by *starting to accept*), and 31 (`i1` read as signed, so `true` widened to −1 and `(< false true)`/`(> true false)` were both false). Item 31 is the first W9 fix to move the bootstrap: one `TY-I1` arm in `is-unsigned`, and the sweep the item asked for measured the move at 24 `sext i1`→`zext i1` sites tree-wide with 0 diagnostics changed. It also shows the limit of the self-hosting gate — the compiler carried the wrong value in six of its own locals and could not observe it, because every consumer tested `(!= x 0)`, which −1 and 1 both satisfy. Item 32 (an unsigned GEP index sign-extended, so `(aref p i)` with `i:ui32` ≥ 2^31 read *backwards*) is the counterexample beside it: the same 22-call-site `is-unsigned`, read through one consumer, moved five lines corpus-wide and left the compiler's own IR byte-identical, so the bootstrap it predicted it would move held on the first pass — a fix's reach follows the type's idiom in the code being compiled, not the helper's fan-out. It also gave the ATtiny1634 image its 4 bytes back (942 → 938), exactly as item 15 had measured them lost. Item 33 (a failed argument coercion discarded, so a call passed an argument of the wrong type with no diagnostic) is five lines that give the argument position the check the *return* position already had — and its real subject is not the deliberate type errors the item listed but `(take-f64 3)`, ordinary code that emitted `call double @take-f64(i32 3)` and printed `0.000000`, the one typed slot in the language that did not refuse an int in a float. The finding is what the silence had been hiding: switching it on rejected exactly three corpus programs, **none of which was a wrong call**. Two were `(dyn P)` method calls — a boxed receiver bypasses the vtable whenever the protocol has a single conformer, returning the right answer by luck because the box's data word is the first SysV register (item 41, fixed alongside) — and the third was `examples/implicit-cast.nuc` calling `(show-ptr 0)` under the comment "defcast fires", which it never had, the literal being `i32` and the rule keyed `i64` (item 42, filed). A silent pass-through hides the compiler's bugs, not just the program's. Item 34 is its sibling two lines away in the same loop: the guard compared **lowered** IR type strings, and every pointer flavour lowers to `ptr`, so a `CStr`, a `raw` or a `(ref T)` in a `(fn …)` parameter compared equal and was never checked — the callee called a string. The guard now asks `type-eq` and `safe-coerce-val` answers through `coerce-int-val`, the chokepoint every other typed slot already funnelled through, so the ruling the item asked for turned out to have been written with item 20: the argument position simply had to stop answering it alone. Its refusal matrix now matches `let`'s row for row, the same-width sign-change pairs it had also been skipping reach the literal range check (`(take-ui32 -1)` was 4294967295, now the error `let` always gave), and the whole corpus is **0 IR diffs, 0 diagnostic diffs, 0 status changes** — replacing a lowered-type comparison with a type-identity one moved nothing that was already correct. **Item 36** closed the neighbouring silence in the `.nuch` import path: a header entry whose name the importing unit also *defines* was dropped whole — no binding, no `declare`, no method — so a **qualified** `(lib2/helper 3)` emitted `call i64 @helper`, the unit's own function, at a type the library's never had. The discriminator turned out to be *where* the declaration is written rather than what it says: `--emit-nuch` never re-exports a top-level `declare`, so a `.nuch` entry can never be a forward declaration of the importing unit's own function, while a `.nuc` one — W1e's cross-file cycle-breaker — always may be. A signature comparison was tried first, passed the corpus and the bootstrap, and was wrong: it breaks the cycle-breaker, and the pre-existing test that had *recorded* this very defect uses identical signatures, so a signature test cannot see the case at all. Accepting path untouched by construction and by measurement (all ten in-tree skips are libc re-declarations), **0 IR diffs corpus-wide**, headers unchanged; the prefixed-import shadowing measured beside it is filed as item 43. **Item 37** made a generated C header usable: a reference to a user type is spelled `struct NAME`, and when the type is defined in *another* unit the header named a tag C never completes — `lib/string-split.h`'s `struct StrView cur;` would not compile at all, and a by-value *parameter* of such a type parses and then cannot be called, which is worse for being silent. The ruling went neither of the two ways the item proposed: not the import list (which presumes a search path) and not a forward declaration (which cannot carry a by-value field), but the **defining unit of each type the header actually names**, spelled by the convention the Makefile already commits to (`lib/%.h` beside `lib/%.nuc`, which is what a quoted include resolves against). Two supports were missing: `StructDef.src-file` was uniformly null in this mode, since every writer of it is a definition-time writer and `--emit-cheader` emits no struct — the prescan now records it — and the recording happens during emission, so a pre-pass over the signature positions runs the same renderer before the preamble is printed. That pre-pass had to replicate the emitters' four not-exported tests: without them `lib/vector.h` and `lib/combinators.h`, which spell no `struct` at all, took includes for a parametric field and a template's return type, and the change was 10 headers rather than the 5 that genuinely need one. All 34 lib headers now compile standalone; **0 IR / 0 diagnostic / 0 status diffs** over 396 programs and a byte-identical bootstrap; the C side is asserted by linking and *running* a consumer that copies an imported struct by value, and by stripping the include back out to watch the original error return. The `!T` return-type spelling it does not reach — `struct _BANGui8` over a value the IR returns as `i64` — is filed as item 44. **Item 37 closed the last cheader defect that was not a naming ruling; item 44** closed the naming one, and the probe rewrote its premise. The IR's `i64` return is not evidence that `!ui8` is unrepresentable in C — it is SysV coercing an 8-byte struct, and a hand-written C declaration of the right shape decodes it (`tag=0 v=65`, measured against the committed object). The real defect is that **`!T` is a `(Result T Err)` template instance in sugar spelling**, and `--emit-cheader` already refuses template instances with a comment; the sugar escaped only because `cheader-template-instance` tests a cell head while `!ui8` arrives as a symbol. So the fix makes two spellings of one type agree — the third instance of the trap items 3 (`usize`) and 25 (`Char`/`Err`) fixed. An omission still beats the tag, because the tag invites a wrong guess that is *silent*: completing `struct _BANGui8` as `{uint8_t v;}` compiles, links, runs and prints 0 where Nucleus reads 65. Wider than the row's title — parameters, `defvar` and `ptr:!T` carried it too, and `?` came free — while `!ptr:T`/`!ref:T` stay declared, since they really are a bare `T*`; that exemption is gated by linking and *running* a C consumer, not by grepping the header. Hoisting the emitter's four refusals into one `cheader-defn-skip-reason` let item 37's include pre-pass ask the same question of the whole form, which also dropped a surplus include from `examples/dyn-protocol.h`. 5 committed headers / 14 declarations, 0/0/0 over 396 programs, byte-identical bootstrap, 620 tests. Restoring these declarations wants the general feature the emitter is waiting on — a C typedef for a stamped template instance — not a special case. **Item 39** then closed the last naming defect, taking neither answer its row proposed: the codebase has answered this question since SM-1 — a Nucleus-legal, LLVM-illegal name is *mangled* (`?`→`_QMARK`), so `(defn even? …)` works — and a leading digit is the same class, so it gets one leading `_` in the same function rather than a rejection or LLVM's quoting syntax. Measuring first found the row far too narrow: the transform was applied at the GLOBAL-symbol layer only, so a parameter, `let` binding, `match` binder and `label` target all reached LLVM raw, and that half is live rather than latent — `define i32 @add_QMARK(i32 %ok?.arg)` mangles the function name and not the parameter three tokens from it. `ir-name-token` now knows LLVM's first-*position* rule as well as its character class, and all fifteen producers of an LLVM local apply it (`scope-define` still keys on the source name). `sanitize-for-c` stays a pure character map: C's identical rule belongs on a *complete* identifier, so the escape sits beside the reserved-word escape in `cheader-c-ident`, and a union arm reads `_2Shape_2circle` rather than `_2Shape__2circle`. Same `_` on both sides, so the C spelling **is** the link symbol and no `asm()` label appears. 0/0/0 over 396 programs plus 0 header diffs, byte-identical bootstrap, 623 tests — each new case links and *runs*, because parsing was never the claim. **Items 43 and 35** then closed together, being one ruling with one audit under it: B4's deferred bare-reference audit (a generic is the one registry R2 keys BARE, so a prefixed import's overloads joined the same unqualified set a bare call resolves in) came back **clean** at every `Method.src-ns` writer, and the filter is one predicate — `name-ref-key-at`'s three slots stated as a predicate, with `g-ns-declared == 0` as the byte-identical hatch every other resolver already has. The work was one layer down and is the finding: **a monomorphized template body was resolved in the CALLER's import environment.** `MonoJob` was the one deferred-work record carrying no environment, where `DynAnnot` and `InitJob` both carry theirs for the stated reason that the drain runs later — and unlike those two a stamp's body is the *library's* text, so the restored globals are the template's. `b4-qualified-template` was passing only by accident: delete one of its callee's two overloads and the identical program fails on `HEAD`, because a merged bare-keyed generic resolved from anywhere while a solitary name went through `globals-lookup-ref`, which has filtered since B2b — so whether a library's template could call its own namespace's functions depended on how many overloads the callee happened to have. Item 35 then relaxes R4's rule to the emitted **ir-prefix** rather than the namespace name (which states the concrete harm, a symbol emitted twice, instead of a proxy for it) and moves the refusal to the ambiguous *use*; allowing the pair also rotted a key nobody predicted — `(name, param-types)` stops identifying a method on the DEFINITION side, so `defn-ir-name` gave both files `@qa__describe` until it was taught to ask `src-ns` too. 0/0/0 over 396 programs plus 0 header diffs, byte-identical bootstrap, 637 tests, one in-tree diagnostic deliberately moved to a more specific tier. **Item 38** then closed the last header defect, and its row's "structural rather than one missing check" was right about the kind of cause and one layer too low about its location: the compiler has **two** validation layers, a permissive prescan and a strict emitter, and every prescan *deliberately* defers its diagnosis to the emitter (`defn-params-to-types`: "the located diagnostic is emit-defn's job"; `prescan-file-imports`: "a missing library is diagnosed by do-import"). `--emit-cheader` and `--emit-nuch` run the first layer and never the second, so nothing downstream ever asked — which makes the three surviving segfaults and the unresolvable-import residue **one** defect, and explains why item 26 fixed most of it and structurally could not fix the rest.
