Surveyed: across 15 headers, **18 wrong-size rows / 16 distinct types** out of 65 comparable structs (`jmp_buf` 24/200, `siginfo_t` 24/128, `in6_addr` 8/16, `itimerspec` 16/32, `__fpos_t` 24/16), and a census of 232 struct bodies over 30 headers finds **163 (70%) carry a shape the body parser cannot read** — though most of that fails *safe* as an opaque type, and the silent class is the narrower set reached indirectly through a typedef or an opaque tag. Three findings outside the brief: `struct timespec` is unusable because `clang -E` emits a `#` linemarker **inside the body** and `c-skip-ws` skips whitespace only (which also blocks `stat` and `itimerspec`, so it must land before the array work); `struct epoll_event` is 16/12 because `__attribute__((packed))` is ignored (same class, different mechanism, out of scope); and `struct __attribute__((__may_alias__)) sockaddr` registers a phantom opaque type named `__attribute__`. Cost constraint already met and verified: programs with and without `(import-use "setjmp.h")` link **byte-identical at 15848 bytes**. Staged L1 (member → opaque) → L2 (linemarkers, then array members with real extents) → L3 (array typedefs decay in parameter position) → L5 (a C typedef becomes a Nucleus type name: `x:off_t` is `unknown type` today, because `g-cheader-typedefs` has exactly one reader, inside the C parser — written up as a recommendation and **approved 2026-08-25**) → L4 (`returns_twice`, which must come from a name list because glibc's `setjmp.h` contains **zero** `returns_twice` annotations — clang supplies it as a library builtin). L5 sits before L4 because it needs L3's array typedef and L4's reason for being last — the sole setjmp-specific item — still holds. On integrating C typedefs with `deftype`: **separate registries, shared lookup path** — the brief's hypothesis that the array case existed only for want of an `(array T N)` `Type*` is right and is what makes L2/L3 cheap, but the C table has no `Node` to be lazy with, its order semantics are deliberately the opposite of `deftype`'s and are what make a cycle impossible by construction, and its redefinition and export policies are opposite too. **The standard 186-module IR sweep is the wrong gate here twice over**: it is blind (the whole tree imports six C headers, exposing 14 of the 65 surveyed types) and it is not neutral (163 of 186 modules change under L1), so the bar becomes a characterized diff confined to named `%X = type` lines with the 96 header-mode outputs still byte-identical. **As built (2026-08-26):** all five landed, in the staged order L1 → L2 → L3 → L5 → L4; `make test` 834/834 (was 813), `make abi-test`/`make layout-test` PASS, `make check-headers` 69/69, `make bootstrap` PASS. Three corrections found during implementation: the L3 aggregate path needed two sites kept name-for-name, not one — `cheader-scan-opaque-decl` (the whole-unit prescan) and `struct-upgrade-aliases` (which emits the `%X = type {...}` line) both have to agree with `c-parse-struct-decl`, or the alias step emits a type the real import never defines; `g-array-ok`, the consume-once permission gating where `(array T N)` may appear, does not survive delegation — `parse-type-from-node` reads and clears it before handing off to `parse-type-name`, so a deftype'd array-typed field was refused until the delegation was reclassified consume-vs-inherit (this incidentally fixed a pre-existing bug: `(deftype Buf (array i32 4))` + `(defvar env:Buf)` was refused before); and the REPL hazard is on the *read* side, not the write side — a rolled-back form leaves `g-cheader-typedefs` referencing a stale struct and the next getelementptr dies with `base element of getelementptr must be sized`, fixed by a `ReplState.cheader-typedefs` save/restore field mirroring the neighboring `cheader-skipped` field. L4 is not inert for every other header, contra the original staging note: the compiler's own `unistd.h` import needed a boot refresh to pick up `declare i32 @vfork() returns_twice`. The setjmp/longjmp worked example (§7) is now a running test (`examples/setjmp-guard.nuc`, `tests/expected/setjmp-guard.out`) and surfaced three glibc-specific caveats: `setjmp`/`sigsetjmp` are unconditional macros reaching `_setjmp`/`__sigsetjmp` respectively, so Nucleus's `(setjmp env)` — which imports the real exported symbol, not the macro — actually saves the signal mask where C source does not; `longjmp` already carried `noreturn` needing no new work. Seven items surfaced and deliberately not fixed, recorded in §6: an inline function-pointer struct member still abandons the struct (blocks `sigaction`/`sigevent_t`); the with-body aggregate array typedef form still discards its extent; `__attribute__((packed))` is still ignored (`epoll_event` 16/12, silently wrong); C11 anonymous struct/union members still fail safe to opaque (`rusage`); `--emit-cheader` does not emit an `#include` for the header a rendered C typedef came from; `Sym.returns-twice` is write-only by design (no reader outside the two writer sites); and `context/build.md`'s doom-port failure entry was checked against the current tree and still matches (`src/w_file.nuc:43` `SEEK_SET` redefinition) — flagged, not touched. **C-boundary defects DONE (2026-08-26 through 2026-08-28)** — [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md), reading [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §4.1's three "frictions" as language defects rather than costs of a hypothetical libclang binding. Measuring them turned up two live silent miscompiles nothing had caught: `coerce-int-val`'s `sk == dk` short-circuit (`src/nucleusc.nuc:3605`) waved a signature-mismatched function through any typed fn-pointer slot (D3 — prints `-1780842467`), and `emit-funcall-value` performed no coercion, vararg promotion or ABI lowering on an indirect call at all (D4 — the same function called directly and through a pointer printed `907` and `7`). **Phases 1–4 (D1–D8 and D6, the last via C1/C2) landed 2026-08-26**: FP-1 puts the signature check ahead of the short-circuit; FP-2 routes both indirect-call emitters through the shared ABI path; FP-3 renders a real signature in diagnostics instead of the internal `__fnty_N` key; FP-4/FP-5 stop the C importer and `--emit-cheader` from erasing function-pointer types in all four declarator positions; SV-1/SV-2 let a by-value struct's read materialize into a fresh slot while `.set!`/`.&` keep taking the receiver's own; C1/C2 parse a bare `unsigned`/`signed` and record a skip reason. **The three remaining C-parity gaps closed on the same standard — Nucleus should express anything C can.** Wide floats (D8, FL-1…FL-7): `f16`/`x86_fp80`/`fp128` needed no new numeric tower, since f64→f80/f128 are exact widenings and LLVM already ABI-classifies all three per target; a decimal literal at those widths still rounds through f64 (staged separately, [future/decimal-float-literals.md](future/decimal-float-literals.md)); **857 tests**. Packing (D10, PK-1…PK-3): `(defstruct :packed …)`/`(defstruct :align N …)`, a packed field forced to `align 1` wherever `emit-load`/`emit-store` derive alignment from the type, and a cross-target `clang --target=<t> -ffreestanding -fsyntax-only` oracle that caught AVR byte-aligning every type (`BIGGEST_ALIGNMENT` 8 bits) since before packing existed; **864 tests**, then **871** once `aligned(N)` landed (2026-08-28). **Bitfields and anonymous members landed together 2026-08-28**, sharing one prerequisite: `struct-walk` (FR-1) becomes the single source of truth for a struct's size, LLVM element list, GEP index and bit position, which anonymous members needed anyway for name lookup through a member (AN-1/AN-2) and bitfields (BF-1…BF-4) needed for a resolved field reference to carry a GEP path plus an optional bit range. The cross-target oracle found three real psABI divergences no spec reading would have: AVR ignores C's declared-type bitfield rule entirely, `packed` drops the byte-crossing rule but not the zero-width one, and aarch64 gives every bit-field its declared type's alignment whether or not it is named. `FILE` now lays out at 216 bytes matching `cc` (`_flags` at 0, `_lock` at 136) and `cmsghdr`'s flexible array member is `(array T -1)` (`0` was already the layout prescan's provisional-length marker). **The census closes at 111/111** — every one of the nine formerly-blocked libc types (`sigaction`, `sigevent`, both pthread cleanup structs, `max_align_t`, `FILE`, `sigcontext`, `rusage`, `cmsghdr`) now sizes exactly as `cc` does; **883 tests**. [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §8 then re-evaluated its own §7 trigger and **the recommendation holds — finish the parser** — but withdrew one of its own arguments: "Nucleus has nothing to lower anonymous members onto" was stale (the anon-struct/union memoizer already existed for the C parser to call), and the real count is that only 4 of roughly 15 remaining items were parser work, which a linked libclang would not have bought for the other 11. The full FR-1/BF/AN mechanics and the psABI divergence table are in the dated "Known constraints / gotchas" entries below (2026-08-26 through 2026-08-28), not repeated here. **Bool type (Part 1) DONE (2026-08-22), `db895b2`** — the divorce of `bool` from the integers ([stage16-ergonomics/bool-type-plan.md](stage16-ergonomics/bool-type-plan.md), executing part 1 of [stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md](stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md)'s Recommendation; part 2 is the truthiness entry below). `TY-I1` → `TY-BOOL`, and the *representation* question splits from the *arithmetic* one: `int-width`/`is-unsigned` stay true of `bool` (kept **unsigned**, which is what lets `(as i32 b)`, `(unsafe/cast bool n)` and `vararg-promote` reuse the existing widening machinery and keeps `false < true` under the unsigned `icmp`), while `is-int-type` now **excludes** `bool`, so `+ - * / % bit-*` refuse a `bool` operand outright — the headline defect this fixes is `(+ true true)`, which used to evaluate to `false` by one-bit wraparound. Implicit coercion is **none** in either direction: `coerce-int-val` gets no `bool` arm, so `(let (b:bool 1) …)` is a type mismatch and the `{0,1}` range rule is deleted, not narrowed — `true`/`false` are `bool`'s only literals. The `i1` spelling is retired, not aliased: naming it is `i1 is no longer a type — use bool`, replacing the four `w9-i1-*literal*` fixtures that pinned the rule this deletes. `type-spelling`/`type-mangle-token`/`type-to-c` follow (`"i1"` → `"bool"`/`"bool"`, `_Bool` → `bool`), and ~40 `i32`-returning predicates (`lib/` first, then `src/`) retype to `bool`-returning, four of them (`c-fn-noreturn`, `gcheck-special-form`, both `contains?`s) beyond the annotation sweep because each returns a now-bool expression verbatim. Bundled in the same commit as the Container type sugar item above (`deftype`/`deftype-`), whose **799 tests (was 778)** count covers both; `make bootstrap` byte-identical. A silent miscompile surfaced during the divorce and was left unfixed as its own, unrelated item: a non-void function whose return-position `cond` collapses to void (a `bool` branch joined with an `i32` literal did, once `bool` left the integer family) emits the return type's zero constant with **no diagnostic at all**, catchable only by running the compiled binary, not by any build gate. Two doc items were this feature's own debt and are closed by this audit: `docs/types.md` no longer documents `i1` as a live spelling (the Built-in Types row, the `{0,1}` range-rule paragraph and the Literal Values entries all now read `bool`, with `i1` kept only where it names the emitted IR type), and this entry is that debt's other half. **Truthiness (bool part 2) DONE (2026-08-29)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md](stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md) §"As built"): nil punning at the six condition sites, the matching narrowing arm, and the non-null-pointer diagnostic. One shared rule, `condition-bool` (`src/union-emit.nuc`), replaces the five inline `(!= kind TY-BOOL)` tests the six sites each carried — `raw`/`CStr`/`?T` lower to `icmp ne ptr … null`, a value-`(Maybe T)` (recognised **structurally** by the new `value-maybe-union-of`, beside `result-union-of`, because a stamped instance's name is `Maybe.<mangled-arg>` and is not a stable key) to an `extractvalue` of the tag plus an `icmp eq`, a non-null `ptr`/`(ref T)` to "is non-null, so this test is always true", a `!T` to "a Result is neither true nor false", and everything else to "condition must be bool, not `<type>`". It is an **elimination rule, not a coercion**: `coerce-int-val` is untouched, so a `bool` parameter/field/slot still refuses a pointer and `(defn g (b:bool))` does not become a universal dispatch candidate. **888 tests (was 883), `make bootstrap` converges with no boot refresh** (nothing in `src/` uses the sugar yet, and the new arm is a no-op for every condition the compiler currently writes); abi/layout/check-headers green. The plan's one wrong prediction: it called for "roughly five lines in each function", but `test-false-nonnull` needs **no** arm — a bare symbol being *false* proves the binding **null**, so there is nothing to record, and `(when (not m) (return))` still narrows because the existing `not` arm delegates to `test-true-nonnull`. A mirror arm would have been unsound. Two smaller findings: `emit-while` and `emit-short-circuit` blamed `(node line)` directly, which is **0** for an interned symbol — harmless while a bare symbol could not *be* a condition, the common case once it can, now `node-line … (cc line)` like `emit-cond`; and `(when c"lit" …)` compiles as always-true, since a `c"…"` literal is a non-null `CStr` and `CStr` is nullable by *type* (same class as `(when true …)`). Deliberately not done: "all primitive values are true" and zero-is-false (the design's own rulings — `(when n:i32 …)` staying an error is what keeps zero-is-false available), `TY-FN` in condition position (not on the Recommendation's list, and `is-ptr-like` excludes `TY-FN` deliberately, so admitting a fn pointer is a separate ruling), and adopting the sugar across `src/`'s 1,135 null tests (mechanical, but it moves the compiler's own IR and needs its own refresh cycle). Found and not fixed, both part 1's debt: `docs/types.md` still documents `i1` as a live spelling and its `{0,1}` range rule although part 1 retired both, and part 1 (`db895b2`) never got a progress entry of its own. **keyword-markers.md §7 follow-ups + user-declarable declaration attributes DONE (2026-08-29)** — three small items, two of them §7's own "deliberately not done" list ([stage16-ergonomics/keyword-markers.md](stage16-ergonomics/keyword-markers.md) §9-§10, [stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md) §10). **(1) The `has-rest`-by-count bug.** Two registration sites inferred `has-rest` from `(< (defn-params-count …) (node-len …))`, which is equally true of an `:optional` list, so every `:optional` defn registered `has-rest = 1`. `defn-params-has-rest` (a real `marker-named … "rest"` scan, beside `defn-params-count` in `src/generics.nuc`) replaces both. §7 filed it as a stale flag; it is not — `finalize-generics` binds the prescan's Type for a **solitary** name, so a call *above* the definition took the `:rest` path and a program with no `:rest` in it died `a :rest call needs the node runtime`; and three widening tiers gate on `(= (m has-rest) 0)`, so an **overloaded** `:optional` method was skipped by all of them and reported `no matching method`. Fixing it exposed the sibling: `generic-register-method` also never set `nopt`/`opt-defaults`, so the forward call then said `expected 2 args, got 1` for a function that takes 1-2 — `defn-params-set-opt` writes those two from the same two sites. **The generalizable half: a registration that builds a partial copy of a Type another pass builds fully is a divergence per FIELD, not per bug.** **(2) A never-instantiated parametric union's `:repr`.** `register-union-template` retained the form and parsed no arms, so a template validated nothing until it was stamped. The mode is decidable without a stamp, so the registrar now calls the real `defunion-strip-repr` for its diagnostics and discards the filtered chain — no need to open §7's general "when is a template body checked" question. §7 undercounted: the same call site carries **four** diagnostics a template never reached, including §3's own retirement of `&repr`, so the marker sweep's fourth chokepoint was true only of *concrete* unions. **(3) `returns_twice` on a Nucleus `defn`** (c-header-layout.md §3.4's scope note, taken). What landed is the attribute **slot**, not one attribute: `:noreturn` / `:returns-twice`, a run in either order, between the return operand and the body (`defn`) or after it (`declare`), with the bare `noreturn` / `returns_twice` spellings **retired** — adding a keyword beside a bare symbol would have recreated the two-spellings-for-one-idea state this very item removed. Seven dispatch sites, not the two the description implies; the one genuinely **missing** before this change is the `.nuch` export, which never carried `noreturn` either — `nuch-declare-import` had a reader with no writer. Two boot refreshes, `keyword-markers.md` §4's shape exactly, because `src/reader.nuc`'s own `die-at` is inside the compiler's translation unit; the sweep is two lines rather than 58 files. Still write-only by design (LLVM derives the caller-side handling from the attribute on the call), so §6's note is revised rather than retracted. **905 tests (was 902)**, each new assertion checked to fail against a compiler built from the pre-change `boot/nucleusc.ll`; bootstrap reconverged; abi/layout/check-headers (69)/avr (8) green. Measured and left alone: a solitary `:optional`/`:rest` defn exports a `.nuch` its own importer refuses (`declare-param-type` rejects the marker by design), and overload dispatch matches exact arity and declared types, so an overloaded `:optional` needs every argument and an overloaded `:rest` is unreachable — both now stated in `docs/toplevel.md`. **Template-ref type equality DONE (2026-08-29)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/template-ref-equality.md](stage16-ergonomics/template-ref-equality.md)) the last known silent-miscompile class filed against Stage 16: `(ref (Vector i32))` bound to a `(ref (Vector i64))` slot, passed as that parameter and stored into that field with no error anywhere, printing `8589934593` for `(invoke v 0)`. It is **D3's chokepoint one arm lower** — `coerce-int-val`'s `(when (= sk dk) (return v))`, the line FP-1 inserted the function-pointer answer immediately above — and `fn-slot-type-compat` was never fn-specific, so the rule is that function renamed `slot-type-compat` and *called* there and in `safe-coerce-val`. Two corrections to the shared-cause story: a **third** site, `defvar-addr-of-ir` (the constant renderer, conventions.md's second typed-slot path on its sixth bite), and the fact that only the **pointer** kinds were silent — a struct/array/union mismatch was already caught by the LLVM verifier at a line in a file the user did not write. Also: **`as` had refused this the whole time**, so the implicit path was looser than the explicit one. **The rule was the easy half.** Blast radius measured with the predicate installed as a report-and-accept probe: **61 sites — 60 in `src/`, 0 in `lib/`, 1 in `examples/`** (a lambda declared `:ptr:(ptr i32)` returning a `(ptr i32)`, a real mistake the laxness hid). All 60 are one call, `vector-new-in`, resting on a **second defect (SE-2)**: a generic whose type variable appears only in its *return* type is memoized and mangled on its parameter types alone, so the compiler contained exactly one `vector-new-in` — stamped `(Vector i32)` by whichever site got there first — and the other 59 silently took it; benign only by layout accident, since `(Vector T)` has no `T`-typed field. Fixed by a three-part split: the memo and symbol gain the bound return type (`.$r.<token>`) when `method-undetermined-tyvar` says the parameters do not determine it, the **reference** lookups (`generic-find-method-exact`, `generic-resolve` tier 0, and thereby `node-type-call`) skip such stamps so the want channel decides, and the **definition** side (`defn-ir-name`) gains a `ret` parameter — W9 item 35's split, one field further. Two findings fell out: **a computation with no reader is not "advisory", it is unverified** (the full A1 determination fixpoint was built at template registration and never read, while its one consumer used a params-only approximation — which, once a symbol depended on it, called `reduce`'s constraint-recovered `S` return-only and renamed every `reduce` stamp), and `pattern-determines-tyvar` was the one of three copies of the §3.7 alias expansion that did not expand **parametric type aliases**, though it documents itself as mirroring `unify-tpat`. **912 tests (was 908)**; boot refreshed (the compiler's own IR grows 41 stamp symbols, +1.15%) and the new fixed point confirmed; abi/layout/check-headers (69)/avr (8) green; **185 of 187 modules byte-identical** in the `--emit-llvm` sweep, the two that move being `.$r.` renames and nothing else, and all **374** header-mode outputs (`--emit-nuch` + `--emit-cheader`) unchanged. Deferred: `!T` pairs, which `type-eq` has no `TY-ERR` arm for and so still compare equal (zero probe hits; `TY-ERR` identity is its own question), and deduplicating the 40 near-identical `vector_new_in` stamps. **REPL JIT symbol precedence DONE (2026-08-29)** — closes the last item [stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §6 left open, and its diagnosis was wrong ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-jit-symbol-precedence.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-jit-symbol-precedence.md)). `(import-use node)` did **not** fail because `-rdynamic` makes ORC prefer the host's copies: LLJIT already links its own `<Process Symbols>` JITDylib **last in the default link order** (`LinkProcessSymbolsByDefault`), so a module's own definitions win. The compiler was attaching a *second, redundant* process generator to the **main** JITDylib — and a definition generator does not merely resolve a name, it **defines** it where it is attached, so the first macro module that referenced `alloc-node` claimed that name in main permanently and every later module defining it was a redefinition. The fix is the deletion of those two calls in `jit-ensure-init`; `-rdynamic` is untouched and stays load-bearing, measured both ways. Five probes against the real LLVM 19.1.7 C API pin each step, including that the `LLVMOrcSymbolPredicate` filter the item's first candidate would have needed **does** exist and is consulted per lookup — it simply was not necessary. The second candidate (declare-instead-of-define) was rejected as a *general* rule: its discriminator is `dlsym` on a name, which cannot tell "the compiler links this very file" from "a user's library reuses a compiler-internal name", turning today's loud `Duplicate definition` into a silent signature mismatch. All **34** `lib/` modules now import in a fresh session (R2 recorded 33 of 34). One residual, pinned rather than papered over by `tests/repl/host-runtime.in`: after `(import-use node)` the session defines its own `intern-symbol`, so a macro **first expanded after** the import mints symbols from a second intern table and `emit-node`'s pointer-identity special-form dispatch answers `unknown: cond`. It is loud, confined to special-form heads (a function head resolves by spelling), and absent for a macro already expanded before the import, since ORC materialisation is lazy and one-shot; its root cause is symbol *identity*, not *precedence*, and §5 names three routes with a recommendation (register-and-declare the host's own runtime). **913 tests (was 912)**; **187/187 + 96/96 outputs byte-identical**, so batch is provably unaffected; boot refreshed (one string constant removed renumbers the pool below it) and the new fixed point confirmed; abi/layout/check-headers (69)/avr (8) green. **`&` type sigil DONE (2026-08-30)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/ref-sigil.md](stage16-ergonomics/ref-sigil.md)): `&T` is sugar for `ref:T` in every type position, the payoff on the `&` the keyword-markers item freed. `x:&(Vector T)` and `):&T` fall out of the existing colon-paren fuse for free, and `&&T` / `?&T` / `&raw:T` compose. The decision that kept it to two functions is **where** it expands: in the lexer (`expand-ref-sigil`, called from `lex-atom`'s symbol and keyword tails), not in the type parser beside the `?`/`!` prefixes — a type spelling is read by more than the type parser, and `(Vector &T)` would have reached `collect-pattern-tyvars`, which collects any unresolvable symbol as a **tyvar**, so a parser-side sigil would silently have registered `&T` as a type variable rather than failing. Expanding before the parser hands desugar, generics and type-parsing the canonical spelling, so none of them changed. Two atoms it must not claim, and does not: `.&` (the rule keys on segment-start position, so interior `&` is untouched) and the four retired `&x` markers, left unexpanded so `reject-legacy-marker` still names their keyword replacement instead of failing later as `unknown type: rest` — their roster is now the shared `legacy-marker-tail` rather than a second copy. Deliberately **not** adopted in `src/`/`lib/` (that needs `make update-bootstrap` first), which is what keeps it a pure addition: **919 tests (was 914)**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical with no boot refresh, abi/layout/check-headers (69) green. **`&` address-of DONE (2026-08-30)** — ([ref-sigil.md](stage16-ergonomics/ref-sigil.md) §6): `&x` is sugar for `(addr-of x)`, the ~854-site form (530 `src/`, 198 `examples/`, 109 `lib/`) that is the most verbose thing in ordinary code. One line in `build-rmacros` beside `@` → `deref`, because the reader-macro table is matched in `next-tok` **before** `lex-atom` and only at a token boundary — so the two `&` rules partition the character by position *within the token* (a `&` that begins a token is the operator; a `&` inside one is the sigil) and neither consults context. The alternative, extending the sigil's lexer expansion into value position, is wrong and is recorded as such: value position already has a grammar for `name:Type` (an ascription lowering to `as`, via `emit-symbol-ref`'s `split-typed` + `as-convert`), so `ref:x` there already parses as *the variable `ref` cast to type `x`* — and `ref` is a legal variable name. Wrapping with the existing `addr-of` head instead cost **zero** value-side changes (emitter, `node-type-addr-of` and the closure capture rewrite all already match that cell). The two meet only at a standalone `&T` in a type slot, which the reader has already written as `(addr-of T)`; `parse-type-from-node`'s `ref` arm and `node-is-ptr-wrapper` read that head as `ref` (the latter carrying `collect-pattern-tyvars` and `unify-tpat` along for free), leaving `(addr-of T)` a legal-if-strange spelling of `(ref T)` as the only cost. `at-legacy-marker` (`src/reader.nuc`) keeps the reader macro off the four retired markers — measured, without it `&rest` degraded from its located diagnostic to `unknown type: rest` — consulting the same `legacy-marker-tail` roster. **Pointer-kind names reserved (2026-08-30)** — ([name-resolution.md](stage15-stress-test/name-resolution.md) §15): `ref` and `raw` were legal definition names. Only `ptr` was refused, and not because anything knew it was a pointer kind — `ptr` doubles as a standalone type (elem-less `void*`) so it sits in `g-primitive-type-set` and the one-kind rule caught it in passing; `ref`/`raw` name no type on their own, so no registry held them and `(defn ref …)` / `(defstruct raw …)` compiled. Fixed with a two-name `pointer-kind-named` roster answered by `binding-probe`'s BK-PRIMITIVE arm, with the noun refined per name in `binding-noun` (that row's payload IS the spelling) so they report **a pointer kind**, not "a built-in type". Deliberately NOT added to `g-primitive-type-set`, whose second consumer is `tyname-resolvable` — `ref` must stay unresolvable there or `collect-pattern-tyvars` loses its targeted `'ref' is a pointer kind, not a type argument` message; and deliberately NOT a new BK row, since rows are probed by index and one appended past `BK-DISPATCH-FIRST` would join `emit-dispatch`'s walk. Scope is **top-level definers**, which is what the rule always was: local binding names stay unguarded for every name, `i32` and `addr-of` included, so extending it to `let`/parameter names is a separate decision and is not taken. **922 tests** (3 new assertions), `make bootstrap` byte-identical with no boot refresh, abi/layout/check-headers (69) green. **Dot-forms phase 0 DONE (2026-08-30)** — ([dot-forms.md](stage16-ergonomics/dot-forms.md)): every member form now accepts a **quoted** field name, so `(.set! p 'x v)` and `(. p 'x)` mean exactly what the bare spellings do. `selector-literal-sym` already took either a bare `NODE-SYM` or a `(quote sym)` cell, but only the `get`/head-position read paths went through it; `.set!`, `.&` and `_get` (where `.` lands) each demanded a raw `NODE-SYM`, plus the `node-type-field` mirror in `src/generics.nuc` — four sites, all routed through the one helper now. `_get` was missed on the first pass and caught by the new IR-diff assertion, which is why it compares a whole member vocabulary rather than one form. Purely additive: it is what lets the migration spell the quoted selector under the CURRENT compiler, before a bare symbol in selector position becomes an ordinary variable reference. **924 tests** (2 new assertions), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **Dot-forms step 1 DONE (2026-08-30)** — (`--strict-selectors`, [dot-forms.md](stage16-ergonomics/dot-forms.md) §5): the migration driver. One helper, `note-bare-selector`, called from the four sites step 0 unified — `emit-field-set`, `emit-field-addr`, `emit-field-get`, and `emit-get-with-callee`, which covers both `get` and head position. It **reports and continues**, failing in `main` on the count with no output written, because enumerating a tree is the whole point and a fatal diagnostic would need one compile per site. In `emit-get-with-callee` the call sits *after* the W7 demotion, so a selector that already reads as a value is not reported — that case is what the end state makes the universal rule. Off by default, so `make`, the bootstrap and every other test are unaffected. First real measurement of the migration, which the plan's sampled estimate understated by 5×: compiling `src/nucleusc.nuc` (all of `src/` plus the `lib/` prelude) reports **6106 sites** deduplicated on file:line:name — 4976 head-position/`get`, 1094 `.set!`, 21 `_get`, 15 `.&`. So head position is 81% of the work, the flag is the only way to do step 2 at all, and `.` is already dead. Two blind spots recorded for step 2, both from its being a check on *emitted* code: a site inside a generic reports once per instantiation (`lib/vector.nuc`'s 29 `_get` sites report 629 times — raw stream 7555 lines for 6106 sites), and a generic this unit never instantiates is never checked — 27 files under `src/`+`lib/` report nothing. **926 tests** (2 new assertions), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **Dot-forms step 2 DONE (2026-08-30)** — the tree migrated to the quoted selector: **7254 sites** (6240 in `src`+`lib`, 983 in `examples`+`tests/fixtures`, 31 in `tests/repl/*.in`), flag reports zero everywhere, 926 tests, clean-room `make` from the committed `boot/nucleusc.ll` reconverges. Four things the plan did not anticipate. (1) **`make update-bootstrap` comes FIRST**: `make` compiles `src/` with `bin/nucleusc`, so the boot must already accept `(.set! p 'x v)` before a single file is rewritten — the pre-refresh boot answered `.set!: field name must be symbol`. (2) The flag found a **defect no source migration could reach**: `fn-rewrite-captures` SYNTHESIZES `(. self field)` with a bare selector (12 construction sites, plus the env-drop and `__alloc` paths), so step 3 would have broken every closure in the language; now routed through a `quoted-selector` helper. (3) A `.set!` inside a **quasiquote template** is data, reported at the use site with a gensym receiver — six in `with-handler` (`lib/error.nuc`), one `macrolet` in `examples/macrolet.nuc` where `(. p ~f)` becomes `(. p '~f)`; hand-fixed. (4) `nucleusc -i` reports every REPL form as `<repl>:1`, so transcripts match on (form, receiver, selector) with no line at all. The rewriter needed a real s-expression parser rather than regexes, for `->` threading (source says `(_ field)`, the compiler reports the EXPANSION's receiver, and a wrapped chain reports every step at the chain's opening line) and chained access `((tt sdef) name)` (receiver is a cell, `recv=()`). Verification is **IR identity, not review**: the same compiler on pre- and post-migration `src/` produced byte-identical output across all 6240 sites, and the 12-slot closure fix emitted byte-identical IR for all 154 examples; the one legitimate exception is a macro template, whose DATA changes when its selector is quoted (29 examples grew by exactly the added `quote` nodes), held instead by `make test`'s byte comparison of runtime output. **Dot-forms step 3 DONE (2026-08-30)** — the selector rule flipped: `selector-literal-sym` now accepts `(quote x)` and nothing else, so a bare symbol in selector position is an ordinary variable reference. Five pieces, each of which had to be built rather than merely allowed. (1) The three **fixed-position** forms (`.`, `.&`, `.set!`) refuse a bare symbol instead of reading it as a variable — they name a field statically, so a computed selector has no meaning there; `die-nonliteral-selector` says which spelling is missing (`-- write 'x`), because "field name must be symbol" would describe a bare symbol as failing a test it appears to pass. (2) Head position and `get` now route on the **receiver**, not on the argument's shape: `emit-callable-value` reached the member path only for a single literal-symbol argument, so with the literal gone `(p sel)` fell through to `emit-invoke-with-callee` and died with "quote needs the node runtime". The gate is a new `is-member-access-receiver` (`src/generics.nuc`), which answers what `emit-get-intrinsic` actually asks — struct/union value, or pointer to one. `access-receiver-sdef` could **not** be reused: it answers with a `StructDef` and so cannot speak for a `TY-UNION`, which is exactly the case a receiver-shaped gate must not drop (found by `(slot 'acv)` on a union). (3) An **unbound** bare symbol naming a real field is a missed quote, not an undefined variable, and `emit-computed-field` says so with the receiver and the spelling (`-- write (p 'x)`) — the message every un-migrated downstream line will hit. (4) The **W7 demotion is dead** (`callee-has-field`, `selector-shadowed-by-local`): the value reading is now unconditional, and the collision case W7 could not express at all — `(m 'count)` the field vs `(m count)` the key — is just the quote. (5) The **`k:CStr` annotation hatch** and `--strict-selectors` itself are both retired. `w7-local-not-a-field` now pins the opposite refusal (an `i32` local reaches the computed path and is refused for the selector's *type*); the two `s16-quoted-selector*` units, which asserted quoted≡bare, are replaced by `s16-selector-rule`, which pins the difference at every member form. **924 tests**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Docs re-migrated: 102 fixed-position selectors plus the head-position examples across `docs/*.md`. **Dot-forms step 4 DONE (2026-08-30)** — `.` and `.&` retired. `.&` became an **arity overload** on `addr-of` (1-arg = a binding's address, 2-arg = a field's; they cannot collide because a binding address takes a bare symbol and a field address a receiver plus a quoted selector), and `.` became `get`. Both spellings stay **reserved** so a retired-form message carrying the replacement always reaches the user — the shape Stage 14 gave `cast`/`ptr+`. Three findings. (1) **`node-type` had to split on the same arity**: without `node-type-field-addr`, a 2-arg `addr-of` falls into `node-type-addr-of`'s 1-arg path and types `(addr-of p 'f)` as `ptr:ptr:Point` — the BINDING's address — which is invisible in the value and surfaces only where the type is load-bearing (an argument position, an annotated binding). The mirror must also reproduce emit's array decay from the RAW field type; reusing `node-type-field`'s already-decayed answer gives `ptr:ptr:elem`. (2) **`.` was `_get` verbatim**, and `_get` bypasses a user `get` override while `get` respects one, so "already equivalent" holds only for a plain struct — the rename is proved by IR diff, not assumed. Nothing moved. (3) **The compiler synthesizes both forms** (8 `(intern-symbol ".")` + 2 `".&"` in the closure-capture and env-drop paths), the same hazard step 2 found; they became `_get` and `addr-of`, `_get` because a synthesized read of a compiler-generated env struct means raw field load. Those ten string constants are the ONLY intended IR change: 152/154 examples byte-identical, the exception being `macrolet.nuc`, whose quasiquote template holds a `.` as data. `_get` and `.set!` deliberately survive — `_get` is the documented override bypass, and `.set!` becomes the place form in step 5 rather than being renamed twice. **927 tests** (2 new units), `make bootstrap` byte-identical, boot refreshed. **`(Vector Char)` instantiation fix (2026-08-30)** — `examples/comb-shapes.nuc` emitted IR with `@count.pVector.Char` / `@vector_init.pVector.Char` called but never defined; LLVM's parser was the only thing that caught it. Pre-existing (reproduces identically on the pre-Stage-16 boot), and `Char` was the ONLY element type affected. Cause: `tyname-resolvable` (src/generics.nuc) answers "does this symbol name a concrete type or a free tyvar" from `g-primitive-type-set` plus a hand-maintained extras line; the set answers a DIFFERENT question (one-symbol-one-kind) and omits `Char`/`usize`/`ssize`/`raw`, and the extras line patched three of the four. Read as a tyvar, `Char` made the stamped `(Vector Char)` look like an unmonomorphized template, so `defn-has-receiver-tyvars` returned 1 and **emit-defn skipped its define** while the call site still named its ir-name — the same failure the `__fnty_N` (Stage 13 L7) and `ptr:X` cases already fixed twice. Fixed at the root: `tyname-resolvable` now consults `builtin-type-name`, the probe extracted FROM `parse-type-name` — the set it claims to mirror — so it cannot drift again. `comb-shapes` gained a `tests/expected/*.out` (it had none, which is why it rotted unnoticed) and a `builtin-tyname-resolvable` unit pins the whole builtin set rather than the one name that was missing. **929 tests**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **Dot-forms step 5 DONE (2026-08-31)** — `set!` takes a **place**, and `.set!`/`ptr-set!`/`aset!` are retired (reserved, each answering with the place that replaced it). **2068 sites**: 1982 in `src`+`lib`, 63 in `tests/run-tests.sh`, 23 in the docs' fenced blocks. Each place rebuilds the call its writer would have been given — same children, same line — and hands it to that writer's emitter, so the spelling change is provable by IR identity and inherits one set of diagnostics instead of growing a parallel set; those emitters' messages were renamed to `set!`, the only spelling left. The **return-value asymmetry is preserved exactly**: the name place yields the assigned value (the REPL echoes it), every other place yields `void`, because unifying them would change `cond`/`do` branch typing. Three findings. (1) **The capture hazard, a third time** — `.set!` walked its *receiver* explicitly, and the place form moves that receiver into **head position**, which `fn-capture-walk` skipped as "a call name, not a value reference" and `fn-rewrite-captures` kept verbatim. Both walk index 0 now; `is-local` and membership in `caps` are what keep a call to a global from being read as a capture. This closes the same hole for head-position member *reads*, which steps 3-4 made idiomatic without noticing: `(get p 'x)` captured `p` and `(p 'x)` did not. (2) **`()` reads as a null node**, so the place judgement has to precede any deref — `emit-set` read `(target 'kind)` unconditionally, so `(set! () 3)` segfaulted, before this step as well. (3) The rewriter needed the s-expression parser again, plus sigil-aware spans: the parser consumes `'`/`` ` ``/`~`/`@`/`&` *before* the atom, so a naive span turns `'x` into `x`, and the backwards scan must be floored by the previous sibling's end or `v:&(Vector T)` claims the `&` twice — every file was reassembled with rewriting off and asserted byte-identical first, 434 of 434. Verification is IR identity: pre- vs post-migration `src/` differs by **five string constants**, all inside the `with-handler` quasiquote template in `lib/error.nuc` (a template's selector is data — the same exception step 2 documented), and 29 of 154 examples differ by exactly those five and nothing else. **932 tests** (3 new units), `make bootstrap` byte-identical, boot refreshed. **Dot-forms step 6 DONE (2026-08-31)** — the `set` generic, which completes the stage's dot-forms work. A member place whose key is COMPUTED — `(set! (m k) v)` or `(set! (get m k) v)` — is the ordinary call `(set m k v)` when the receiver's type has a `set` method; there is no new dispatch machinery, the place *becomes* a call and the multimethod resolver does the rest, which is what pairing it with `get` buys over `setf` expanders. Three things the one-line plan entry did not say. (1) **A literal selector is always the field, and it is load-bearing.** The first probe defined `set` on `(ref (Vector T))` and broke `Vector`'s own `drop`: `(set! (self 'data) null)` dispatched back into that method — the write side of the `_get` recursion trap. The fix is the split `emit-get-with-callee` already makes (Branch A literal = member access, Branch B computed = value-keyed dispatch), applied to `set`; a symbol-keyed `set` is reached through a variable, exactly as a symbol-keyed `get` is. (2) **The route is decided from `node-type`, not from an emitted Val** — the read path can emit the receiver first because it has one consumer, but a write has two (the generic call takes a value, the field write takes storage), so emitting to decide would emit twice. (3) **One function is the whole route**: `set-place-call` is called by `emit-set-place` and by `node-type`'s `set!` branch, which types the place as `node-type` of the very cell emit builds — the lockstep discharged by having one answer instead of two that must agree, and a `set` method with a return type is therefore usable as a value. `_get` never dispatches and neither do the storage places. A computed key on a READABLE collection with no `set` method gets a directed message rather than a missing-quote note about something that is not a field name. `Vector` and `HashMap` both gained `set`: the vector's is new (bounds-checked like `invoke`, refusing to grow so a typo cannot silently extend it), the map's is `assoc` under the name the place form dispatches on. **935 tests** (3 new units), `make bootstrap` byte-identical, boot refreshed. **`case` alternatives DONE (2026-08-31)** — one arm matching several values ([stage16-ergonomics/case-alternatives.md](stage16-ergonomics/case-alternatives.md)), spelled `(:or v …)` rather than the filed bare list. The item's open question — is a parenthesised value the thing to match, or the things to match? — has no answer that reads the elements: 17 of the tree's 19 collapsible arm-runs are bare enum constants (`TY-STRUCT TY-UNION`), indistinguishable from a call's head, and the two shapes that *look* like set literals both denote real values (`[1 2]` a `(Vector i32)`, `'(1 2)` a runtime `Node` list). A keyword head decides it instead, so a plain parenthesised value stays a call and Stage 15's W4d `case-clause-hint` — the diagnostic that exists to defend exactly that reading — stands verbatim, which is what the decision turned on. Ten lines inside `case` in `lib/macros.nuc`, **no compiler change**; one result copy behind an `or` of `=`s, not a result per alternative. Two things implementation found: a macro body sees only what precedes it in the file (`case` is above `when`, so the shape test uses `cond`), and the degenerate `(:or)` must therefore *have a value* rather than a diagnostic — `false`, like `(or)` — because a macro body cannot raise a located error. 935 tests (unchanged count; `examples/case.nuc` carries the coverage), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **Adopted in `src/`** at 19 sites across 5 files (-32 lines; `type-utils.nuc` alone -37), and the **committed boot binary compiles the result unchanged** — the prelude is read from `lib/macros.nuc` at compile time, so a new prelude macro is usable in `src/` with no boot refresh. The adoption rule is *collapse where the arms share a reason, not merely a value*: five runs were left alone because a per-arm comment is a reason (`TY-ERR`/`TY-CHAR` each explain why an i32-repr non-integer answers yes to `is-int-type`), or because the coincidence sits inside a width ladder whose regularity is the table's point. |

  **S2 done (2026-07-04)** — boot refresh: `make update-bootstrap` regenerated `boot/nucleusc.ll` from the S1 dual-accept-capable `build/nucleusc`, and `make windows-boot` regenerated both cross-compiled boot artifacts (`boot/nucleusc-x86_64-windows-gnu.ll`, `boot/nucleusc-x86_64-windows-msvc.ll`) from that same S1-capable compiler; these three `boot/*.ll` files are the only tree changes. A full `make clean && make && make bootstrap` reconverged byte-identical (stage1.ll == stage2.ll, as expected since S1 was itself additive/byte-identical) and `make test` passed 162/162. Practical effect: the checked-in boot compiler now itself accepts both defn signature styles — the prerequisite for S3 (the tree-wide mechanical rewrite to new-style syntax), which needs a boot that can already parse what it's about to be fed. S3 and S4 remain pending.
  **S3 done (2026-07-05)** — the tree-wide mechanical rewrite to new style. A paren-aware Python rewriter (byte-span-recording s-expr parser; skips strings/comments/char literals; handles multi-line param lists, line-spanning `&where`, the colon-paren-fuse name `make-adder:(BoxedFn (i32) i32)`, and both list-head forms) rewrote `src/` (`defn`=622/`declare`=6), `lib/` (`defn`=277/`declare`=1/proto=51), and `examples/`+`tests/` (`defn`=373/`declare`=8/proto=12) — **no `.ll`/`.nuch`/`docs/` inputs**. The five internal synthesizers emit new-style (lambda lift, closure `invoke`/`drop`, defunion arm ctor + its `.nuch` declare reshape, type-erasure forwarding; the stamper is style-preserving). The `.nuch` writer flipped to new style — `(declare NAME (params) :ret)` / `(defmethod "@sym" NAME (params) :ret)` via `emit-nuch-ret`+`defn-name-only` (retiring `print-defn-name-legacy`); the `defmethod` importer + defunion-ctor reshape moved to the new arity; SM-3/S1-3a `.nuch` test expectations updated. One S1-missed consumer fixed: `check-generic-template` and `valid-check-instance` hardcoded body-start `3`, so a new-style **CELL return** (`constantly`'s `(BoxedFn (V) V)`) was walked as a body form → "unknown function 'V'"; now `(if (sig-name-is-bare …) 4 3)` (conventions.md). Verification: the pure head-rewrite of `src/`+`lib/` kept `build/nucleusc.ll` **byte-identical**; compiling legacy vs new-style source with one compiler produced **byte-identical IR for all 120 compilable examples** (excl. `comb-shapes.nuc`, a pre-existing HEAD compile-time-JIT failure not in the suite); the synthesis flip is IR-neutral (8 closure/union/dyn examples identical before/after). `make test` 162/162; `make bootstrap` byte-identical (no `update-bootstrap` — the boot only compiles `src/`, which has no new-style CELL-return template).
  **S4 done (2026-07-05)** — retire the legacy style. The legacy `name:ret` / list-head `(name ret)` spelling is now a **hard error** at every def-time chokepoint, each with the same targeted diagnostic quoting the offending name: `defn-parse-sig`'s legacy branch (plain `defn`/`defn-`), `register-generic-defn` (the **bounded-generic-template** path, which bypasses `defn-parse-sig` — the prescan routes templates there and `emit-defn` returns early for them; a gap the plan's "one accessor" framing missed), `emit-nuch-declare-import` (`declare`), and the sig-storage loop in `protocol-register-form` (protocol method sigs — stored verbatim + lazily parsed, so `proto-sig-parse`, which only reads the `(dyn P)` path, was hardened too as belt-and-suspenders). The dual-detection machinery (`sig-name-is-bare`/`legacy-ret-node`/`defn-name-only`) is retained exactly — the diagnostics depend on it. **Committed `.nuch` files were converted first** (else hardening the readers breaks the build): `src/llvm.nuch` (imported by the compiler itself), `lib/mathlib.nuch`, `lib/boxlib.nuch` legacy→new-style; `tests/run-tests.sh` heredocs/probes and `tests/repl/*.in` inputs (S3's `.nuc`-only rewriter skipped them) converted too. Negative tests: `tests/fixtures/s4-legacy-{defn,declare,proto,template}.nuc` + a `run-tests.sh` S4 block asserting each dies with its message. `docs/` rewrite: ~116 code-example sites across 14 files → new style (paren-aware rewriter + hand-fixed metasyntactic prose and colon-paren-fuse heads); `docs/toplevel.md` `defn` Signature prose rewritten to one grammar (legacy = retired hard error); the two `.nuch`-showing docs use the writer's exact keyword-`:ret` output. The hardening is bootstrap-neutral (both stages compile the same new-style source), so `make bootstrap` held before the refresh; the three `boot/*.ll` were nonetheless regenerated from the S4 compiler (`update-bootstrap` + Windows IRs) at this milestone, converging in a **single round**. Final: `make` green, `make test` **166/166**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical (`bin/nucleusc` is gitignored/regenerated from `boot/nucleusc.ll`). | [stage14/defn-signature.md](stage14/defn-signature.md) |
| 17 (native strings) | Replacing the compiler's C strings with Nucleus native strings — `CStr` retreats to declared C-interop seams only (LLVM C API, `argv`/`getenv`, `popen`ing `clang -E`, the linker command line). Dogfooding: every awkward conversion is a defect report against the string library, and the standing rule is **fix the library, not the call site**. Executes and supersedes [future/native-io.md](future/native-io.md). | **Planned (2026-08-31)** — plan written, not started. Inventory measured: 928 `fprintf`/`fputs`/`fputc` sites, ~650 `fmt-*` calls (9 fixed-arity shapes), 20 `open_memstream` sinks, 881 `CStr` mentions, 201 `strcmp`/86 `strlen`/37 `strchr`, ~237 `intern*` references across 40,712 lines of `src/`. Two rulings re-decided on new evidence: (a) the **interned substrate moves too** — a `Symbol` type (one word at interned bytes, length+hash header *behind* the pointer) keeps `=` a single `icmp eq ptr` while deleting the `strlen`s, because "keep it a raw `char*`" is the work-around-the-weakness move this stage forbids and the real weakness is that the stack has no interned type; (b) **two of native-io.md's three prerequisites are already gone** — struct payloads through `!T` work and `(Maybe Struct)` works AOT (re-tested 2026-08-31), leaving only `!void` (`Result.void.Err: arm 'ok' takes a different number of fields`), so three documented string-library "v1 limitations" are stale workarounds to retire rather than carry. Four tracks: A prerequisites, B library (`fmt`/`io`/`file`/`intern`, built complete and used by nothing), C conversion (diagnostics → emission → memstreams → source loading → REPL → comparison/scanning → substrate → close-out), D tooling. Gate is **emitted-output identity** per batch — byte-identical bootstrap is impossible when every emission call is rewritten. **Track A complete (2026-08-31)**: A1 `!void`, A2 the `!T` struct-payload workarounds, A3 `(Maybe Struct)` in JIT modules — all three prerequisites turned out to be stale beliefs rather than compiler limits, and verifying them found two silent miscompiles in the string lattice itself (gaps #25, #26). **B0 blockers closed (2026-09-01)**, and the borrow-convention question B0 raised is **settled**: lvalue-only implicit address-of is implemented, the `(ref T)` read/mutable split is deferred ([borrow-conventions.md](stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md)). **B1 done (2026-09-01)**: `lib/fmt.nuc` — `Writer`, `ToStr`, and the `str` family, with no format string at all (arity is settled at macro expansion). **B2 done (2026-09-01)**: `lib/io.nuc` — the standard streams as `Writer`s over raw descriptors, `print`/`println`/`eprint`/`eprintln`, `read-line`; no `FILE*` anywhere. **B3 done (2026-09-01)**: `lib/file.nuc` — `File` (`Drop`-closed) and `BufWriter`, the buffer that makes bulk emission viable without a `FILE`; it also found a `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep hole left by the implicit address-of (gap #29, fixed). **B4 done (2026-09-01)**: `lib/intern.nuc` — `Symbol` (one word, header behind the pointer, `=` is `icmp eq ptr`) and the `Keyword` rebase, retiring the 256-keyword cap; its benchmark caught `strview-hash` folding through a closure per byte (gap #30, fixed — 8x). **Track B complete**. **C1 done (2026-09-01)**: all 636 `fmt-*` sites → `fstr` and 39 stderr sites → `eprint`, `src/format.nuc`'s helper zoo deleted, driven by `scripts/stage17/rewrite-fmt.py` with zero refusals. C2 (IR emission, 928 sites) next.

| 19 (process control) | `lib/process.nuc` — start a program, control its streams, wait for it, read a typed exit status. Verified library work, not compiler work. | **Done 2026-09-04** (P1–P6) | [stage19-process/overview.md](stage19-process/overview.md) |

  **A1 done (2026-08-31)** — `!void`. Two changes, the second unplanned. (1) **`defunion-register` drops a `void` field** (`src/union-registry.nuc`): a `void` field carries no value, so it contributes none — a general `defunion` rule, not a `Result` special case. `(Result void Err)` then stamps a payload-less `ok` arm exactly like `Maybe`'s `none`, `(ok)` constructs it, `((ok) …)` matches it, and `union-layout-classify` already answers the ordinary tagged `{i32 tag, Err}` for `{payload-less, Err}`. That is the whole representation change. (2) **`try` moved out of `lib/error.nuc` into the compiler** — `emit-try` (`src/union-emit.nuc`) + its `node-type` lockstep arm (`src/generics.nuc`), reserved in `g-special-form-set` — because **the ok arm's binder count depends on the operand's TYPE and a macro cannot see one**: `!void` needs `((ok) (do))`, `!T` needs `((ok v) v)`. `emit-try` synthesizes the same match, reading the arm shape from `result-ok-type`; `try` consequently no longer needs `(import-use error)`. Bootstrap cost one generation: a `defmacro` may not shadow a special form, so the macro and the form cannot coexist even for a commit, while `src/reader.nuc`'s 19 `try` sites had to keep compiling under the *previous* boot — the macro was renamed `try-boot` and `src/` repointed for exactly one `make update-bootstrap`, then both removed. Generalized into `context/conventions.md` ("A form whose SHAPE depends on its operand's type cannot be a macro"). 936 tests (was 935), `make bootstrap` byte-identical, `examples/result-void.nuc` covers `(ok)` / `((ok) …)` / `try` on `!void` inside both `!void` and `!T`. Found in passing: `lib/macros.nuch` was **already** stale in the committed tree, so `make test` was failing `headers-generated` before this work began. | [stage17-native-strings/overview.md](stage17-native-strings/overview.md), [library-gaps.md](stage17-native-strings/library-gaps.md), [migration-tooling.md](stage17-native-strings/migration-tooling.md) |

  **A2 done (2026-08-31)** — retire the `!T` struct-payload workarounds. Both were built on the belief that a struct payload cannot travel through `!T`; it can. `strview-from-cstr` returned a **heap-allocated** `ptr:StrView` the caller had to free, and `strview-sub-bytes` returned `!ptr:StrView` for the same reason; both now return by value (`:StrView` and `:!StrView`), and nothing allocates. The `ByteStr` protocol's `sub-bytes` and its `StrView`/`String` conformances were retyped in lockstep, since a protocol signature and its conformers move together or not at all. Six examples migrated (`strview-test`, `strview-read-test`, `split-iter-test`, `cstr-fold-test`, `parse-test`, `string-split-test`), with `FoldFn` conformances retyped to `(FoldFn i64 StrView)`. Migration hazard worth recording: the first rewriter regex anchored the binding name at line start, so a binding sharing its line with the opening `(let (` was skipped — those sites **compiled clean and segfaulted**, because a `ptr:StrView` slot silently accepted the by-value result (the same defect A3 later isolated as gap #25). A by-value/by-reference retype is not safely done by a rewriter that can miss a site quietly. `docs/strings.md` corrected in three places.

  **A3 done (2026-08-31)** — `(Maybe Struct)` in JIT/CT modules, and the two defects the verification exposed. The premise was **stale**: `(Maybe StrView)` stamps in the macro-expansion JIT module, both in a macro body and in an `(Iterator StrView)` conformance. Nothing needed fixing at the anon-union stamp. `SplitIter` and `LineIter` are now `(Iterator StrView)`; their `cur` scratch fields, the niche encoding that packed a `(ref StrView)` into a bare `ptr`, and the `doseq-split` macro that decoded it are all deleted, and `doseq-iter`/`reduce`/`match` drive both directly. Because each segment became a **value**, every consumer taking `(ref StrView)` needed `(addr-of seg)` — and that call-site change found a silent miscompile. **(1) A `StrView` decayed to `.data` at any pointer target** (gap #25): the NS-2/NS-3 borrow rule in `src/abi.nuc` fired on `(or (= dk TY-PTR) (= dk TY-CSTR))`, and `(ref StrView)` *is* `TY-PTR`, so passing a `StrView` value — or a string literal — to a `(ref StrView)` parameter compiled clean, passed the data pointer where a `%StrView*` was expected, and the callee read `len` out of the string's own bytes (`(show v)` printed `len=1688849864211203`). A structurally identical `(defstruct P x:i64 y:i64)` was correctly rejected, so this was not a general value→ref hole; it was the string lattice punching one, in exactly the type this stage spreads through the compiler. Fixed with `strview-borrow-target` (`src/type-utils.nuc`), which admits only a **byte** pointer — `CStr`, a bare `ptr`/`raw`/`?ptr`, or a pointer to an 8-bit integer (`data`'s own type) — called from both `abi.nuc` arms. Deliberately an error rather than an implicit address-of: that is gap #24 option (c), still open, and `(addr-of x)` is a one-token repair. **(2) `defstruct` silently accepts a duplicate field name** (gap #26): `done:bool done:bool` compiled and `--emit-cheader` emitted the field twice, producing a header no C compiler accepts. Caught only because a test compiles a committed header; deferred to B0. That test (`w9-cheader-committed-header-usable`) read `SplitIter.cur`, which A3 removed, so it was repointed at `lib/string.h`'s by-value return and by-value parameters — the same completeness property, on a surface that still exists. Also recorded: a join of two string literals collapses to `CStr`, so `(if c "one" "two")` cannot fill a `StrView` slot either literal alone fills (gap #27, folded into MC-2's join work). 936 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical — so nothing in `src/` relied on the over-broad borrow. `docs/strings.md` §6 and `docs/iterators.md` rewritten.

  **B0 blockers done (2026-09-01)** — the string-stack gap closure. **#6 `Vector` bulk append**: `vector-extend-raw` (raw buffer) and `vector-extend` (another vector) reserve once and `memcpy` once. Not spelled `extend` — that is the conformance form, and a symbol may name only one kind of thing. No trivially-copyable *branch*: the copy is bitwise, which is what `conj`'s element store already is, so a second path would select nothing different. **#5 `string-push-str`**: `string-push-bytes-raw` was `(dotimes (i n) (conj v (aref p i)))` — a bounds test, a capacity test and a call for every byte on the path that appends every byte the compiler emits; it is now one `vector-extend-raw`. The register's other half was **wrong and was not followed**: it proposed dropping the UTF-8 re-validation because "a `StrView` is documented as valid UTF-8". It is not — `StrView` is a byte slice, which is precisely why `string-from-view` validates, and `String` is the type carrying the UTF-8 guarantee. The check stayed where it belongs; `string-push-str-unchecked` was added beside it (mirroring the existing `string-from-cstr-unchecked`), and that is the entry point the emission conversion will use. **#8** the `strview` value constructor (`strview-from-cstr` is now one line over it); **#9** `string-as-cstr`, which writes the NUL *past* `len` without counting it, so the String is unchanged for every other operation and a second call is free; **#10** `strview-find-byte` / `-rfind-byte` / `-rfind` / `-find-char` / `-rfind-char`, all `(Maybe usize)` — the codepoint searches are byte searches for the encoding, sound because UTF-8 is self-synchronizing. #10 forced one rename: the existing substring search `strview-byte-find` became **`strview-find`**, because shipping it one character away from `strview-find-byte` meaning something else is a defect to create, not to inherit. **#13** `string-push-str` and `string-truncate` re-signed to `!void`; **#20** `strview-len` retired in favour of `strview-byte-len`; **#26** a duplicate `defstruct` field is now an error, checked in `defstruct-fill-layout` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) where the names are interned, strict pass only — the layout prescan abandons rather than dies. 938 tests (two new: `examples/string-b0-test.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/s17-dup-struct-field.nuc`), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **#18/#24 left open deliberately** and written up as [stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md](stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md): by-value producers meeting by-reference consumers is **structural, not a style lapse** — the producer side must be by value (the alternatives are the `malloc` #7 deleted and the `alloca` dance #8 deleted) and the consumer side must be by reference (`ByteStr`/`Str` bind `Self` to both `StrView` and `String`, and `String` has `Drop`), so neither uniform convention is reachable and the question is who bridges the seam. Unrestricted implicit address-of is unsafe while `(ref T)` conflates read and write — `(string-push-str (string-new) sv)` would append into a temporary and drop it, #25's class. Recommends lvalue-only implicit address-of now, with a `(ref T)` read/mutable split (justified on signatures-as-documentation across `src/`, not on ergonomics) as a separate decision that is cheaper before B1 than after. Docs: `strings.md` construction/search/mutation tables, `collections.md` bulk append, `stdlib.md` (which still described `strview-from-cstr` as heap-allocating — stale since A2).

  **B1 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/fmt.nuc`: the replacement for a format string. `Writer` (single-method `write-str`, so it can be erased as `(dyn Writer)` and stored in a global) with `String` and `CFile` conformances; `ToStr` (a value's text, appended to a `(ref String)`) with conformances for `i64`/`i32`/`usize`/`ui64`, `f64`/`f32`, `Char`, `bool`, `StrView`, `CStr`; and `str-into` / `str` / `str-alloc`. Integers are formatted natively (`string-push-u64` builds digits LSB-first into a stack buffer and reverses; `string-push-i64` negates in **unsigned** space, since `-(-2^63)` is not representable and negating first prints the wrong number for i64's minimum). Floats delegate to `snprintf` — permanently, per §2.6. **Arity is a macro-expansion fact**, which is what retires both `src/format.nuc`'s fixed-arity helper zoo and the format-helper arity trap in `context/conventions.md`, where a mismatch segfaults the compiler. Three deviations from the plan, each argued in the file header and in [stage17-native-strings/overview.md](stage17-native-strings/overview.md): **`Fmt` is not built** (it would box through `(dyn Writer)` once per piece, and neither real shape wants it — a diagnostic builds a `String` and writes it once, emission writes chunks straight to the sink); **`ToStr`'s receiver is `Self` by value**, which is what makes `(to-str 42 out)` legal at all — a `(ref Self)` receiver makes a literal an rvalue with no address to take — so `String`, which has `Drop` and would therefore be *moved*, deliberately does not conform and is written through its O(1) view (`Symbol`/`Keyword` land with B4, which defines `Symbol`); and **`Writer`'s byte argument is by value**, not `(ref StrView)`, because it is not `Self` and so the cross-conformer constraint of borrow-conventions.md §2 never reached it. `str-into` recurses one piece at a time — the shape `+`/`-` in `lib/macros.nuc` already use — rather than building the call list with `make-cell`: each piece needs *wrapping* in a `(to-str … out)` call, which `~@` cannot do, and a `defn` helper cannot serve because `node` is `import-ct`'d, so `make-cell`/`intern-symbol` exist only inside the JIT'd expander. New error `io-write-failed`. `examples/fmt-test.nuc`; 941 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Docs: `strings.md` §9, index.

  **B2 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/io.nuc`: the standard streams as `Writer`s over raw descriptors. Deliberately **not** `FILE*`-backed — a `FILE`'s hidden libc buffer would let output reorder against whatever `fprintf` sites have not flipped yet during the C-track conversion, and that ordering bug would read as a codegen bug. Explicit buffering is B3's `BufWriter`. Two deviations from the plan: **one `FdOut` type with an `fd` field** rather than separate `StdOut`/`StdErr` (the two would have identical conformances differing only in a constant, and `(dyn Writer)` erases them to the same thing; `(std-out)`/`(std-err)` are constructors, and `FdOut` is *borrowed* — the owning, `Drop`-closed descriptor is B3's `File`); and **`print`/`println`/`eprint`/`eprintln` share one module-global format buffer**, cleared per call, so a line is one `write` and the spelling is allocation-free after the first growth — the cost is that they are not reentrant, which nothing in `lib/` violates. Each expands to the `!void` of its write, so a caller may `try` it and discarding is the default, as with C's universally-ignored `printf` return. `fd-write-all` loops over short writes (ordinary on a pipe, not defensive) but does **not** retry a negative return: `EINTR` is only reachable behind a handler installed without `SA_RESTART`, and retrying an unclassified error would spin forever. `read-line` returns `(Maybe String)` — `none` only at end of input, so a blank line comes back as a zero-length String and a final unterminated line is still returned — over an 8 KiB input buffer, since the unbuffered spelling is a `read` syscall per byte. It is gated by `tests/fixtures/s17-read-line.nuc` with piped input rather than by an example: an example inherits the harness's stdin and would block on a terminal. `examples/io-test.nuc` covers the rest. 943 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. New doc `docs/io.md`.

  **B3's #28 deferred, not accepted (2026-09-01); BUILT 2026-09-03 — see the addendum below** — a Linux-only file library is not an acceptable end state. `lib/file.nuc` hardcodes Linux/glibc `open(2)` flags because Nucleus does not import C macros, so it opens the wrong kind of descriptor on Darwin with no diagnostic. Deferred out of Stage 17 with a named fix rather than merely recorded: [stage17-native-strings/platform-constants.md](stage17-native-strings/platform-constants.md) — admit object-like `#define`s whose replacement list folds as an integer constant expression. Both halves already exist (`cheader-run-cpp` already carries `--target=`/`--sysroot=` to `clang -E`, so `-dM` gives the *target's* values; `c-cexpr-*` already folds C constant expressions and fails closed). Out of Stage 17's scope because it is a C-header-importer change, not a string-stack one. The same wall already cost `EINTR` (`lib/io.nuc` therefore does not retry) and `CLOCKS_PER_SEC` (the B4 benchmark), so it is a recurring tax.

  **B3 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/file.nuc`: `File`, an owning descriptor closed by `Drop`, with `file-open-read`/`file-create`/`file-open-append`/`file-open-flags`, `file-read-to-string` (bytes, not validated UTF-8 — the compiler reads source whose encoding is the program's problem to diagnose, not the reader's to refuse), `file-write-bytes`, and a `Writer` conformance; plus `BufWriter`, a `File` and a 64 KiB staging buffer, which is the load-bearing half — IR emission is millions of small writes and a `FILE`'s buffer was the only thing `fprintf` had that a bare descriptor does not. A `write-str` of at least a bufferful skips the buffer: staging it would be a memcpy that buys no syscall. `file-close`/`buf-writer-close` exist beside the `Drop` path because `close` can fail late and `Drop` returns `void`; both set the descriptor to -1 so a later `Drop` cannot close a number some other `open` has since been handed. New errors `io-open-failed`, `io-read-failed`. Two findings. **#28, not fixable in the library**: `open(2)`'s flags are C preprocessor macros, which Nucleus deliberately does not import, so `O_RDONLY`/`O_CREAT`/`O_TRUNC`/`O_APPEND` are spelled out as Linux/glibc `defconst`s and the module is Linux-only as written — the real fixes (a target-keyed constants module, or object-like `#define` evaluation in the header import) are out of B3's scope. **#29, fixed in the compiler**: `(try (write-str f sv))` died with *"match: arm 'ok' binder count does not match its field count"* whenever `f` was a struct **value** binding rather than an already-`(ref T)` parameter. `emit-try` sizes its ok arm from `(node-type operand)`, and `node-type-call`'s tier 0 was still `generic-find-method-exact` — `params-type-eq` only — so the lvalue implicit address-of that `generic-resolve` gained was invisible to the type side; the call typed unknown, `try-ok-nfields` fell back to its 1-field default, and the generated `match` was one binder wide against `!void`'s payload-less arm. Fixed with `generic-find-method-accepting` (`src/generics.nuc`), the call-side companion run as node-type-call's tier-0 second pass, kept separate from `generic-find-method-exact` because that one also answers the *definition*-side question where `(name, param-types)` must stay an exact key. This is the `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep charging for a change made on one side only; bootstrap stayed byte-identical because nothing in `src/` yet calls a method with a struct-value receiver. `examples/file-test.nuc`; 944 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. `docs/io.md` §4–§5.

  **B4 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/intern.nuc`: `Symbol`, the interned-identity type whose absence was the sole justification for Stage 14's "the interned substrate stays raw `ptr`" ruling. The layout is the design's: `[hash:usize][len:usize][bytes…][NUL]` with the `Symbol` holding the address of the first **byte**, not the header — one word, and all four properties at once. `=` emits a single `icmp eq ptr` (verified in the emitted IR, not assumed); `symbol-len`/`symbol-cached-hash` are loads behind the pointer; `symbol-as-view` allocates nothing; `symbol-as-cstr` is free, because the bytes are still NUL-terminated, so every surviving FFI seam takes a Symbol with no copy. Table: open-addressed, linear probing, grows at 3/4 load — not a `HashMap`, whose keys want to be interned, which would be circular. Conformances `Eq`/`Hash`/`ToStr`/`ByteStr`/`Str`, the last two through the view. No collision with the compiler's `Sym` (a binding record), so the `Name` fallback spelling was not needed. **`Keyword` rebased** (#4): it is now one `Symbol` and nothing else — one word instead of three, no `id` counter, and `lib/keyword.nuc`'s 256-entry fixed array with a linear scan and a `strcmp` per probe is deleted, cap and all. **The benchmark the plan asked for earned its place immediately.** `tests/fixtures/s17-intern-bench.nuc` timed `symbol-intern` at **8x slower** than the compiler's own `intern-symbol` over the same corpus — a stage-level failure by the plan's own standard. The table was not the cause: `strview-hash` folded through `reduce` over a `ByteIter`, so every byte cost an indirect call through a closure plus a `(Maybe ui8)` construction and match (#30) — a cost paid by every interned name, every `HashMap` probe and every `StrView` key compare in the library. Replaced with a direct byte loop (same FNV-1a, same results): 8x → 1.4x at -O0, and at -O3, which is how the compiler is actually built, `symbol-intern` is ~1.5x **faster** than `intern-symbol`. The generalizable finding: an `Iterator` fold is a fine default and the wrong tool inside a primitive everything else calls — `strview-eq` and the `ByteStr` defaults are worth the same look before the C-track conversion puts them under compiler-scale load. `examples/intern-test.nuc` (identity, the header, the two bridges, HashMap keying, and identity surviving a rehash past 3000 names); 945 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Docs: `stdlib.md` `Symbol` + rewritten `Keyword`, `types.md` (the 256-keyword limit is gone).

  **C1 done (2026-09-01)** — diagnostics converted and `src/format.nuc`'s helper zoo deleted. All **636** `fmt-*` call sites became `fstr`, and the **39** non-REPL `fprintf stderr` sites became `eprint`; `format.nuc` went 258 → 198 lines and is now only the IR/C identifier layer. What this removes is not typing but a hazard: each `fmt-*` took exactly as many conversions as its name said, and one too many made `snprintf` read a garbage vararg and segfault the compiler (the trap `context/conventions.md` documents). Arity is now a macro-expansion fact. **`fstr` (`src/strfmt.nuc`) rather than `str-alloc` directly**: it is `str-alloc` on the compiler's arena — the process lifetime `arena-strndup` gave, so no call site changes how it stores the result — handed back as a **bare `ptr`** rather than the honest `CStr`, because a branch join of a `CStr` arm and a `ptr` arm collapses to void and `(if c base (fstr …))` is a shape that occurs. Two compiler-local conformances ride along and are scheduled for deletion in C6–C7: `ToStr` on bare `ptr` (the compiler's strings still are one, and the specifier census over all 636 sites found no `%p`, so a `%s` argument was a C string by construction — this deliberately does NOT go in `lib/`, where "every pointer prints as text" would turn a type error into silent garbage), and a `Hex` value type for the `%016lX`/`%04lX`/`%016lx` float bit patterns. **The rewriter** is `scripts/stage17/rewrite-fmt.py`, paren-aware per migration-tooling.md §2, with a `--stderr` mode for the `fprintf` half; it **refused zero sites** — the census came back `%s`=781, `%d`=141, `%ld`=18, `%%`=17, `%016lX`=9, `%04lX`=4, `%016lx`=2, `%c`=1, all modelled — and is run to a fixed point, since a `fmt-*` nested in another's argument list is consumed as source text in one pass. Three findings. **(1) The boot compiler gates the source**: `fstr` needs the lvalue implicit address-of, so `src/` could not use it until `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` and `bin/nucleusc` — one shim generation, as `try-boot` needed. **(2) One name collision, and only one**: importing the string stack early brings `char-at` into scope, which `src/reader.nuc` had defined for `(ptr, i64)`; renamed to `cstr-byte-at` (284 uses), the suffixed-name convention `src/` already follows. **(3) Two `%s` arguments were typed `ptr:Node`** — `Node.car` holding a path string, which `%s` read as bytes without complaint and `to-str` will not; both now carry an explicit `(as ptr …)`. The type system found a place the format string was lying. `sanitize-for-ir`/`ir-name-token` and friends stay as they are: they never used `snprintf`, so none of C1's hazard applies, and `ir-name-token`'s pointer-unchanged fast path is an identity contract that belongs with the substrate conversion (C6–C7). Self-compile time unchanged (1.29s before and after); 945 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Deferred as planned: the 69 `printf`-to-stdout sites (C2 — converting them before the IR stream would reorder against a block-buffered `stdout`) and the REPL's 44 stderr sites (C5).

  **Lvalue-only implicit address-of done (2026-09-01)** — the resolution of #18/#24, argued in [stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md](stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md). A struct **value** held in a binding now reaches a parameter that wants a pointer to exactly that struct, with no `&`: `(strview-byte-len sv)` where `sv:StrView`. Restricted to a **binding**, because `(ref T)` does not distinguish a read borrow from a write one — an rvalue would let a mutating callee append into a temporary and drop it, which is #25's class of defect — so `(show (strview-from-cstr "x"))` stays the ordinary argument-type error. Three pieces: `Val.lvalue-sym` (`src/compiler-types.nuc`), the binding a value was loaded from, set at the single place a Val becomes an lvalue (`emit-symbol-ref-bound`'s load arm) so a call result or literal has it null by arena zero-init; `binding-address-val` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`), factored out of `emit-addr-of` so the explicit `&x` and the implicit form share one owner for the escape-taint rule; and `params-accept-args` (`src/generics.nuc`), the dispatch half. **The dispatch half is not optional and its tier is load-bearing.** `generic-resolve` picks a method from argument types before any coercion runs, so without it every protocol method — `byte-len`, `sub-bytes`, the whole `ByteStr`/`Str` surface, which is most of #24's real friction — failed with "no matching method". Writing it into the tier-2 widen pass (which already receives argument nodes, so it looked like the natural home) broke `parse-test`: `from-str` selects its overload by a phantom first argument, and tier 2 lets every argument widen, so `i32` also matched the `i64` and `f64` overloads. The adjustment is **exact**, not a widening, so it runs at tier 0 as a second pass after the strict one finds nothing — an exact overload still wins and no resolving call can become ambiguous. Generalized into `context/conventions.md`. Dogfooded immediately: the 45 two-line `X__v`/`X` bindings A2's migration script created across five examples collapsed to one line each, and A3's four `(print-sv (addr-of seg))` sites are `(print-sv seg)` again — all byte-identical output. The implicit and explicit forms emit the identical call (the slot address is already an SSA name); the pre-coercion load is left dead and removed by the optimizer. Only a *binding* is an lvalue — a field, an array element and a deref are not, though C++ counts all three. 940 tests (`examples/lvalue-addr-of.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/s17-rvalue-addr-of.nuc`), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **The `(ref T)` read/mutable split is deferred**: §3.4 removes the friction that raised it, so it no longer blocks Stage 17 and wants its own stage.

  **D1 done (2026-09-01)** — `scripts/stage17/ir-snapshot.sh`, the emitted-output identity gate ([migration-tooling.md](stage17-native-strings/migration-tooling.md) §1). `snapshot` / `verify`, three emissions per input (`--emit-llvm`, `--emit-cheader`, `--emit-nuch`) plus captured stderr, plus both Windows cross-emissions of every `lib/*.nuc`; 420 inputs, 2,596 artifacts, ~2 min. Re-baselining needs `--force`, so it can never be silent.
